# Write plainly

**Resident, with no `paths:`.** It applies whenever a session writes prose. A
path scope would miss item briefs, commit messages, pull request bodies and
replies, because no read comes before them. The project owner asked for this
rule on 2026-10-17, and `PL-8ZTQ` has the measurement and the sources behind
each point.

Write all prose that a person will read in plain English: docstrings, comments,
error messages, documents, rules, item briefs, commit messages, pull request
titles and bodies, replies, and every question or review request sent to the
project owner, including each field of the line `bin/docket arm` prints. In
those, say what changes for the owner or a learner, and never name a test,
check or tool by its internal name (project owner, 2026-11-02, `PL-BGN1`).
Write for the readers that the table below names for that kind of text. They
should understand each sentence the first time they read it (project owner,
2026-11-02, ratified, `PL-H76S`).

| Kind of text | Reader | What they know | What they do not know | What they will do with it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `docs/MODEL.md` | A clinician or educator | Anesthesia pharmacology and physiology. | Numerical-methods terms such as "operator split", software terms, e-notation such as "4e-4", and this repository's own names. | Decide whether to trust a displayed value, or learn how the simulation works. |
| `docs/MODEL.md` | A developer, or someone who forked the project | Python, and the basics of anesthesia. | Numerical-methods terms such as "first-order method", and this repository's own names. | Change a value or an equation for a goal of their own. |
| `README.md` | An educator or trainee in anesthesiology or critical care | Anesthesia and critical care. | Software terms, numerical-methods terms, and this repository's own names. | Decide whether to use the simulator, then install it and run a case. |
| `README.md` | A contributor who knows both anesthesia and code | Anesthesia, Python and software development. | This repository's own names and history. | Decide whether to work on the simulator, then set it up. |
| `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/interface-provenance.md`, `docs/machine-abstraction.md` and `docs/machine-survey.md` | A contributor who knows both anesthesia and code | Anesthesia, Python and software development. | This repository's own names and history, and the outside designs and machines it studied. | Find where a change belongs, and what it must keep. |
| `docs/machine-survey.md` and `docs/references/` | A clinician or educator | Anesthesia pharmacology, physiology and equipment, and how to read a paper's methods. | Software terms, and this repository's own names. | Check a stored value, or a difference between machines, against its published source. |
| `ROADMAP.md` | The project owner, or a contributor deciding what to build next | Anesthesia, critical care, and what the project is for. | The internal names of tests, checks and tools, and decisions taken in sessions they did not see. | Decide what a release holds and what it leaves out. |
| `docs/consultant-brief.md` | An outside reviewer: a new session given the text below the brief's dividing line as its first message | Python, anesthesia and software review. | This repository, its history and its rules. | Review the repository critically, and report what it finds. |
| Docstrings and comments in `src/` and `tests/` | A Python developer who knows the basics of anesthesia | Python, and the basics of anesthesia. | This repository's own names and history, and any numerical-methods term the code does not explain. | Change the code, or check that it is right. |
| String literals in `src/`, such as interface notices and refusal messages | A learner: a trainee in anesthesiology or critical care using the simulator | Anesthesia and critical care. | Software terms, numerical-methods terms, and this repository's own names. | Act on a notice beside a value, or learn from a refusal which condition failed and the value that failed it. |
| String literals in `src/`, such as interface notices and refusal messages | A Python developer who meets the message in a log, a traceback or a test | Python, and the basics of anesthesia. | This repository's own names and history. | Find the input that failed and its value, and fix it. |
| Replies, and questions and review requests to the project owner | The project owner, an anesthesiologist and intensive-care physician | Anesthesia, critical care, and what the project is for. | The internal names of tests, checks and tools, and what the session did. | Make a decision, or learn what changed. |
| Item briefs in `docs/items/`; the workflow's files in `.claude/`, `tools/`, `subprojects/docket/src/`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/apparatus.md`, `docs/maintainer.md`, `docs/worker.md`, `docs/resident-instructions.md`, `docs/WORKING_NOTES.md`, `docs/dead-ends.md` and `docs/prose-review/README.md`; commit messages; and pull request titles and bodies | A later session, or the owner reading the history | Python, anesthesia, and this repository's rules. | What the writing session knew and did not write down. | Pick up the work, or find out why a change was made. |

The two `README.md` rows are the readers that `README.md` § "What it is for"
names. `docs/releases/` has no row, because `bin/docket release` writes each
note from item titles, and an edit there would make a note disagree with its
item. `tools/new_prose.py` reads this table. It prints a changed file's passages
for the copy editor only when a row names that file, and it gives each passage
that row's readers. A new row therefore brings the files it names into the
review with no other change.

**Write what the reader needs to act on, and stop.** Find the reader and what
they will do with the text in the table above. Put what they see first, then
what it means for them, then why. Move each reason, derivation, test name and
piece of history that they do not act on to where its own reader looks, or cut
it. Decision history goes in the item. In `docs/MODEL.md`, the rest goes in a
note for developers at the end of the section it came from, a paragraph that
opens with "**For developers.**" That note may name the tests for the paragraphs
directly above it (`PL-W8FL`). That detail stays in `docs/MODEL.md`, because the
document must specify the numerical method (`PL-TZ7R`). State a model limitation
only where it could change what a clinician concludes from a displayed value
(`PL-BS6N`).

**Give a chosen value its reason from a record.** Where a docstring or a
comment gives a chosen value, such as a tolerance or a limit, say what the
value does, why it is this number, and what changing it would do. In
`docs/MODEL.md`, say it in the note for developers. Take each answer from the
code or test that uses the value, or from a cited record. When the reason is
not at hand, trace the value's history first, with `git log -S` and the items.
Search for the value as the code writes it. The step-size test's 0.5% tolerance,
removed in `PL-VXM0`, was `5e-3` in `tests/reference/test_sevo_patient.py`, and
`git log -S 5e-3` finds the commit that first set it. When the trace finds no
reason, file an item for the missing record, and say in the text what the trace
found. Never state a reason that no record holds.

1. **Write whole sentences.** Keep the small words that show how a sentence
   fits together: "a" and "the", "that", "which" and "who", and the verb.
   Start a docstring or a paragraph with a sentence, not a phrase and a colon.
2. **Do not stack clauses.** "Each entry the plan was not read whole at" makes
   the reader work out where each clause ends. Write "Each entry at which the
   plan was not read in full."
3. **Use words in their everyday sense.** Prose here has drifted into private
   senses that a newcomer cannot guess. Write "returns" and "result", not
   "answers" and "answer"; "review", not "read" as a noun; "treats as", not
   "reads as"; "enforces", not "holds"; "still applies", not "stands";
   "applies to", not "reaches"; "less than", not "under"; and "bypasses", not
   "writes past". Name the rule or the exception you mean instead of calling it
   a "floor" or a "door". When the project needs a term of its own, define it
   where it first appears.
4. **Make one point per sentence.** Put the subject and its verb early and
   close together. When a sentence needs a semicolon, a dash or a second
   parenthesis, split it. Keep a cause and its consequence in one sentence
   where they fit: "One step for all agents keeps the last MAC digit worth the
   same amount whatever the agent", not three sentences joined by "So".
5. **State the reason in words.** An item id, a pull request number or a
   section name can follow as a pointer, but the sentence must make sense
   without it.
6. **Say who does what.** Make each "it", "this" and "that" point at a noun the
   reader can find.
7. **Plain is not longer.** Say each thing once, where the reader needs it.
   Cut words that add nothing. Keep every fact where its own reader will look
   for it: in the paragraph, in the note for developers of a `docs/MODEL.md`
   section, or in the item. A unit, a value, an equation, a condition, an
   exception and a citation are facts. Moving or restating one never changes
   its value.
8. **Read it aloud.** If you would not say it that way to a colleague, rewrite
   it.

**Five patterns that make prose read as machine-written.** The points above
catch prose that is too compressed. These five catch the opposite failure:
padding that sounds careful and adds nothing. The project owner judged
`docs/MODEL.md` to read "like the worst kind of AI slop" on 2026-11-02 because
of them (`PL-ZT47`).

1. **A tautological aside.** A clause restates what its sentence already
   says: "The naming is Hendrickx and De Wolf's, which is the field's naming."
   Delete it. Where it carries a real fact, make that fact a sentence.
2. **A contrast nobody claimed.** "X, not Y" and "rather than Y" reject an
   option no reader proposed. State the positive claim. Keep a contrast only
   where a reader would otherwise assume Y.
3. **Narration about the text itself.** The sentence describes how the
   document records, sources or decides something, instead of the subject:
   "it is recorded at that depth", "the pages above are what the citation is
   being used for". Give the citation with its page numbers and stop. Who
   supplied a source, and how a choice was reached, belong in the item file.
4. **A defensive scope statement.** The sentence says what is not relied on,
   against an objection nobody raised: "no page beyond them is relied on
   here". Delete it. Where a reader would plausibly assume a source was used,
   say once that it was not, as a limitation.
5. **A table cell or paragraph that has grown into an essay.** Prose ordinals
   ("First", "Second" through "Sixth") or several separate points inside one
   table cell mean the content wanted to be a list. Move it to a numbered list
   or a short section, and leave the cell one sentence and a pointer.

Before, from `docs/MODEL.md` on 2026-11-02: "The naming is Hendrickx and De
Wolf's, which is the field's naming. They set out the gas-phase cascade and the
symbols F_D, F_I and F_A on pp. 161-162. [...] The project owner supplied this
source, and it is recorded at that depth. The pages above are what the
citation is being used for, and no page beyond them is relied on here."

After: "Symbols and the gas-phase cascade (F_D, F_I, F_A) follow Hendrickx and
De Wolf (pp. 161-162)." The full citation follows unchanged, and the last two
sentences go.

**A pull request title names the change plainly and briefly.** The title
becomes the squash commit's subject, the one line of `main`'s history that a
reader sees for the change. After the item ids that the `pr-title` check
requires, write a short phrase rather than a whole sentence: what the change
does, to what, and the reason when it fits. Use names a reader of the
repository already knows, such as a CI job, a check or a panel, not a term
coined while building the change. Give each number the thing it counts. Do not
describe the hoped-for effect in loose words instead of the change. When a
branch closes several items, name what they change together.

**Match the code around you, not its prose.** Copy the structure, naming and
idiom of the code you are working in. Much of the existing prose here breaks
the points above, so do not copy its sentences. Write each sentence you add or
change plainly, even when the text around it is dense. Rewriting sentences you
did not need to touch is work for its own item, in the `prose-quality`
feature. The exception is the copy editor at close-out, which may also rewrite
the paragraph or table row on each side of a passage you changed (`PL-22T5`).

This rule decides how a text is worded and ordered, and what it leaves out. The
safety-critical standard in `CLAUDE.md` and `.claude/rules/sources-and-docstrings.md`
decides which facts a docstring, an error message or a displayed value must give,
and this rule never cuts one. `.claude/rules/instruction-writing.md` decides the
shape of a reply. Code, commands, paths and quoted output stay exactly as they are.

## Three examples from this repository

Before: "Refuse a volume or tissue:gas coefficient whose capacity is under the
agent held."

After: "Reject a volume or tissue:gas partition coefficient that would make the
tissue's capacity less than the amount of agent it already holds."

Before: "The fresh gas admitted over the step, and as much circuit gas
exhausted. In circuit volumes it is the step in time constants, dt/tau, formed
without the time constant, which overflows to `inf` below about 2e-306 L/min in
a 6 L circuit."

After: "`admitted_gas_l` is the volume of fresh gas that enters the circuit
during the step. The same volume of circuit gas leaves through the exhaust.
Dividing `admitted_gas_l` by the circuit volume gives the step's length in time
constants, dt/tau. The code does not compute tau first, because tau overflows
to `inf` when the fresh gas flow is below about 2e-306 L/min in a 6 L circuit."

The project owner judged three versions of one pull request title, on
`#1523`, on 2026-10-17:

- Too cryptic: "PL-ZWQS: give the first CI part 2 of every 5 ordinary tests".
  A reader cannot tell what a "CI part" or an "ordinary test" is, or what the
  2 and the 5 count.
- Too conversational: "PL-ZWQS: make CI's two test jobs finish at about the
  same time". It describes a hoped-for effect and does not say what changed.
- Plain: "PL-ZWQS: rebalance the CI test split to offset part 1's lint steps".
  It names the change, what it changes and why.

The writing rule ends here, and the prose to review follows.

Structure read of an outline. The reverse outlines of every chapter of one document follow, in order, as passage 1. Report only what crosses chapters.

Readers of ROADMAP.md:
  - The project owner, or a contributor deciding what to build next.
    Knows: Anesthesia, critical care, and what the project is for.
    Does not know: The internal names of tests, checks and tools, and decisions taken in sessions they did not see.
    Will: Decide what a release holds and what it leaves out.

[1] ROADMAP.md, the outlines of its 48 chapters. For: The project owner, or a contributor deciding what to build next
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Project roadmap", rows 1-11
  - Row 1: the document title, "Project roadmap".
  - Rows 3-5: the file is the authoritative version and milestone map for the project. It takes precedence over a build guide, an issue or a conversation that conflicts with it. When one does, update the conflicting artifact or deliberately amend this file, in the same change.
  - Rows 7-11: the file covers releases only. Discrete tasks are tracked and prioritized in `docs/items/`, not here. These tasks are defects, fixes, cleanups, optimizations and small features. A piece of work is a milestone rather than a "queue item" when it needs four things of its own: a goal, a required scope, a definition of done and an explicit out-of-scope list. The term "queue item" is not explained, and it appears to mean one of the tasks in `docs/items/`.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Versioning decision (run 1 of 2)", rows 13-161
  - row 13: heading "Versioning decision".
  - rows 15–17: the versioning rule. Milestone-based semantic versioning during early development; the next number is chosen for the capability boundary a release crosses, not by incrementing the patch number.
  - rows 19–25: the v0.3.0 exception (project owner, 2026-08-26). Under the rule, clearing Gate 0 would be a patch, since it crosses no capability boundary. Gate 0 held two safety fixes, four refactors, two tooling defects and a performance fix, and afterwards the simulator does nothing new. It still ships as minor v0.3.0, because clearing the inherited pre-MVP backlog is the boundary the project most needs to point at, and a patch number would bury it.
  - rows 27–35: not a precedent. Applies to Gate 0 alone, the backlog from before the debt gate existed, "twenty items deep". Gate 1 and later gates hold one milestone's findings and take no version; their work ships in patch releases on the preceding milestone's track as it clears. A milestone's number marks the boundary its scope crosses (see § "The cadence" under § "The debt gate"). The capability-boundary rule applies unless a further exception is recorded here.

Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Versioning decision (run 2 of 2)", rows 163-276
  - 163-173: every Completed version is tagged, and the tag says what shipped.
    - Every version that the table above marks Completed carries an annotated tag.
    - The section does not list which versions those are: the table is the list, and a second copy was twice wrong.
    - `tools/doc_check.py` checks the completed rows against `git tag`, so a release shipped without a tag fails the check.
    - `git describe --contains` resolves for every commit up to and including the latest release, verified 2026-08-30 across the whole of `main`.
    - The commits that do not resolve are the unreleased ones after the newest tag, and the next release's tag will cover them.
    - That span of resolving commits is the provenance guarantee that PL-J3ZK was opened to restore.
  - 175-186: a tag must sit on its own release's commit, with v0.2.0 as the one exception.
    - A tag's name cannot show which commit it sits on.
    - `v0.5.3` was pushed before its release merged, onto the commit before its own, and every check treated it as tagged (`PL-YKSD`).
    - `tools/doc_check.py` reads `pyproject.toml` at each release tag. It fails when the declared version differs from the tag's, and it names the commit and the version.
    - Bold: one version shipped with a stale version file, v0.2.0.
    - v0.2.0's tag is on release commit `69a5888c`, whose `pyproject.toml` read 0.1.0.
    - The version bump `8687bdf2`, "Fix stale app version", went out in v0.2.1.
    - Moving the tag onto the bump would make that commit appear to have shipped in v0.2.0, so the tag stays and the exception is named here.
  - 188-200: where and when the tag is made.
    - The tag goes on the release's cut: the commit that added the release's notes file, which is the cut's own squash commit on `main`.
    - It does not go on whatever `main` holds when the tag is made (PL-QHCW).
    - `.github/workflows/tag-release.yml` pushes the tag once the cut lands on `main` (PL-2FY6).
    - Between that merge and the workflow run, the newest version is Completed and has no tag, and the check stays silent.
    - Failing in that window would turn `make check` red on every release branch, the failure PL-8HJ2 removed.
    - The check's former warning could not tell a tag that was never pushed from one this checkout had not fetched (PL-R7C0).
    - `bin/docket release` refuses to cut the next release while that tag is missing, which closes the window.
  - 202-213: work that merges while a release branch is open ships in that tag but is missing from its notes (`PL-028F`, decided 2026-09-14).
    - The notes are rendered at the cut, and the tag goes on the merge.
    - Work merged while the release branch is open is inside the tag's span, and no notes name it until the next release claims it.
    - This is not rare, and it is not visible afterwards.
    - Count on 2026-09-14: across 47 tagged spans, 12 closing pull requests merged inside a span and were not named in that span's notes. They fell in 11 spans, close to one release in four.
    - A squash merge gives the release commit one date, so `main`'s history cannot show how long the branch was open.
    - Recount on 2026-09-22: across 65 spans, 20 closing pull requests in 16 spans, including the two most recent completed spans. The rate held (`PL-P669`).
  - 215-225: the two records answer different questions.
    - The tag span is what shipped. `git describe --contains` is the authority for it, and it is the provenance guarantee.
    - The notes are what the cut stamped, the release's own account of its work.
    - Where the two differ, another release's notes describe the work, usually the next release's.
    - That convention is already written down for a release's own cut item.
    - It is not always the next release: `#412` was attributed back to v0.4.6, the release its code shipped in, and `#225` was recorded twelve releases later, by v0.4.15.
    - The pointer therefore names the release.
  - 227-245: since 2026-09-22, each notes file carries a pointer to work in its span that its notes do not name.
    - The section is headed `### also inside this tag's span` and names the pull request and the release that describes the work.
    - `check_tag_span_covers_its_notes` in `tools/doc_check.py` fails `make check` on the next span that holds such work and lacks the section.
    - The section only adds, so the cut's own account is untouched.
    - It is written after the tag. That is the only edit made to a notes file, and it is safe because a tagged release is never re-cut.
    - Exemption 1: a release's own cut pull request, which is inside its own span by construction and which the next release stamps every time.
    - Exemption 2: the newest tag's span, until the next release's notes exist, because its pointer would have to name a release nobody has cut. The cut that writes those notes is asked for the pointer on its own branch (`PL-JLYG`).
    - The claim is about navigation only. It fails if the tag stops going on the merge commit or the notes stop being rendered at the cut.
  - 247-259: nothing reconciles the two records automatically, by decision.
    - The pointer helps find work and does not reconcile.
    - Re-stamping at merge would need a job triggered by the merge, and such a job cannot write to `main` here. A push made with `GITHUB_TOKEN` starts no workflow, so required status checks never report on it (`PL-N5WZ`).
    - Every variant that would land needs a person at the merge.
    - Absorbing a newcomer, meaning work the base took in after the cut, would make the notes describe work the session that cut the release did not do. No tool should make that judgment.
    - `bin/docket check` warns on a checkout that carries an unmerged cut, while re-running `make release VERSION=X` would still absorb newer work.
    - The warning names what the base took in since the cut, and both dispositions.
  - 261-263: no version has gone out untagged.
    - Such a version would be named in a bold sentence here, and the checker requires the stated count to match the versions named.
    - There are none.
  - 265-273: the tag gap closed on 2026-08-30.
    - v0.1.0 (`97cc66a`) and v0.2.0 (`796bf4f`) were the last two untagged versions.
    - They were once recorded as untaggable, because `main` seemed to have three unrelated roots and `git merge-base 796bf4f 97cc66a` returned nothing.
    - That came from a shallow checkout, where boundary commits report no parents.
    - The full history has one root: `97cc66a` (v0.1.0) is an ancestor of `796bf4f` (v0.2.0), which is an ancestor of `bc5f823`.
    - The tags were placed on the actual release commits.
    - The old reasoning is kept because a shallow checkout reproduces it.
  - 275-276: there is no active v0.0.3 milestone.
    - Any guide that labels the first patient sevoflurane build v0.0.3 is superseded by this roadmap.

Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "The plan", rows 345-511
  - 345: heading "## The plan".
  - 347-349: The plan has one timeline. Clearing debt and building feature milestones are steps on the same plan, in the order they happen. Gates are half the work, not a background assumption.
  - 351: heading "### The planning model: a rolling wave".
  - 353-362: The plan is a rolling wave: near-term work in detail, later work at a higher level. Progressive elaboration adds detail as work nears (PMI, *PMI Lexicon of Project Management Terms*, entries *rolling wave planning* and *progressive elaboration*, https://www.pmi.org/pmbok-guide-standards/lexicon). Rows not yet scoped, entries with no definition of done and provisional version numbers are intended.
  - 364: Lead-in: each horizon is described to one level of detail, in one place.
  - 366-367: Table header: Horizon | Written in | Detailed to.
  - 368: Near horizon. Written in the section of each milestone that is scoped and not yet shipped, below. Detailed to goal, frozen debt list, required scope, definition of done and explicit out-of-scope list. Its tasks are the queue items named in required scope.
  - 369: Middle horizon. Written in § "The timeline", in rows not yet scoped. Each row has a name, its catalogue items and a place in the order, with no scope and no size.
  - 370: Far horizon. Written in § "Development pathway", which orders § "Planned milestones". Intent only: phases over a catalogue whose entries have no goal, required scope or definition of done.
  - 372-377: § "The timeline" orders every release it names. § "Development pathway" orders only work not yet named, under the last row, Beyond. If they disagree, the timeline wins, because reorders are recorded there. Each reorder is dated on the row it moved.
  - 379-388: Detail moves nearer at two moments: when an entry is placed on the timeline, and when its row is scoped (beat 1 of § "The cadence", which writes the row's section). A decision that a nearer horizon depends on may be taken sooner and recorded where that horizon cites it. Example: break-out's display tier split, settled on 2026-09-16 because it fixes the shape v0.6.0 builds. § "What MVP means here" was the outer boundary of the first waves, reached on 2026-09-21.
  - 390-405: No second plan is kept (`PL-LJVD`).
    - Release order and milestone scope are written only here. Other documents name a release and point at its section. Dated decision records remain history.
    - Generated views (`bin/docket wave`, the session digest's plan line) are recomputed on every run, so they cannot drift. Prose plans can drift, nothing compares them, and sessions remember nothing.
    - Example: pull requests #59 and #60, an external proposal closed unmerged on 2026-08-26, are the source of this section's vocabulary. Their `docs/PLANNING.md` stated the prohibition and restated the order as v0.3.0, v0.4.0, v0.5.0, v0.6.0 the schematic, v0.7.0 multi-substance. Four days later, v0.2.8 was inserted ahead. By 2026-09-16 the schematic was v0.8.0 and multi-substance v0.9.0. On 2026-09-26 both "moved back one release" when break-out came off the timeline.
  - 407: Source comment: `docs/PLANNING.md` is absent.
  - 409: heading "### What MVP means here".
  - 411-413: The MVP is complete when a learner can run a case, branch it at a decision point and compare the two managements side by side, in clinicians' units, on a time base that spans a case.
  - 415-421: This is Gas Man's Graph and Overlay; Gas Man is the reference simulator the project builds on. After the MVP, work extends a working teaching tool. The boundary is at comparison because comparison isolates the variable: one run teaches a curve, two runs teach why it moved. Without comparison the simulator is a demonstration.
  - 423-427: Two feature releases reach the MVP. Both are interface releases on the existing, validated model. Two releases come before them: the v0.2.8 patch, which makes the development loop reliable, and v0.3.0, which adds no capability and clears the ground.
  - 429-430: v0.3.0, the foundation. Clears Gate 0's inherited backlog, adds no new capability. Versioning exception under § "Versioning decision".

Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Completed: v0.1.0 - first patient sevo model", rows 513-589
  - 513: chapter heading, "Completed: v0.1.0 - first patient sevo model".
  - 515: heading "Goal".
  - 517-518: Goal. Extend the existing circuit model into the smallest useful, testable volatile-anesthetic patient model.
  - 520-523: diagram of the model path. Delivered sevo -> breathing circuit -> alveoli -> blood -> vessel-rich group / muscle / fat -> mixed venous return.
  - 525-527: sevoflurane only. Should show recognizable wash-in, uptake, tissue distribution, mixed-venous return and washout, while keeping deterministic behaviour and explicit units.
  - 529: heading "Required scope".
  - 531: keep the v0.0.2 circuit model and its analytic regression tests.
  - 532: add an explicit alveolar gas compartment driven by alveolar ventilation.
  - 533-534: add sevoflurane blood:gas and tissue:blood partition data, with source provenance and validation.
  - 535-536: add cardiac output and perfusion-limited vessel-rich, muscle and fat tissue groups.
  - 537-538: compute mixed-venous return from tissue outflow and couple it back to the lung-blood exchange.
  - 539-540: fresh gas flow, delivered sevo concentration, alveolar ventilation and cardiac output can change during a run without resetting state.
  - 541-543: show at least the delivered, circuit/inspired, alveolar/end-tidal, and mixed-venous or representative tissue concentrations, in snapshots and in the interface.
  - 544-546: track agent delivered, exhausted, and stored in gas, blood and tissue compartments, so that mass balance can be checked within a documented numerical tolerance (value and place not given).
  - 547-548: keep explicit simulation time, deterministic results, and the scientific core separate from Flet.
  - 549-550: document equations, units, assumptions, parameter sources, numerical method and model limitations in `docs/MODEL.md`.
  - 551-552: add unit, integration, invariant and independent reference tests before the release is tagged.
  - 554: heading "Definition of done".
  - 556: v0.1.0 is complete only when all of the following hold.
  - 558-559: the locked Python 3.14 environment passes Ruff formatting and linting, strict mypy, pytest and GitHub Actions.
  - 560: the v0.0.2 circuit reference behaviour is unchanged.
  - 561: zero-flow and zero-ventilation limits behave safely.
  - 562: concentrations stay finite and nonnegative.
  - 563-564: more ventilation or cardiac output gives the expected directional effects in documented reference scenarios (not named).
  - 565: wash-in and washout are stable across supported step sizes (values not given).
  - 566: total sevoflurane mass closes within the stated tolerance (not stated here).
  - 567: Start, Pause, Reset and live parameter changes behave deterministically.
  - 568-569: the interface states that this is an educational model, not a clinical prediction.
  - 570: no deferred feature has entered the release by accident.
  - 572-574: all of these criteria were met when the release was tagged (no date given). `docs/MODEL.md` § "Release gate" holds the matching checklist for the scientific documentation.
  - 576: heading "Explicitly out of scope for v0.1.0".
  - 578: out of scope: desflurane, isoflurane, nitrous oxide, simultaneous gases.
  - 579-580: out of scope: vaporizer interlocks, agent switching, direct injection, flush, automated end-tidal control.
  - 581-582: out of scope: multiple alveolar units, dead space, shunt, ventilation-perfusion mismatch.
  - 583: out of scope: metabolism, renal or hepatic clearance, ECMO, cardiopulmonary bypass.
  - 584-585: out of scope: MAC, BIS/eBIS, nociceptive response, hemodynamic response, decision support.
  - 586: out of scope: IV anesthetics and effect-site models.
  - 587: out of scope: scenario persistence, replay, comparison, forking.
  - 588-589: out of scope: packaging, signing or release installers beyond what is needed to run and test the milestone.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Completed: v0.2.0 - isoflurane and desflurane", rows 591-676
  - 591: heading. Milestone v0.2.0, completed, adds isoflurane and desflurane.
  - 593: subheading "Goal".
  - 595-601: aim. Prove that the v0.1.0 patient model (circuit, alveolar, tissue and venous compartments, with governing equations in `docs/MODEL.md`) is generic across agents and not specific to sevoflurane. Do it by adding isoflurane and desflurane as validated, data-driven volatile agents, each with independent reference cases. No governing equation changes in the milestone. It adds only per-agent data and the loading capability to select among agents.
  - 603: subheading "Required scope".
  - 605-607: add `data/agents/isoflurane.json` and `data/agents/desflurane.json`, schema-validated like `data/agents/sevoflurane.json`, with cited blood:gas and tissue:gas partition coefficients and source provenance.
  - 608-615: extend agent loading in `core/parameters.py` so that an agent is selected by id instead of always loading sevoflurane. `load_sevoflurane_parameters()` is "currently" the only path `RespiratorySystem.default()` uses, and it is hardcoded. Consider migrating the module's JSON validation to Pydantic, because the boilerplate in its `_require_*` helpers grows with each agent schema. The module is isolated behind plain dataclass returns, so the switch is low-risk.
  - 616-619: add independent reference tests for isoflurane and desflurane wash-in, uptake and washout. Follow the sevoflurane pattern of directional solubility, equilibrium and mass balance in `tests/reference/test_sevo_patient.py`.
  - 620-623: document parameter provenance of both new agents in `docs/MODEL.md`. Note agent-specific numerical points, such as desflurane's low blood:gas solubility, though they do not change the governing equations.
  - 624-625: keep the v0.0.2 circuit and v0.1.0 sevoflurane reference tests unchanged.
  - 627: subheading "Definition of done".
  - 629: lead. v0.2.0 is complete only when all of the following are true.
  - 631-632: `isoflurane.json` and `desflurane.json` pass the same schema and range validation as `sevoflurane.json`.
  - 633-635: each new agent has independent reference tests showing correct directional solubility behaviour and mass balance closing within the documented tolerance.
  - 636-637: the v0.0.2 circuit and v0.1.0 sevoflurane reference tests are unchanged and still pass.
  - 638: `docs/MODEL.md` documents parameter provenance of both new agents.
  - 639-640: Ruff formatting and linting, strict mypy, pytest and GitHub Actions all pass.
  - 642: subheading "Explicitly out of scope for v0.2.0".
  - 644-652: out of scope: a UI control for selecting the agent. Agent switching and interlock behaviour belong to the anesthesia-machine milestone (item 1, § "Planned milestones"). The milestone needs only the model able to run as isoflurane or desflurane, not a choice in the interface. This was the scope recorded at tagging. A basic picker was added afterwards in commit `00791b1`. It restarts the run rather than switching agents mid-run. Interlock and residual-washout work is still outstanding.
  - 653-655: out of scope: physics of the vaporizer as a delivery device, such as desflurane's heated, pressurized vaporizer. The milestone models uptake and distribution only.
  - 656-661: out of scope: halothane, enflurane, ether and xenon, a stretch goal beyond this milestone. Halothane is clinically obsolete but has reasonable modern published data. Enflurane, ether and xenon have sparser data and are harder to source. Each of those three needs its own provenance check before it is added. None should be assumed available because the Gas Man reference simulator shows it.
  - 662-663: out of scope: nitrous oxide, because it is not a halogenated volatile. Items 6-7 in § "Planned milestones" cover it.
  - 664-666: out of scope: any change to the v0.1.0 governing equations. The milestone proves them agent-generic and does not change them per agent.
  - 668-676: outcome. All criteria were met when the milestone was tagged. Isoflurane and desflurane load through `load_agent_parameters(agent_id)`, added for this milestone. `load_sevoflurane_parameters()` is now a thin wrapper over it, so no existing call site changed behaviour. `tests/reference/test_multi_agent.py` covers directional solubility and mass-balance closure for both new agents. The parameter provenance table in `docs/MODEL.md` and its new "v0.2.0: isoflurane and desflurane" subsection record where both agents' parameters came from.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Completed: v0.2.8 - the workflow works", rows 678-1074
  - 678: Heading "Completed: v0.2.8 - the workflow works".
  - 680: Heading "Goal".
  - 682-687: Aim: make the development loop reliable before two long milestones run through it. All work is machinery the project already depends on: the merge path, the release script, the queue's ranking, the session-start digest, the type-check and lint gates, and the instructions a session reads first. None of it touches the simulator.
  - 689-697: Why this release comes first.
    - A standing decision in `CLAUDE.md`, dated 2026-08-30: workflow work outranks product work until the workflow is settled. The reason: friction in the loop is paid again every session, a deferred feature once.
    - v0.3.0 clears twenty entries of inherited product debt, and v0.4.0 builds the first teachable case on it. Both are long runs through the loop.
    - Three faults cost more than fixing them: the merge path does not wait for CI, the release script stops halfway, and `docket next` ranks work the current milestone excludes.
  - 699-703: Versioning. A patch under the ordinary rule in § "Versioning decision". It crosses no capability boundary and adds nothing to the simulator: no change to any equation, parameter, numerical method, unit or displayed value. Gate 0's minor-version exception is not extended to this release, and it does not need it.
  - 705: Heading "Debt gate: the frozen list".
  - 707-713: The freeze. Frozen 2026-08-30, the day the release was scoped, at seventeen entries. More were admitted later under the completion rule and the scope test, and one at the owner's direction. The list is the entries below. The text after the list sets out what the freeze closes. Each entry records its outcome when it closes, per § "The cadence".
  - 715-725: The list is not a fourth gate in the cadence.
    - The first group is debt as "What counts" defines it, and every entry in it is classed `defect`. The second group is new workflow capability, which "What counts" says is not debt and does not hold a gate closed.
    - It sits under "Debt gate" because `bin/docket wave` reads that subsection. A release records a gate to say it comes ahead of an already open gate, so the beat follows the release (queue item `PL-NSN9`).
    - The numbered gates are unaffected. Gate 0 is recorded under v0.4.0, still binds v0.4.0, and becomes the nearest gate again once this release ships.
  - 727-730: The closed count is not recorded here, for the reason given in the v0.4.0 section: a written count goes stale when an item closes. `bin/docket wave` checks the entries against `docs/items/` and reports closed and open.
  - 732-735: The list's size is not restated in prose, for the same reason. The group headings and the two table rows that name this release give it, and `tools/doc_check.py` enforces that those three match the entries.
  - 737: Group label: "The loop is visibly broken without these — twenty-two entries".
  - 739: PL-J786 (S): require a green `checks` run before any merge into main.
  - 740: PL-64LS (S): detect items stranded on an unmerged branch.
  - 741-742: PL-8HJ2 (S): `make release` stops midway on the ROADMAP table it does not write, so every release ends in a red test.
  - 743-744: PL-M5FK (S): this file's tag statements go stale every release, and no check reads them.
  - 745-746: PL-1TPM (S): `docket next` ranks work the current milestone excludes and gives no sign of it.
  - 747-748: PL-019F (S): the "what should we work on next" rule answers one level below the roadmap step that should decide it.
  - 749-750: PL-5YK8 (S): the `verify:` advisory nags about grandfathered items, so it can never reach zero.
  - 751-752: PL-H7XN (M): `CLAUDE.md` keeps every rule resident, which reduces adherence to the rules a session needs.
  - 753-754: PL-NSN9 (S): a self-gating milestone reports `implement` when its gate clears, where `release` is due.
  - 755-761: PL-J295 (S).
    - Problem: the release-train check reads a tag missing from a shallow clone as never tagged, so `make check` fails on unmodified `main` in every web-session container.
    - Added after the freeze because it completes `PL-XCYB`. That entry established that a check must refuse to answer in a shallow checkout rather than answer wrongly, and it fixed the pull-request reader. The tag reader had the same exposure and was missed. Points to the rule beneath the list.
  - 762-768: PL-KWC1 (S).
    - Problem: `docket flight` reads item ids from branch names only. A branch the web harness named carries none and is invisible, so `docket next` hands out items another session is implementing.
    - Added after the freeze because it completes PL-64LS. That entry read the same refs through the same module and left the in-flight signal blind on every branch the project produces.
  - 769-777: PL-1CYR (S).
    - Problem: nothing re-checks the branch against `main` once a session is underway, so a discussion that becomes implementation builds on a base that moved.

  - 936-943: What the freeze closes (project owner, 2026-08-30).
    - Closed to new scope: new behavior, new capability, or another workflow idea raised during the release.
    - Open to work an entry needs before it is done: a post-freeze finding belongs if it addresses what a frozen entry fixes, and it is recorded with what it completes.
    - Shipping a half-fixed entry keeps the list's length but defeats its purpose.
  - 945-949: Not a new rule: it is the correct reading of "The gate is a snapshot, not a moving target", which already admits a finding that continues or completes a frozen item. This paragraph first said the opposite, and that was wrong.
  - 951-961: The list is a scope, not a set of items (project owner, 2026-08-31).
    - The scope decides membership, and the completion rule is one case of it. The scope is the goal: a loop reliable enough for two long milestones, across the named machinery (the list repeated).
    - A finding that a named piece misfires is in scope however late, whether or not it completes an entry. Otherwise a release titled "the workflow works" would ship with the workflow broken.
  - 963-971: "New scope" means a different goal: a new workflow capability, a better tool nobody is blocked by, or an idea the release prompted. It does not mean a new instance of the frozen goal. A defect in named machinery is a third case, and the list admits it. The paragraph once offered only two cases, and on 2026-08-31 nine findings went to the queue as neither, though seven were defects in the digest, the release script, the merge path and the lint gate.
  - 973-979: Two limits.
    1. The finding must be a defect in machinery the goal names. An improvement does not count, and neither does unnamed machinery.
    2. It must be workable here. A cause outside the repository means the finding is captured and left off the list, because nothing in the tree could close it and the gate would stay open forever.
  - 981-983: A `safety` or `science` finding goes to Gate 0, because it concerns the simulator, which this release does not touch. A `P0` finding is a hotfix on its own branch under the `docket` skill and joins no list.
  - 985-988: Four entries came in under the completion rule: PL-J295, PL-KWC1, PL-XCYB and PL-WFJ9. "The rest" came in under the scope test, each marked with its date and reasoning. "The scope test is described below this rule."
  - 990-997: PL-XCYB is paired with PL-ZQ9C. It was found while ZQ9C was handed to a session, during a check of whether ZQ9C's migration was feasible. Without it, the check fails loudly on correct data in the environment where most sessions run, and ZQ9C would close half-fixed. The two are worked and closed together.
  - 999-1007: PL-WFJ9 is the same case one step later, found by the entry it completes. Widening the gate to `subprojects/docket/src` exposed the `render.py` suppressions that let the gate pass over that file. Closing PL-020 without it would be a half-fixed close, leaving unchecked the digest and `status` renderers, which sessions read first and which answer "what next". It is worked with PL-020.
  - 1009-1022: PL-68XK (check that a recorded commit hash resolves) is not admitted.
    - It predates the freeze and was left off the approved list on purpose. While entangled with PL-ZQ9C, its brief was refreshed, not worked.
    - When ZQ9C landed, `pr:` beside `commit:` made `commit:` optional, which narrowed 68XK to requiring that a `commit` value, when present, resolves.
    - PL-T63T: the owner retired the field because 21 of the 42 checkable hashes resolved nowhere, and 68XK was dropped with it. PL-XCYB's shallow-checkout discipline is unaffected and still serves the `pr:` check.
  - 1024: Heading "Definition of done".
  - 1026-1027: Every entry is `done`, or `dropped` with its reason recorded. `bin/docket wave` reports the split against `docs/items/`.
  - 1028-1029: `make check` passes: `ruff format`, `ruff check`, `mypy`, `pytest`, `docket check` and `tools/doc_check.py`.
  - 1030-1034: PL-J786 (green `checks` run before merge) and PL-S4M2 (squash-merge) change repository configuration. Both are confirmed in effect on a real pull request. No command can verify them, hence `not-delegable`.
  - 1035-1042: No equation, parameter, numerical method, unit or displayed value changes. The v0.0.2 circuit and v0.1.0 sevoflurane reference tests pass unaltered. Three entries may reach into `src/` and `tests/`, within their briefs: PL-020 may add type annotations, and PL-ZN0N and PL-69J3 may add, remove or annotate `noqa` directives. A change any of them needs to a modelled value is a Gate 0 finding and an item of its own.
  - 1044-1058: Two of the three counts are left unchecked (`PL-GLBF`, 2026-09-13).
    - "Seven entries are marked `not-delegable`" is checked by `tools/doc_check.py` as a query over the `not-delegable:` field. The two counts in the bullets are not checked.
    - `touches:` records files an item is expected to change, not the scope it may reach. A checker over it computes two where the answer is three: `PL-ZN0N` declares only `pyproject.toml` yet may annotate `noqa` in `src/`. Such a check would fail a correct sentence, which is worse than no check.
    - The list is frozen and every id on it is closed, so neither count can drift. The same shape in a live section could drift, and that question is unsettled.
  - 1060: Heading "Explicitly out of scope for v0.2.8".
  - 1062-1064: Out of scope: anything on Gate 0's frozen list, recorded under v0.4.0. This release does not touch that gate, and clearing it is what v0.3.0 ships.
  - 1065-1068: Out of scope: any simulator change, meaning `src/anesthesia_sim/core/`, `src/anesthesia_sim/app/` and the scientific content of `docs/MODEL.md`. A release claiming the loop is reliable cannot also move the model.
  - 1069-1074: Out of scope: workflow capability beyond the entries, meaning new tools, better tools, or ideas the release prompted. The queue holds more process items than this release ships, and they wait for the next release. The scope test admits only defects in named machinery, and none of these is one. A release that absorbs every idea never ships.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Completed: v0.3.0 - the foundation", rows 1076-1123
  - 1076: Heading "Completed: v0.3.0 - the foundation".
  - 1078: Subheading "Goal".
  - 1080-1084: What the release is for.
    - It clears the inherited backlog, so feature work starts with known defects closed rather than carried forward.
    - It adds no capability. Afterwards the simulator does what it did before, but correctly.
    - The verification gate is widened to cover the inputs the interface can actually reach.
    - The failure path can no longer leave partial state behind.
  - 1086-1091: What the release contains.
    - Its contents are exactly Gate 0's items that fall outside the teachable case's own scope.
    - Those items are listed under § "Debt gate: the frozen list" in the v0.4.0 section below.
    - The release has no scope of its own, so the section is short.
    - The frozen list is the specification, and nothing may be added to it (pointer: § "The gate is a snapshot, not a moving target").
  - 1093-1095: Version number.
    - v0.3.0 is a minor release rather than a patch, by a deliberate exception recorded under § "Versioning decision" above.
    - The exception covers Gate 0 alone.
  - 1097: Subheading "Definition of done".
  - 1099-1100: Every item on Gate 0's frozen list that is outside the v0.4.0 milestone's Required scope is `done`, or `dropped` with its reason recorded.
  - 1101-1102: `make check` passes. It runs `ruff format`, `ruff check`, `mypy`, `pytest`, `docket check` and `tools/doc_check.py`.
  - 1103-1108: The model does not change.
    - No equation, parameter or numerical method has changed.
    - The v0.0.2 circuit and v0.1.0 sevoflurane reference tests still pass unaltered.
    - The list has two safety items: one tightens a verification bound, and the other makes the step transactional.
    - Neither may change a modelled value. If either turns out to need one, that is a finding for the next gate and an item of its own, not v0.3.0's work.
  - 1110: Subheading "Explicitly out of scope for v0.3.0".
  - 1112-1113: Out of scope: anything in the v0.4.0 milestone's Required scope, including six gate-0 items that the milestone clears itself.
  - 1114-1121: Items captured after the freeze.
    - An item captured after Gate 0 was frozen is out of scope if its problem did not exist at the freeze.
    - A finding that was present at the freeze re-enters by the presumption in "The gate is a snapshot, not a moving target".
    - Anything at `P0` or classed `safety`/`science` re-enters, whether it was present or not.
    - History: the bullet once named only the `P0`/`safety`/`science` half. That made it narrower than "The gate is a snapshot" and than step 4 of "The cadence", which agreed with each other (queue item PL-9PMV).
  - 1122-1123: Out of scope: new capability of any kind. The reason given is that a release whose claim is "the ground is now solid" cannot also move the ground.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Completed: v0.4.0 - the teachable case", rows 1125-1620
  - 1125: chapter heading "Completed: v0.4.0 - the teachable case".
  - 1127: heading "Goal".
  - 1129-1136: why the model was not teachable when the milestone was scoped. There were three measurable reasons, stated as they stood on 2026-08-25 and not amended later. A rule for writers says a Goal records the problem and is not rewritten. Required scope addresses all three reasons and records the outcomes.
  - 1138-1142: the run advanced at 1x real time (`app/simulation_view.py`, one 0.1 s step per 0.1 s sleep). Sevoflurane at reference settings has time constants of 135 min (muscle) and 42 h (fat), so the reservoirs behind context-sensitive emergence could not be observed.
  - 1143-1146: the chart showed a rolling five-minute window, on an axis in seconds scaled to the vaporizer's dial maximum. A 1 MAC run filled the bottom quarter, and anything slower than the vessel-rich group scrolled away flat.
  - 1147-1148: every value was a percentage of an atmosphere, so three agents whose MACs differ threefold looked comparable.
  - 1150-1154: the end state. A learner runs one case from induction to emergence, with time compressed, values in the clinicians' unit and a time base that spans the whole case. Changes are marked on the record, and after the vaporizer is turned off the level falls against a labelled reference.
  - 1156-1159: no equation, parameter or numerical method changes. It is a minor release, not a patch, because it adds a displayed clinical unit, a new time base and a run-rate control, which are capability boundaries in what the interface asserts.

The reason: it is an M restructure of the interface layer unconnected to the frozen list, and v0.3.0 adds no capability and clears only inherited debt. Old unrelated debt is the "refilling-queue problem" that the gate replaced Phase 0 to solve.
  - 1345-1357: the deferral stands, and none of PL-WB0X shipped in v0.3.0. The owner settled the rest on 2026-09-02. The brief had given three conflicting placements: stage 1 before PL-DHV7 (v0.4.0), the whole item at Gate 1 (inside v0.5.0), and "a `v0.3.x` patch step". PL-4C41 was filed against the conflict, and it was resolved by splitting the item. Stages 1 and 2 (the pure-module extractions) are in v0.4.0's Required scope, gating the MAC unit and the chart work. Stage 3 (the `SimulationView` decomposition) is PL-B9PY, at Gate 1 inside v0.5.0, required only by v0.5.0's two-runs-at-once comparison.

The rest (this file's duplicated v0.2.3 table row and stale "Current baseline" heading) surfaced later as PL-SWFM. Under the presence rule (project owner, 2026-08-25) it was cleared here rather than at the next gate.
  - 1370: PL-010 (S): stop rebuilding render objects every frame.
  - 1371-1372: PL-Y2GG (S), done: Apache-2.0, chosen by the project owner (commit `d40ff83`).
  - 1374-1380: cleared by v0.4.0 itself, 6 items: PL-DHV7 (MAC unit), PL-VM40 (time from step count), PL-F52R (MAC-awake band), PL-ZRSP (F_A/F_I trace), PL-R3KB (agent selection discarding a run) and PL-011 (bounded history). PL-011 was cleared by being dropped on 2026-09-05 with its reason recorded, which "What counts" accepts. Six of six.
  - 1382-1383: findings made while clearing go to Gate 1, except P0 and safety/science findings, which re-enter this gate.
  - 1385: heading "Required scope".
  - 1387-1392: PL-VM40: simulated time is an exact function of the step count. "Today" `elapsed_s` accumulates. Derive it from an integer counter and fix the step at 0.1 s. A fixed number of steps per tick, never catching up to the wall clock: a slow machine runs slower, not differently.
  - 1394-1402: landed 2026-09-04. `SimulationState` holds the step count and the step size, and `elapsed_s` is their product. The step is fixed per run rather than hard-coded: a different step is refused, and a reset frees it. The interface cadence stays 0.1 s, and a step-refinement study can use a smaller step. `docs/MODEL.md` § "The reproducibility guarantee" states the guarantee and the four things it does not cover.
  - 1403-1405: PL-SN2C: a playback multiplier made of more steps per tick, never a larger step. The rate is always visible beside the clock.
  - 1406-1418: PL-WB0X stages 1 and 2. Stage 1 moves the precision formatters into a module free of Flet. Stage 2 moves chart-series shaping beside the Flet chart-series module (deleted with `PL-25KS`). Neither changes behaviour, and both must pass the existing view tests. The brief names the modules. Stage 1 lands before the MAC unit, which rewrites every formatter and extends `docs/MODEL.md` § "Displayed precision", because that derivation ends in a private static method on a Flet view class. Stage 2 lands before the chart work, because four items touch the chart in the same place.
  - 1419-1421: PL-DHV7: MAC multiples alongside percent across every readout and both chart axes. The agent's `mac_percent` provenance is traceable from the display.
  - 1422-1434: PL-SSBP: a case-length time base in minutes, with selectable scales and a fit-the-run scale. Scoped as 15, 30 and 60 minutes, then settled wider (project owner, 2026-09-04): 15 minutes to 12 hours, geometrically spaced. It was widened because scales over an hour had cost too much per frame until `PL-D9WD`'s bucket cache removed that limit. The gridline interval is derived from the scale. Labels carry units (`3m`, `1h30m`, `12h`) because a bare number would be ambiguous. "Fit run" is the default and always contains the whole run. Past the widest scale the ladder doubles, and it never shows part of a case under a name that claims the whole.
  - 1435-1444: PL-CC23: the vertical scale is in MAC rather than the dial maximum, for an honest comparison across agents. Scoped as "fits the run" and decided otherwise (project owner, 2026-09-04), because a fitted axis rescales a rising curve mid-lesson and the item requires a rule stable within a run. Fixed at 0 to 3 x MAC for every agent, 3 being desflurane's dial maximum in MAC. A trace above the ceiling is named, not drawn as a plateau. Reasoning in `docs/MODEL.md` "s" "The chart's vertical range is set in MAC, and fixed".
  - 1445-1455: PL-DR1Z: every change to fresh gas flow, vaporizer dial, alveolar ventilation or cardiac output is stamped with the simulated time it took effect, held with its state and marked on the chart. This is item 8's recording half, not replay. Changes are kept as `(simulated_time, control, value)` entries rather than a fixed struct, because nitrous oxide (item 6) adds a control and a struct would need a new field and a migration.
  - 1457-1468: landed 2026-09-04 as a frozen `ControlChange` record, which keeps the same immunity through one `control` field holding a stable identifier. It also carries the old value (required by the brief), the history index, the unit and the drag adjustment. A 3-tuple would have grown into an unnamed 7-tuple. See `docs/MODEL.md` § "The control-input timeline".
  - 1469-1478: PL-W3DD: `SimulationHistorySample` keyed by substance rather than six flat floats. There is one substance today, so only the representation changes. Item 12 ("promoted below to Designed for forking") writes its element-wise proof against this shape, and reshaping it afterwards would mean reworking a validated safety property. Nitrous oxide (item 6) needs a second substance, and the MAC readout becomes a fold over substances.
  - 1480-1490: landed 2026-09-05, extended to the store and the chart binding to avoid a half-migration. `RunHistory` is keyed by `RecordedSeries` (substance and quantity). Its substances are fixed at build, and any other set is refused. Wash-in stretches are per substance. A trace is bound to a `RecordedSeries` and paired with the agent in the frame's snapshot. Nothing displayed changed. See `docs/MODEL.md` § "Interface boundary".
  - 1491-1492: PL-R3KB: changing agent becomes an explicit new case, not a silent discard of the run.
  - 1493-1496: PL-F52R: a MAC-awake band showing a cited population median for return of responsiveness. It is labelled as a population reference, never as an individual time prediction.
  - 1497-1500: PL-ZRSP: an F_A/F_I trace with a caveat: it means what the textbook curve means only while the inspired concentration is held constant.
  - 1501-1502: PL-011: a bounded concentration history, needed once a four-hour run at 10 Hz records 144,000 samples.
  - 1504-1507: dropped 2026-09-05, the day of "the cut". The only Required-scope entry the milestone shipped without, kept in the list so the list can be audited.
  - 1509-1519: left at needs-decision, carried nominally to v0.4.1, and closed the same day. The "score architecture" removes the store: state as a closed-form function of the control-input timeline (`PL-T691`), with a score plus one keyframe per control event, about 140 KiB for a 30-day ICU case against 3.16 GB. `PL-2FM6` deletes `RunHistory`. PL-011's measurements are in `PL-T691`'s brief.
  - 1521-1529: this release made the growth reachable, and the score work is unscheduled. At 300x, 12 simulated hours take 2.4 min and about 55 MB, a day 8 min and 110 MB, and a week 34 min and roughly 770 MB. The growth is unbounded and untracked until `PL-T691` and `PL-2FM6` land.
  - 1531-1533: PL-RCTQ: documentation sweep of `docs/MODEL.md` (interface boundary, minimum displayed outputs, displayed precision) and `README.md`.
  - 1535: heading "Designed for forking".
  - 1537-1542: forking is item 12 and is not built here. It requires a branch to reproduce its parent element-wise at every sample up to the branch point. That property is expensive to retrofit and cheap to preserve.
  - 1544-1546: an integer step count removes the accumulation order that would make a resimulated prefix differ.
  - 1547-1548: a fixed 0.1 s step at every playback rate removes the divergence a changing step would cause.
  - 1549-1551: never catching up to the wall clock makes the step count independent of machine speed. This is the subtlest of the three; without it, runs would not reproduce on another computer.
  - 1552-1553: the control-input timeline lets a point between samples be reached by resimulation.
  - 1554-1556: the substance-keyed record keeps item 6 from reshaping the record after item 12's proof exists.
  - 1558-1563: not designed in advance: per-compartment amounts in the snapshot, a schematic view, run comparison and a snapshot policy, all cheap later. The test is whether retrofitting would invalidate recorded runs or merely add a field.
  - 1565: heading "Definition of done".
  - 1567: complete only when all of the following are true.
  - 1569-1571: identical inputs give element-wise identical history at every multiplier and on any machine speed, asserted by a test.
  - 1572-1573: the step is 0.1 s at every multiplier, asserted by a test.
  - 1574-1576: every graphed compartment reads in MAC multiples and in percent, and the MAC value is traceable to its cited source.
  - 1577-1579: a three-hour case runs end to end in a few minutes of wall-clock time, with muscle and fat visibly diverging from alveolar.
  - 1580-1581: every control change is recorded with its simulated time and shown on the chart.
  - 1582-1584: history is keyed by substance, so item 12's proof is written against a shape a second substance will not change (PL-W3DD).
  - 1585-1586: changing agent cannot discard a run without telling the user what will be lost and getting confirmation.
  - 1587-1588: the MAC-awake band and the F_A/F_I trace show at display what they do and do not assert.
  - 1589-1591: `docs/MODEL.md` states the MAC transformation, what a MAC multiple on a non-alveolar compartment does not claim, the reproducibility guarantee and the interface's new obligations.
  - 1592-1594: the v0.0.2 circuit, v0.1.0 sevoflurane and v0.2.0 multi-agent reference tests are unchanged and pass. No equation, parameter or method has changed.
  - 1595-1596: Ruff formatting and linting, strict mypy, pytest and GitHub Actions pass.
  - 1598: heading "Explicitly out of scope for v0.4.0".
  - 1600-1602: nitrous oxide, coadministered gases, and the concentration and second-gas effects (items 6-7). This milestone is deliberately taken ahead of them; see "Development pathway".
  - 1603-1605: the multi-substance patient state (Phase 1) is not required. The view consumes an immutable snapshot, so the MAC readout is the only piece that changes shape.
  - 1606-1607: the anesthesia-machine abstraction, interlocks, agent switching with residual washout, direct injection and end-tidal control (items 1-5).
  - 1608-1610: bookmarks (26), forking (12), save/load (9), replay (10) and run comparison (11), including item 8's replay half. Only recording is in scope.
  - 1611: schematic compartment view (27) and agent cost (28).
  - 1612-1613: a second patient, weight-based scaling, age-adjusted MAC, dead space, airway sampling delay, and any depth, BIS or effect-site model.
  - 1614-1615: horizontal panning (PL-Z7LY), made optional by the fit-run scale.
  - 1616-1620: stage 3, the `SimulationView` decomposition (PL-B9PY). Stages 1-2 are in scope. Stage 3 stays at Gate 1 because only v0.5.0's side-by-side comparison of two branches needs it (project owner, 2026-09-02).

  - 1622: heading "## Completed: v0.4.26 - the interface moves to Qt".
  - 1624-1626, scoping: scoped 2026-09-10 after `PL-QXSB`'s decision the same day. `PL-55DH` built the Qt spike, and `PL-X9T3` measured it on the owner's machine. The owner chose to move.

  - 1632-1649, answered by counting: same method as § "Sequenced past v0.5.0" for the gate entries on 2026-09-14. v0.5.0 has 8 open Required-scope ids:
    - 1 wholly in the rewritten surface: `PL-8PSW`, the two-branch overlay;
    - 2 with a slice there: `PL-RD3B` and `PL-LPLD`, whose durable halves are in `app/controller.py`;
    - 5 never touch it: `PL-CTD7`, `PL-B8MK`, `PL-Z3W6`, `PL-W7H9` and `PL-49R8`.
    Exactly 3 modules imported Flet: `app/main.py`, `app/simulation_view.py` and the chart-series module. From `PL-9KDK` until `PL-25KS`, `tools/import_boundary_check.py` limited `flet` and `flet_charts` to those 3. `PL-25KS` freed all 3 and confined both packages to no module. The check's unused-allowance error asks each port commit that frees a module to drop its entry. The landed work is on the surviving side: `PL-T691` score, `PL-J2TD` keyframe opening, `PL-TFX5` fork and `PL-B9PY` decomposition. So the premise failed, and the move rests on 3 other grounds.
  - 1651-1661, ground 1, `PL-8PSW` would be built blind:
    - `PL-2QMK`: no web-container session can visually confirm a chart change, because Flet fetches Flutter assets and the egress proxy denies them.

  - 1662-1666, ground 2, two P1 safety-classed items were blocked behind the port: `PL-GS3R` (0.26 MAC of drawn departure at the 12 h base, worst in the steep early wash-in) and `PL-YVHK` (the hover readout). Both were designed and waited only on the toolkit. Putting v0.5.0 first would have kept them blocked for a whole milestone.

  - 1678-1685, why a patch number: § "Versioning decision" numbers a release by the capability boundary it crosses. After the port, a learner can do nothing new. The interface-pass row made the same argument for its restyle; that row was written `v0.5.x` until 2026-09-16 and has followed item 34 since. Precedent: v0.2.8, with 38 frozen entries and no simulator change.

  - 1840-1843, input latency: 0.85-2.46 ms p90 on Qt against 20-30 ms on Flet. Latency is what "laggy" named. The gap comes from the mechanism: a control-tree diff blocks Flet's event loop every frame, and Qt has no diff.

They are still named here because `MilestoneStates.ships_with` reads only Required scope. That separates the two meanings of `blocked-by: <version>`: "ships with" and "waits for scoping". `PL-L09X` costed this relation, and nobody writes it twice.
  - 1963: heading "### Fixes this port carries, and why that is not scope creep".
  - 1965-1967: the owner asked on 2026-09-10 whether known fixes should be done in the port (quoted). Answer: yes, under a rule narrow enough to keep a patch.
  - 1970-1975, the rule: a queued fix rides the port when the port rewrites the code the defect lives in and the fix changes nothing a learner can do. Everything else waits. Porting a defect means reproducing it and then rewriting it again, so the fix is cheaper inside the port.
  - 1977-1981, what the rule does not license: new capability of any size, which keeps § "Versioning decision"'s patch argument true; defects in `core/` or the Flet-free modules, which the port does not touch.
  - 1983-1987, the enumeration: parity with the Flet build is the checkable definition of done. A behaviour change breaks the comparison, so every fix is named in advance, and parity means "identical except for this list", which is stricter.

Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Completed: v0.5.0 - the case you can branch", rows 2122-2143
  - **Row 2122, heading:** "Completed: v0.5.0 - the case you can branch". It states that v0.5.0 is completed.
  - **Rows 2124-2135:** The Qt port moved ahead of v0.5.0 and changed only how its display parts are built.
    - v0.5.0's display half now builds on Qt (project owner, 2026-09-14, on `PL-RKWB`).
    - The Qt port moved ahead of this milestone.
    - § "Completed: v0.4.26 - the interface moves to Qt" carries the decision, the count behind it and the three grounds.
    - `PL-8PSW` (the two-branch overlay) and `PL-LPLD`'s bookmark list are built once, on pyqtgraph, not first on Flet and then again.
    - Nothing else about the milestone moves. Its goal, required scope, definition of done and out-of-scope list are unchanged.
    - The port does not touch the milestone's port-neutral spine, which ships on the schedule it had.
    - The spine is `PL-CTD7`, `PL-B8MK`, `PL-Z3W6`, `PL-W7H9`, `PL-49R8`, and the durable halves of `PL-RD3B` and `PL-LPLD`.
    - The milestone keeps its number and its meaning: it is the release that crosses the branching capability boundary. The port crosses no capability boundary.
  - **Rows 2137-2143:** What moving the port ahead cost and bought, stated "so it is not re-litigated".
    - It costs the MVP its place in the queue for the length of the port.
    - It gives `PL-8PSW` a toolkit that a session can actually look at (`PL-2QMK`).
    - It unblocks two `P1` `safety`-classed items that were waiting on the toolkit (`PL-GS3R`, `PL-YVHK`).
    - It makes it possible for Gate 1 to reach zero before the milestone it gates begins.
    - § "Sequenced past v0.5.0" records that as the structural debt that `PL-D143` named and could not pay.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Goal", rows 2145-2204
  - 2145: heading "Goal".
  - 2147-2151: the goal is to make a comparison possible.
    - v0.4.0 made one case observable, with compressed time, clinical units and an axis as long as the case.
    - A single curve cannot teach why it moved, because that needs a second curve that differs in exactly one thing.
    - On 2026-09-06, the day this milestone was scoped, three gaps stood in the way.
  - 2153-2158: gap 1 is that a run cannot be stopped at a target.
    - The playback multiplier made a twelve-hour case reachable, but it cannot be used for anything precise.
    - A learner comparing gas-management strategies has to watch the clock and pause by hand.
    - At 300x, a hand pause overshoots by minutes of simulated time. It lands somewhere different on every attempt, and the learner cannot return to it.
  - 2159-2163: gap 2 is that two managements cannot be taken from one point.
    - The only route is to build the case twice.
    - The two runs then differ in everything that was not reproduced identically.
    - A comparison must not do that, because the learner cannot see which differences they caused.
  - 2164-2166: gap 3 is that two runs cannot be displayed at once.
    - `SimulationView` binds one controller.
    - The readouts, chart traces, clinical references and control marks each assume a single run.
  - 2168-2172: a fourth problem, a debt: the run's sample store has no size limit.
    - `PL-011` was dropped on 2026-09-05 on the promise that the score architecture would remove the store rather than limit it.
    - A comparison holds two runs, so this milestone either keeps the promise or the debt doubles.
  - 2174-2179: the end state.
    - A learner runs a case, marks the decision point, forks the case there, manages the two branches differently, and reads both on one time axis.
    - The branch point is marked.
    - Every curve can be traced to the run and settings that produced it.
    - The history before the branch point is identical in both branches, because it is the same history, not a reproduction.
  - 2181-2186: this milestone completes the MVP, as "What MVP means here" defines it (run a case, branch it at a decision point, compare the two managements side by side).
    - It is a minor release, not a patch, because it crosses a capability boundary that the four releases before it did not.
    - The application comes to hold more than one run, and it asserts a relationship between them.
  - 2188-2198: the milestone changes `core/` and what the numerical method can be asked, not what it computes.
    - The exact matrix exponential landed before this milestone (`PL-GS5X`, pull request 376).
    - The score architecture, described "below", turns a run from a sequence of applied steps into a closed-form function of its control-input timeline.
    - The governing equations, the parameters and their exact solution are unchanged.
    - The state at a given time can be computed without stepping the run to that time.
    - That makes a branch's history before the branch point the same object as its parent's history.
    - Item 12 requires that property.
    - It is the property where a test most easily passes for the wrong reason.
  - 2200-2204: what the release holds and leaves out.
    - It promotes planned-milestone item 26 (bookmarks), item 12 (forking), item 11 (side-by-side comparison), and the replay half of item 8 (the resimulation driver only).
    - The replay control that the user sees is item 10, which stays behind item 9's save/load.
    - Item 9 is out of scope (project owner, 2026-09-06).
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Debt gate: the frozen list (run 1 of 3)", rows 2206-2621
  - 2206: heading "Debt gate: the frozen list".
  - 2208-2218: what the list holds and how it is counted.
    - Frozen 2026-09-06, the day the milestone was scoped. Holds every open item that is debt by § "The debt gate".
    - Debt means classed `defect`, `safety`, `science`, `refactor` or `perf`, or at `needs-decision`.
    - The largest gate the project has held: Gate 0 held 21 items and v0.2.8's frozen scope held 38.
    - The group headings and the timeline row naming this release state the size, and `tools/doc_check.py` checks those numbers against the list.
    - The section records no closed count, for the reason the v0.4.0 section gives: a written count goes stale when an item closes.
