Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Debt gate: the frozen list (run 2 of 3)", rows 2623-3165
  - 2623-2631: An entry, named PL-LT51 only at 2643, is admitted, not deferred, because it has twice been shown it can be closed.
    - The case for deferring: it needs three papers from the 1960s and 1970s, PubMed holds no abstract or full text for them, and the egress proxy refuses every publisher.
    - The case against: the owner turned around two interlibrary loans in three days for this chain, `PL-7HDS` on 2026-09-08 and `PL-8SDL` on 2026-09-10.
    - Reference 26 was read on the day the item was filed.
    - Deferring would shrink the gate on a claim "the last week" disproves.
  - 2633-2641: What the reading has established.
    - ICRP Committee II Table 8, p. 151, is *Organs of standard man*. It gives masses and effective radii and no blood flows.
    - So the perfusion fractions cannot come from the one reference of the four that is not itself a model. These fractions set every time constant the model computes.
    - They come from references 9, 19 or 20, which are uptake-and-distribution models.
    - The chain of sources behind `reference_adult.json` ends at tier 2 in both halves and reaches tier 1 nowhere.
  - 2643: List line, PL-LT51 (M): obtain and read Lowe and Ernst's four upstream references for the reference patient's volumes and flows, starting with ICRP Committee II page 151.
  - 2645: Group heading: added 2026-09-12 under the unconditional safety/science exception, 7 entries.
  - 2647-2653: Who found these entries, and the admission rule.
    - Six were found by the triage pass that took the queue from 46 untriaged captures to none (`PL-3B47`). A seventh was added later the same day and is explained at the group's foot.
    - All seven are `safety` or `science`. They re-enter whatever the presence test says, under the second of the two unconditional exceptions in "The gate is a snapshot".
    - None is in Required scope, so all seven clear before implementation begins.
  - 2655-2669: Four entries are cases of the specification disagreeing with the shipped tree, the class of problem the exception exists for. A reader consults `docs/MODEL.md` to decide whether to trust a displayed value.
    - `PL-7KDC` and `PL-S3Q0` follow from `PL-8ZJQ`, which adopted Davis and Mapleson for the venous pool.
    - `PL-7KDC`: the paragraph counting what the parameter set rests on still says all eleven values are Gas Man's.
    - `PL-S3Q0`: two notes still say a tier-2 source may never be the authority for a stored value, which the project has now done.
    - `PL-Q5NS`: an omission from Known limitations, worth about 20% of every agent's fast pool.
    - `PL-MMWX`: `ROADMAP.md` item 30 scopes weight as an input but does not record that the scaling is safe only as a coupled package. A session scoping from the item alone would ship paediatric kinetics wrong in the direction taught most.
  - 2671-2683: `PL-RFLN` is admitted on the same evidence as PL-LT51, and has since closed.
    - It needs the methods sections of two 1991 papers that neither PubMed Central nor `docs/references/` holds. The owner has turned around three interlibrary loans for this kind of chain.
    - The task is bounded: either name the cause against the methods, or record that the question cannot be answered from what the project can reach, in place of the two open candidates.
    - It closed on 2026-09-13 the second way, and went one candidate further. The methods were read and the apparatus was excluded. End-tidal sampling was struck on Carpenter & Eger's measurement of the gradient it depends on.
    - Only the published value is left, and nothing the project runs can settle it.
  - 2685-2694: `PL-YLKR` is admitted for what the tooltip may imply.
    - The tooltip shows a bare number, with no unit, compartment or agent, and nothing marking the value as modelled.
    - `PL-KP7H` made the tooltip appear only while the run is paused, which is when a reader studies a number: the most time and the least context.
    - A modelled alveolar fraction read as a measurement is the confusion `CLAUDE.md` names.
    - The design does not depend on the toolkit and survives the port; only the implementation moves.
  - 2696-2707: The seventh entry is `PL-DZFJ`: found by the `PL-0QQP` triage pass on 2026-09-12, corrected 2026-09-13.
    - Until 2026-09-13, the paragraph above argued from a mechanism the tree no longer has: every drawn point was an M4 representative of its bucket, about 120 recorded samples at 300x, and the hover "presents it as the value at that instant".
    - `PL-2FM6` deleted `RunHistory`, `HistoryWindow`, `SimulationHistorySample` and the M4 decimation module in v0.4.12.
    - The chart now evaluates the run's score at the instants it plots, and a control event always gets its own column, so the hover's "value at that instant" is correct.
    - The gate's stated reason for admitting a `P1` `safety` entry was false, in the direction that made the entry look necessary.
  - 2709-2719: The admission survives the correction, and the argument does not, so the paragraph was rewritten rather than removed.
    - A bare number with no unit, compartment or agent is a presentation-safety matter, whatever produces it.
    - A correct number with nothing marking it as modelled is a failure `CLAUDE.md` names.
    - `PL-YLKR` stays, on the same ground as `PL-MMWX`: a wrong statement here about what a displayed value represents misleads the session that scopes the work.
    - `PL-YLKR`'s brief carried the same sentence. Its `touches` are `docs/MODEL.md`, `README.md` and the chart-series module, and the brief was corrected with this paragraph.

  - 2731-2735: These entries were found by the triage pass over the fourteen captures open that day (`PL-TQJ4`). `PL-V6M0` is `safety` and re-enters whatever the presence test says, under the second exception. It is not in Required scope, so it clears before implementation begins.
  - 2737-2747: What PL-V6M0 is.
    - `PL-YK2V` widened the catch in `_apply_setting`, so that any exception outside the project hierarchy halts the run.
    - The narrow arm it kept catches `AnesthesiaSimulationError`, the base class.
    - That is right for `SimulationConfigurationError`, where a value was refused and nothing was miscalculated.
    - It inverts `SimulationExecutionError`, whose docstring in `core/exceptions.py` says the run must stop. Such an error would be reported as a refused setting while the run keeps producing readings.
    - `SimulationDomainLimitError` would likewise be mislabelled as a refusal, when it means the run has reached the edge of its supported domain.
  - 2749-2759: Why the `anticipated` class does not defer PL-V6M0.
    - No route raises either type into `_apply_setting` today, which is why the item is classed `anticipated`.
    - `checks.py` exempts that class from the band only at `status: blocked`, and nothing blocks this item. Its trigger is a future route, not a named item or milestone.
    - So the exemption is unavailable, and that is no reason to rank safety work lower.
    - v0.5.0 makes the route concrete. It turns a run from a sequence of applied steps into a closed-form function of its control-input timeline, so a setting can be applied to a run that has already been evaluated. That is the "applied by re-stepping" route the item names, newly in play.
  - 2761-2774: The second entry, `PL-DJYF`, came from the triage pass over the twenty-seven captures open later the same day.
    - It is `science` for the same reason as its twin `PL-4YY1`, which records provenance for the circuit volume and default fresh gas flow, is already on this gate, and shipped in v0.4.20. It re-enters whatever its presence answer.
    - `AlveolarCompartment` carries `gas_volume_l` and `alveolar_ventilation_l_min` as dataclass defaults. `data/patients/reference_adult.json` stores both values with provenance, and nothing keeps the two copies equal.
    - `PL-4YY1` pinned the circuit's copies with a test, which `PL-M85T` later retired along with the default.
    - A constant free to drift gives a run on a value the specification does not name, presented with provenance that looks correct. This is the class of finding the exception exists for.
    - It is not in Required scope, so it clears before implementation begins.

  - 2993-3003: PL-7DMJ (S), added 2026-09-19, `science`.
    - `docs/MODEL.md` never names water vapour. Alveolar gas is saturated at 47 mmHg, so dry agent from the circuit is diluted by 47/760, or 6.2 %, before any uptake. The alveolar equation V̇A(FI − FA) has no such factor.
    - Changing the equation is out of scope: Gas Man makes the same simplification, and the partition coefficients were assembled against it.
    - The fault is narrower. Nine smaller simplifications are listed, but this one is in neither "Assumptions" nor "Known limitations", so a reader sizing the model against a real circle system cannot see it.
    - Its presence answer is yes: the omission predates the freeze by the document's whole life.

  - 3060-3070: PL-D126 (S), added 2026-09-20, `science`.
    - Gas Man is the project's reference implementation, compared against throughout `docs/MODEL.md`.
    - Candries, De Wolf and Hendrickx's prospective in-patient validation (*J Clin Monit Comput* 2022;36(6):1881–1890) splits Gas Man's agreement by phase. Over the first 15 minutes: MDPE 18% and MDAPE 21% on the FLOW-i, and 7% and 13% on the Zeus. Over the last 45 minutes: 0% and 6%, and −1% and 5%.
    - The authors say the model performs better for maintenance than for wash-in.
    - Wash-in is the phase the simulator shows most, and § "Known limitations" does not say so.
    - It is a limit of a comparison, not a defect in this implementation.
  - 3071-3083: PL-WJNS (S), added 2026-09-20, `science`.
    - § "Breathing circuit" says "an equal fresh-gas volume leaves through the exhaust at the current mixed-circuit concentration". § "Circuit exhaust" and the mass-balance identity assume this always holds.
    - That is true of a semi-closed circle, not of every machine in every mode. Meyer et al. record that on the Zeus "the surplus gas valve can be closed to prevent any loss of gas volume", which is a different conservation statement.
    - Confirmed at triage: `docs/MODEL.md` states no semi-closed assumption anywhere, and § "Model boundary" and § "Assumptions" are silent. A reader cannot tell whether closed-circuit machines were excluded or overlooked.
    - It matters before any machine implementation, because this is the one machine variable that reaches an equation rather than a coefficient.

  - 3096-3099: The problem is older than `PL-B8MK` in the code, but not in how easily a learner could meet it. The comparison has always been strict. Before bookmarks could be forked, a mark sat on a fork instant only if the learner had marked a control-event instant.
  - 3101-3124: PL-3K9B (S), added and decided 2026-09-20, `safety`.
    - A branch taken at a halt starts on the crossing it was taken at, yet the panel says the crossing has not happened on that run.
    - Three cases were measured on 2026-09-20, all with one root:
      - A time bookmark on the step grid reads `still_running` ("the run can still reach this mark"), which no step can make true. `crossed_between` is `before_s < instant_s <= after_s`, the branch's first `before_s` is the fork, and every whole-second mark is on the grid (3 600 of 3 600 over 1 s to 3 600 s).
      - An instant off the grid reads `before this branch opened`, so which row a learner gets depends on whether the typed number is a multiple of 0.1 in binary.
      - A MAC target reads `still_running` on the branch and `reached` on its trunk, for the one crossing the two runs share.
    - Four more cases were then measured, two of them on a trunk with no branch: a mark added behind a running clock, a fork taken at a control event, a mark re-added at a fork instant, and a branch `reset()`.
    - The root: `still_running` is inferred from the mark's absence from the halt set, when the question is whether the instant is still ahead on a clock that only runs forward.
    - Decided (project owner, 2026-09-20, ratified, over reusing `REACHED` for the at-or-behind case and over the item's original seed-and-reorder answer): add `PASSED` as a fifth `MarkStanding`, and decide a time bookmark from the run's own clock. `REACHED` keeps its meaning and its word for MAC targets.
    - The decision unmasks `PL-4KZD`.
  - 3126-3138: PL-4KZD (S), added 2026-09-20, `safety`.
    - The bookmark panel is drawn once from `snapshotsPassage 0`. With two runs shown, it gives the reference run's standings for a set every run carries, and nothing on the row names a run.
    - `_refresh_bookmarks` justifies the single read because the marks are shared. That is true of `snapshot.bookmarks` and false of `snapshot.bookmark_standings`, which comes from each run's own clock, `began_at_s` and halt set.
    - Measured 2026-09-20: a trunk halted at 0.4 ×MAC reads `reached`, while the branch forked there reads `still_running`.
    - Captured after the freeze, it re-enters under the `safety` exception. The problem is older than the freeze: the panel has read one snapshot since two runs could first be drawn.
    - The problem stays hidden while a standing behaves almost like a permanent latch. `PL-3K9B`'s decision unmasks it.

Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Debt gate: the frozen list (run 3 of 3)", rows 3167-3219
  - 3167-3176, PL-25DD entry. The wash-in state line and the off-scale notice do not name their run.
    - Size S; added 2026-09-21; tag `safety`.
    - Both texts are placed one per run into two shared columns beside the chart (`_place_run`'s `_off_scale_column` and `_wash_in_state_column` holders), and neither text names a run.
    - `WASH_IN_STATE_TEMPLATE` is `"Now: F_A/F_I = {ratio}"`. `off_scale_notice` names compartments and a ceiling.
    - With a branch drawn, two unlabelled lines stack. Only their stacking order tells them apart, nothing on screen says which is which, and the run names sit in a different splitter.
    - Safety reason: `CLAUDE.md` counts a correct value shown under the wrong patient context as a failure of the value. F_A/F_I read off the wrong run inverts the comparison a branch exists to teach.
  - 3178-3182, PL-25DD admission. Admitted although the problem postdates the freeze, for the same two reasons as `PL-LHBY`:
    - reason 1: the rule below makes `safety` not deferrable whatever the presence answer;

    - the widgets are older than the freeze, but their ambiguity is not.
  - 3184, subheading: "Added 2026-09-21 under the unconditional safety/science exception — 2 entries".

    - both were found while implementing `PL-25DD`, not by the `#784` review;
    - `_place_run` distributes a run's widgets across six shared layouts. Fixing two prompted a reading of the other four, and two of those four have the same gap;

    - Size S; added 2026-09-21; tag `safety`.
    - `_holder(self._notice_column, (run.build_notice(),))` puts one bare `NoticeLabel` per run into a shared column.
    - All three notices it carries say something about a run and name none: `FAILURE_NOTICE_TEMPLATE` "Simulation stopped"; `SUPPORTED_LIMIT_NOTICE_TEMPLATE` "this run reached the supported run length"; `REFUSED_SETTING_NOTICE_TEMPLATE` "The simulation is unchanged and still running its previous setting".
    - Safety reason: the last notice is false of the run it is not about. The hazard table's existing row for reading a failed halt as a pause loses the distinction it makes as soon as two runs are drawn.
  - 3208-3219, PL-C3GS entry. The sidebar panels do not name their run.
    - Size M; added 2026-09-21; tag `safety`.
    - `_holder(self._sidebar_column, run.build_sidebar_panels())` puts two panels per run into a shared column. With a branch, the sidebar reads Agent accounting validation / Control changes / Agent accounting validation / Control changes, and no panel names its run.
    - `CONTROL_TIMELINE_CAPTION` is "What was changed during this run, most recent first". "This run" says nothing about which run, and the panel lists settings and the simulated times they took effect.
    - Safety reason: a branch exists to compare two ways of managing one patient, so a setting change read off the wrong panel says the trunk received an intervention it never got.
    - It is M rather than S because the headings are built inline in `build_sidebar_panels` rather than held on the instance, so naming the run needs the plumbing that `set_run_name` has.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Declined to Gate 2, because this milestone's own work created them", rows 3221-3249
  - 3221: heading. The section holds the Gate 2 entries declined because this milestone's own work created them.
  - 3223-3232: why two findings are deferred from Gate 2. The rule "The gate is a snapshot, not a moving target" requires either decision to be recorded. The same adversarial review of `#784` found PL-WG73 and PL-624C, and it also found PL-25DD ("above") and PL-LHBY. PL-25DD and PL-LHBY were admitted; PL-WG73 and PL-624C were deferred. The rule made the difference, not a judgment about severity. Neither is classed `safety` or `science`, so neither gets the unconditional re-entry. The presence test's result is no. `git log -S` dates both symbols to commit `d048aca` (2026-09-20), the pull request that built the fork control. That pull request was work done while implementing the milestone Gate 2 protects, which is the case the rule names explicitly.
  - 3234-3241: PL-WG73 (S), deferred 2026-09-21. `COMPARING_FORK_LOCK_TEXT` tells the learner to "Reset the case", but the only Reset label in `app/` is "Reset". Once a branch is drawn, the two Reset buttons do opposite things. The trunk's button ends the comparison. The branch's button, the one the learner reaches for, discards everything simulated since the fork and leaves the lock in place. Labels: `defect`, `ux`. It costs the learner the branch's progress and leaves them in a mode they cannot see the way out of. It shows no wrong clinical value, which is why it is `P2` and outside this gate.
  - 3243-3249: PL-624C (S), deferred 2026-09-21. The test `test_the_branch_is_drawn_beside_the_trunk_on_one_time_axis` calls `view.present(False)` itself between the fork and the frame it reads. So the frame it inspects is the one it asked for, and a fork that failed to redraw would still pass. Labels: `defect`, `test`. The project treats a check that passes while its guarantee is void as compounding friction. That rule's arithmetic does not apply here: fixing this makes no other gate entry cheaper, and the capability it fails to cover is this milestone's own. No priority is given.

Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Declined to Gate 2 on the refilling-queue ground (run 1 of 4)", rows 3548-3912
  - **3550-3558:** These twenty-nine are recorded and deferred, as the snapshot rule requires.
    - Facts: project owner, 2026-09-08. The rule "The gate is a snapshot" lets a session defer or decline a finding that passes the presence test, but "either way it must say so and say why". The `PL-36R4` audit found the thirteen admitted above and these twenty-nine. The twenty-nine pass the presence test as clearly as the thirteen. They are deferred on the rule's second ground: pulling them in "would recreate the refilling-queue problem the debt gate replaced Phase 0 to solve".
  - **3560-3566:** The arithmetic is the argument.
    - Facts, all on 2026-09-08: Gate 1 had 132 entries against Gate 0's 21, by far the largest gate the project had held. 89 of its entries were still open. Admitting all forty-two would have made 173 entries, growth of 31% while the gate was not draining. Phase 0 was retired for that shape. A gate that refills faster than it drains is abandoned, which is worse than no gate.
  - **3568-3572:** The four numbers are fixed to 2026-09-08.
    - Facts: they are never recomputed; recomputing them would restate the reasoning in numbers the decision was not taken on. They are not a current gate count. `bin/docket wave` reports where the gate stands now.
  - **3574-3581:** All twenty-nine lie wholly in the workflow lane, which is why this group was chosen.
    - Facts: they are apparatus, held to `.claude/rules/apparatus-standard.md`'s deliberately lower bar. None can affect a reader of the simulator, so deferring them costs the gate nothing it protects. The thirteen with a product-lane half are admitted because they can. Where the two standards compete, the simulator wins. The paragraph applies that rule to the gate's membership.
  - **3583-3599:** An entry's state is read from the store, not written here (`PL-0VFF`, then `PL-B60Q`).
    - Facts: no entry is removed from the list. That permanence is what "the gate is a snapshot" means, and deleting an entry would destroy the record of what was declined and why.
    - History: closed entries once carried a release name, and dropped entries a date. Those marks copied the store. All 48 marks written matched `milestone:` or `closed:`. 36 of 84 closed entries across this list and v0.6.0's deferrals had no mark (counted 2026-09-23, `PL-J6HP`).
    - Now: `bin/docket wave` prints each current-gate deferral with its state: an open entry's status, a shipped entry's release, a dropped entry's date. For an earlier gate's entry, `bin/docket show` prints its status, and its `milestone:` once shipped. Existing marks are kept, because a release name cannot go stale. No new mark is added. The number still open is not written; `bin/docket wave` returns it.

Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Required scope", rows 5222-5497
  - 5222: heading "Required scope".
  - 5224-5233: what the milestone requires.
    - 19 items, listed in the order their dependencies allow.
    - The first 3 are the score architecture that the 2026-09-05 design round filed and the project owner placed here. The next 5 are work on boundaries that this milestone's code moves. The last 11 are the feature.
    - The "nineteenth" item was added on 2026-09-20 (`PL-XJ37`), when the owner settled that forking is what v0.5.0 is. By then every mechanism the Goal sentence depends on had shipped, but no shipped entry point called any of it, so the milestone could have closed with a learner unable to take a branch.
  - 5235-5245: two more items were required here and are already done (`PL-C4RS`, 2026-09-19).
    - `PL-2FM6` deleted `RunHistory`, and the chart is now drawn from the sampler. That deletion paid `PL-011`'s dropped growth debt.
    - `PL-8LXM` deleted the M4 decimation path, with its tests, the cited paper and every reference to them. A sampler that computes any window in closed form has nothing to decimate.
    - Both items moved to the `v0.4.x` track on 2026-09-08 and shipped there. The note on that move said "Required scope below drops to sixteen items".
    - They sit on the frozen gate under the heading that says so, and are named here without a declaration slot, deliberately. This section does not place them.
  - 5247-5252: `PL-T691`, the run becomes a closed-form function of its control-input timeline.
    - Keyframes are kept at every control event, and any window is computed in closed form, so the state at a time is computed, not looked up.
    - It heads the milestone, and every entry below that touches a run depends on it.
    - It makes a branch's history before the branch point the same object as its parent's history.
  - 5253-5259: `PL-P1Z3`, the canonical evaluation rule.
    - Once an instant can be reached by stepping or from a keyframe, the promise that "identical inputs give identical results" needs a rule saying which route is authoritative.
    - The rule is stated in `docs/MODEL.md` and enforced by a test.
    - The item is classed as safety and science, and cannot be deferred within the milestone.
  - 5260-5262: `PL-49R8`, a path-scoped rule against re-introducing a sample store. A later session that adds a convenience buffer is told why the store is absent.
  - 5263-5270: `PL-RD3B`, the trace vocabulary and the boundary between the interface and the simulation core are separated.
    - Two runs need the boundary. Each run needs its own drawn window, addressed through the same vocabulary.
    - Re-briefed 2026-09-14 (`PL-5328`). Until `PL-2FM6` deleted the store, the entry read "The controller's storage … without a second copy of the storage". What is left to separate is the vocabulary that addresses a trace and the window a frame draws. The scope is unchanged.
  - 5271-5273: `PL-TCD1`, the snapshot stops naming six flat compartment floats, so a readout can name which run and which substance it describes.
  - 5274-5276: `PL-1PSX`, the control-input timeline is bounded and is no longer regrouped in full on every frame. In this milestone it is read on every branch operation.
  - 5277-5279: `PL-B9PY`, `SimulationView` is decomposed so that two runs can be drawn at once. v0.4.0 held the item at this gate because only this milestone needs it (project owner, 2026-09-02).
  - 5280-5282: `PL-Y5WR`, the 30-day scenario cap becomes an explicit halt. It is classed as safety. The only item that required it was dropped with `PL-011`, so it is carried here.
  - 5283-5291: `PL-J2TD`, a fork resumes into a live run.
    - The branch opens at a canonical keyframe state of another run, runs on the case's clock, and its run definition opens at the fork instant. From there it advances by the same path as a live run.
    - A learner can manage a branch, which separates it from a second curve.
    - `PL-TFX5`'s fork ("item 12") and the in-loop crossing detection ("two bullets down", `PL-CTD7`) assume this resumption.
    - The work is internal. It adds no user-facing replay control, which stays in planned item 10, behind item 9's save/load.
  - 5293-5309: `PL-J2TD` narrowed 2026-09-14 (project owner).
    - The old wording was "a recorded control timeline can be applied to a run … the mechanism by which a point *between* recorded samples is reached at all".
    - `PL-T691` delivered the applied timeline: the segment list of `RunDefinition` is the recorded timeline, and `state_at` computes any instant in closed form. `tests/integration/test_controller.py` asserts the reproduction to 6.7e-16 as a fraction of one atmosphere.
    - The resimulation driver is refused by § "The canonical evaluation rule", which requires every replayed, compared or branch-opening value to be taken canonically. A driver that re-runs the fixed-step path composes a third order of floating-point operations, whose result is 1.8e-14 from the canonical result ("measured" by that section). The branch-reproduction entry asserts element-wise equality.
    - The resumption is what remains.
  - 5311-5325: `PL-J2TD`'s mechanism replaced (project owner, 2026-09-14, queue item `PL-ZMRT`), with the scope unchanged.
    - The old wording was "with its clock re-based by subtracting the fork instant". That gave the branch a second time frame, and made exactness depend on every caller subtracting the fork instant.
    - The branch's definition now opens at the fork on the case's axis, so there is one time frame. `SimulationController.origin_s` and both subtractions are gone, and the round-trip hazard cannot be represented.
    - This dissolved `PL-2R2C`: a branch's drawn columns were anchored to the branch's own zero and landed at case instants the trunk never draws. `PL-2R2C` needed no fix of its own.
    - `docs/MODEL.md` § "The canonical evaluation rule" carries the measurements.
  - 5326-5339: `PL-LPLD`, time bookmarks and MAC targets as two separately listed collections.
    - The shape follows the Gas Man reference simulator, which sets the minimum scope: an absolute simulated time, and a percent of MAC on any graphed compartment, not the alveolar trace alone. There are two kinds so that a list can show them as Gas Man does.
    - Shipped in v0.4.33 with two deviations. First, the height is stored as a multiple of the running agent's 1 MAC, because every compartment is read in that unit: no conversion is needed, and a target survives a change of agent. Second, the rising/falling/either crossing direction was withdrawn (project owner, 2026-09-20), for the reason under planned-milestone item 26.
    - Halting at a mark is `PL-CTD7`.
  - 5340-5347: `PL-CTD7`, crossings are detected inside the advance loop, with an explicit not-reached outcome that looks different from a reached one.
    - Testing once per drawn frame overshoots by one frame of simulated time, and the overshoot grows with the playback multiplier. A bookmark would then halt at a concentration that depends on playback speed.
    - That is a presentation-correctness failure by the standard in `CLAUDE.md`, and it breaks the reproducibility of a branch taken there.
  - 5348-5350: `PL-TFX5`, a run forks at any control-input event. Forking is flat: one trunk with N branches and no sub-forks. The branch carries its parent's agent and patient.
  - 5352-5359: `PL-TFX5` split 2026-09-14 (project owner).
    - The old wording was "at any control-input event **or bookmark**". The bookmark half was a refused operation: a bookmark's instant is not in general a keyframe, and the keyframe-only rule from `PL-J2TD` admits no other opening point.
    - On the 120 s run with two changes that the fork tests use, 3 of the 1 201 instants where a halt can land are keyframes.
    - The bookmark clause is now the entry below, behind the two items that build a bookmark and its halt. This entry keeps what shipped.
  - 5360-5375: `PL-B8MK` and `PL-TYWQ`, a bookmark is an instant a run can fork at, so a branch can start where the learner stopped.
    - Two routes were compared. Recording a keyframe at the halt makes the branch exact, but moves the trunk's later results, so marking changes the run. Opening at the keyframe before the bookmark costs the trunk nothing and is exact too.
    - Shipped 2026-09-20 on the second route, as `SimulationController.resumed_at_halt` and `BranchedCase.fork_at_halt`. No keyframe is recorded, and the trunk matches the unmarked case element for element.
    - The item waited behind `PL-LPLD` and `PL-CTD7`, which both closed the same day. It stays ahead of the entry below, whose branch "taken between two recorded samples" is this one.
  - 5377-5393: the interface half, `PL-TYWQ`, shipped 2026-09-21.
    - `PL-VKJW` landed the fork panel on 2026-09-20 on a different branch. The panel offered only `BranchedCase.fork_points_s`, the trunk's keyframes, and a bookmark halt adds nothing to that list, because recording no keyframe is what makes the route safe. For a day, the model could fork at a bookmark and the control could not. Example: a learner marks 45 s, is stopped at 45 s, and is offered only induction and the control events.
    - Now a second control sits beside the selector. It appears only while the trunk stands on the halt, and is labelled with the instant the branch will open (project owner, 2026-09-21, ratified, over one list that grows a row while the run is halted). Reason: a row that comes and goes changes the selector's contents under a reader who looked away, while a control that is either there or not can be seen at a glance.
    - The two ways to fork stay as two methods and two fields, which stops a fork at a mark the run no longer stands on. Both ids are declared in this entry.
  - 5394-5397: `PL-Z3W6`, the branch reproduces its parent element by element up to the branch point. A test asserts this at every sampled point, with no tolerance. Where exactness cannot be reached, the divergence is bounded, documented and shown.
  - 5398-5409: `PL-8PSW`, two branches are overlaid on one time axis.
    - Line style and colour show the compartment, as on the single-run chart, and line width shows the run. At most two compartments are drawn while two branches are shown. The branch point is marked.
    - Two stacked panels on a shared time axis were decided against (project owner, 2026-09-06). The comparison is the point of the release, and stacking makes the eye travel.
    - Javed W, McDonnel B, Elmqvist N, IEEE Trans Vis Comput Graph 2010;16(6):927-34, doi:10.1109/TVCG.2010.162: line graphs in a shared space are more efficient for comparisons over small visual spans, and one compartment under two settings is such a comparison.
  - 5411-5421: which run is drawn wider (`PL-8PSW`, 2026-09-16, project owner).
    - The first run is drawn wider, and the second keeps the single-run width.
    - `PL-HLD5` had assumed the curves are merely adjacent. They coincide exactly before the branch point, so the thicker one hides the other there.
    - Widening keeps every trace at 2 px or wider. A 1 px antialiased line renders lighter than its colour and falls below the 3:1 minimum contrast that `.claude/rules/ui-color.md` treats as an error.
    - `docs/MODEL.md` § "The six compartment traces" carries this decision.
  - 5423-5431: the channel assignment was reversed from this bullet's wording before 2026-09-07 (`PL-HLD5`).
    - The old wording was "the run encoded by line style and the compartment by colour". The next day `PL-GVXP` showed that assignment is unavailable: `docs/MODEL.md` § "The six compartment traces" puts the worst pair of traces at 1.01:1 against a 3:1 requirement, so colour cannot carry six categories, and a new palette does not fix that.
    - Line style is the only non-data channel with room for six categories, so it shows the compartment.
  - 5433-5439: why the cap is required.
    - At six compartments no channel is free for the run: style, colour and width are all used, with width from 2 px to 3 px across the six. Capping the drawn compartments frees width.
    - "One shared compartment selection across both runs" is a constraint the design rests on.
    - 2 compartments × 2 runs is 4 curves, which is the comparison a learner makes.
  - 5441-5448: colour shows the compartment in both modes, deliberately.
    - Giving colour to the run would make one channel mean two things across a mode change, and a misread here is a misread of a clinical value.
    - Fallback if two compartments prove too few: three compartments, with the run as a direct label at each curve's end. Position is the strongest channel and survives every colour-vision deficiency. The fallback is to be tested, not assumed.
  - 5449-5457: `PL-VKJW`, a learner takes the fork and the dashboard holds the branch.
    - `main()` builds a `BranchedCase`. `SimulationView` gains a way to add a `RunView` after construction. One control offers `BranchedCase.fork_points_s` and calls `fork_at`.
    - This is the entry the milestone was missing: the four entries above build every part of a branch, none of it is reachable, nothing in `src/` constructs a `BranchedCase`, and `SimulationView` fixes its runs at construction.
    - Added 2026-09-20 (project owner, 2026-09-20, `PL-XJ37`). It delivers the milestone's Goal sentence word for word.
  - 5459-5470: two decisions settled in the item's brief.
    - A second fork is refused while a comparison is shown. The selector is out of scope, so a learner who forks twice could not choose which branch is displayed, and a quiet swap would be hidden state. There is one comparison at a time, and Reset starts the next.
    - A branch's `RunView` shows the running-agent chip in place of the agent dropdown, because `set_agent` refuses on a branch (`PL-TFX5`), and a control that always refuses presents itself as working.
    - These settle `PL-QRD1`'s question, which asked for both to be decided together.
  - 5472-5476: `PL-1XPX`, what the readouts show while two branches are displayed. It is a decision, not an implementation. A readout that does not say which run it describes is a safety-critical failure, because presentation is part of safety. There is more than one defensible answer.
  - 5477-5492: `PL-MN4J`, the chart's hover says which run it is reading.
    - The item is classed as safety. It is milestone-scope debt under § "Debt inside the milestone's own scope", and this gate cannot clear it.
    - The hover is unclear only while two runs are drawn, which happens only in compare mode, so the item cannot be cleared before compare mode. Until then, the hover names everything there is to name.
    - It is the owner's decision, like `PL-1XPX`. It amends `docs/MODEL.md` § "The chart's hover readout: what the tooltip may show", which derives the three-line form, so no session can close it.
    - `PL-8PSW` shipped the legend and panel naming that make a curve attributable, and this item covers the rest. The definition of done requires "no readout, label, legend entry or reference band ambiguous as to which run it describes", which keeps the item in scope.
  - 5493-5497: `PL-W7H9`, what a comparison asserts, in `docs/MODEL.md` and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`. It states what two branches share and what they do not, that a difference can be attributed only to the settings that differ, and that neither branch is a prediction for a patient.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Definition of done", rows 5499-5526
  - 5499: heading "Definition of done".
  - 5501: lead-in. v0.5.0 is complete only when every item below holds.
  - 5503–5505: branch reproduction. A branch taken at any recorded control event or bookmark matches its parent element-wise at every sampled point up to the branch point. A test asserts this.
  - 5506–5508: bookmark halting. A bookmark stops the run on the step that crosses it, at every playback multiplier. A test asserts this. A threshold that is never reached ends in a distinct, visible outcome, not in silence.
  - 5509–5511: comparing runs. Two branches can be read on one time axis. Every curve can be traced to its run and to the settings that produced it. No readout, label, legend entry or reference band is ambiguous about which run it describes.
  - 5512–5513: memory. The memory a run holds grows with its number of control events, not with its length. No sample store remains in `src/`.
  - 5514–5516: documentation. `docs/MODEL.md` states the canonical evaluation rule, what a comparison asserts and what it does not, and the bound on any divergence that could not be eliminated.
  - 5517–5521: backlog. Every item of the frozen list above that is outside v0.5.0's Required scope is `done`, or `dropped` with its reason recorded, or deferred to a later gate on the terms of beat 3 of § "The cadence". `PL-WZVZ` is the only entry deferred: `PL-S5Q9` deferred it to Gate 2.
  - 5522–5524: regression. The v0.0.2 circuit, v0.1.0 sevoflurane and v0.2.0 multi-agent reference tests stay unchanged and pass. No equation, parameter or governing constant has changed.
  - 5525–5526: automated checks. Ruff formatting and linting, strict mypy, pytest, `docket check`, `tools/doc_check.py` and GitHub Actions all pass.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Explicitly out of scope for v0.5.0", rows 5528-5603
  - 5528: heading "Explicitly out of scope for v0.5.0".
  - 5530-5547 (one paragraph): completing the fork and bookmark interaction is out of scope.
    - It is out beyond what Required scope and the definition of done already name.
    - Decision tag: (project owner, 2026-09-21, ratified, over widening this milestone to close the interaction gap before cutting).
    - Owner's quotation: "a bit of a mess with forks and bookmarks ... half implemented in terms of user interaction".
    - On 2026-09-21, nine open items concerned that surface.
    - The milestone keeps four of them and does not defer them. One is in Required scope. Three fall under the definition of done's requirement of "no readout, label, legend entry or reference band ambiguous as to which run it describes".
    - The four are named where they are required, because readers treat this heading as a scope declaration.
    - The five not kept are:
      - `PL-W4XQ`: there is no way back to a mark set behind the clock.
      - `PL-ZW0J` with `PL-8XMV`: whether returning to a mark forks or truncates, and the setting that chooses between the two.
      - `PL-LLBV`: the whole-step fork guard refuses 35.5% of the one-decimal times a user could type.
      - `PL-7TBQ`: `has_recorded_run` is true before the learner has touched the branch.
      - `PL-3QSX`: the unit of a listed MAC target.
  - 5549-5553: the five go to Gate 2.
    - This is "the intended mechanism, not a deferral".
    - Gate 2 freezes on the day this milestone ships and is cleared before v0.6.0's implementation begins.
    - So the interaction debt is paid before the area system is built on top of it.
    - § "The cadence" already arranges this for findings made inside a milestone.
  - 5555-5561: `PL-ZW0J` decided the cut, not the schedule.
    - `PL-ZW0J` asks whether returning to a mark forks, or truncates after a confirmation.
    - The answer decides what the act the milestone is named for does.
    - The owner deferred the question on 2026-09-20 to use the feature first.
    - Widening v0.5.0 would settle the question without that use.
    - So the milestone is cut on its stated definition of done.
  - 5563-5564: scenario save/load (item 9) and a user-facing deterministic replay control (item 10) are out of scope. Only the resimulation driver that replay needs is in scope.
  - 5565-5566: sub-forks of forks are out of scope, and so is any branch structure other than one trunk with N branches (project owner, 2026-08-25).
  - 5567-5575: more than two runs displayed at once is out of scope, and so is the selector that would choose which two (project owner, 2026-09-20, `PL-XJ37`).
    - The comparison is specified for a trunk and one branch.
    - `MAX_DISPLAYED_RUNS` stays 2.
    - N branches may exist.
    - `BranchedCase.branches` returns them in a stable order, so that a later selector does not renumber the branch a learner is looking at.
    - "Selecting which two runs are displayed" is not built.
    - The milestone never compares branch against branch. A comparison is always a branch against the trunk.
    - The milestone does not return to a branch the learner has left.
  - 5577-5584: why the selector was declined.
    - It was declined on the educational question, not the technical one.
    - The display is capped at two runs under either answer, so a selector buys the choice of pair, not more curves.
    - Branch-against-branch was judged not to be a teaching case for a first release.
    - This bullet replaced the sentence "N branches may exist and be selected between", which stated a permitted future as scope.
    - Adding the selector later is additive rather than a rework.
  - 5586-5590: the fork-control entry in Required scope settles a consequence of having no selector.
    - A learner who forks twice could not say which branch is shown.
    - So a second fork is refused while a comparison is displayed, instead of silently replacing the branch on the chart.
  - 5591: persisting bookmarks or branches across a restart is out of scope; it belongs to item 9.
  - 5592: a schematic compartment view (item 27) and agent cost (item 28) are out of scope.
  - 5593-5594: out of scope:
    - nitrous oxide, coadministered gases, and the concentration and second-gas effects (items 6-7);
    - the multi-substance patient state behind them.
  - 5595-5596: out of scope:
    - the anesthesia-machine abstraction;
    - interlocks;
    - agent switching with residual washout;
    - direct injection;
    - end-tidal control (items 1-5).
  - 5597-5598: patient factors (age, sex, weight and body composition) and any change to the MAC basis (item 30) are out of scope.
  - 5599-5600: horizontal panning of the chart window (queue item PL-Z7LY) is out of scope. The fit-run scale makes panning optional rather than necessary.
  - 5601-5603: no change to the governing equations, the parameter set, or the exact solution the matrix exponential computes. The milestone changes the questions the model can be asked, never the answers it gives.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "v0.6.0 - the layout is the reader's", rows 5605-5643
  - 5605, heading: "v0.6.0 - the layout is the reader's". Names release v0.6.0.
  - 5607-5617, what the milestone is and why it is scheduled early:
    - it is the first half of planned-milestone item 34;
    - it was scheduled two releases ahead of its turn (project owner, 2026-09-16, **ratified** on `PL-NMTF`);
    - reason: the design was already settled, and fourteen prerequisites were waiting on a build item that did not exist;
    - the placement, the split and the gate decision were recommendations put to the owner and agreed, so `CLAUDE.md`'s lower bar to reopen applies to each;
    - the owner *specified* item 34's own content, which this milestone builds and does not re-decide;
    - item 34 carries the design and the Blender provenance behind every decision in the section;
    - `docs/interface-provenance.md` carries what was read, what the project adopts and what it deliberately does differently;
    - "This section describes what gets built."
  - 5619-5625, the three words of the layout vocabulary, two of them Blender's:
    - the rule is in `.claude/rules/ui-areas.md`;
    - an Area is a rectangle that reserves screen space and holds one thing;
    - a View is what occupies an Area;
    - a Workspace is a set of Areas geared to a task, and Workspaces are switched as tabs;
    - `PL-C842` wrote `Pane` and `view_kind` while recommending "the container";
    - this section supersedes `Pane` and nothing else in `PL-C842`;
    - a model that says `Pane` while the roadmap says Area is the drift the vocabulary rule exists to prevent.
  - 5627-5643, why the third word is View and not Blender's "Editor":
    - decision tag: (project owner, 2026-09-17, ratified - chosen over keeping `Editor`, and over `Component` and `Widget`);
    - Blender calls an Area's occupant an Editor, and most Blender editors do edit: the 3D viewport, the dope sheet, the text editor;
    - this project's occupants are the concentration graph, the compartment table, the MAC readout and the control panel;
    - only one of the four takes input, and the other three display modelled values;
    - so "Editor" would assert a mutability "the reader does not have";
    - `CLAUDE.md`'s clinical-output standard treats that kind of wrong implication as a presentation defect;
    - `Widget` was unavailable because `QWidget` is the base class of every view;
    - `Component` was rejected as vaguer, not as wrong;
    - `View` restores `PL-C842`'s `view_kind`, is already used by `app/run_view.py` and `app/simulation_view.py`, and collides with no Qt class used here;
    - `PL-GPYV` carries the comparison and the Eclipse precedent;
    - Eclipse uses Perspective/View/Editor, which matches this project on lifecycle but not on multiplicity;
    - wherever `docs/interface-provenance.md` describes Blender, Blender's own editors keep their name.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Goal", rows 5645-5683
  - 5645: heading "### Goal".
  - 5647–5653: the dashboard's arrangement is fixed. Facts: there is one arrangement, and the learner cannot change it. Every surface is an independent widget inside nested splitters whose handles are disabled. That container is `inert_splitter` at `src/anesthesia_sim/app/qt_widgets.py`, a reservation that v0.4.26 built and did not make live. The only way to see a chart larger is to enlarge the whole window. On 2026-09-16, the day this milestone was scoped, there were three gaps.
  - 5655–5659: gap 1, nothing can be resized, split, joined or swapped. Facts: the handles are inert by deliberate reservation, and no set of operations sits behind them. Item 34 asked for four properties: resizable areas, modular views, presets for tasks, and presets the learner owns instead of a fixed shipped set. None of the four is available.
  - 5660–5665: gap 2, no object above the widget tree. Facts: there is no layout model, no View registry, no Workspace and no user-writable state. Nothing under `src/anesthesia_sim/` writes a file, so a layout cannot be named, saved or returned to at the next launch. A saved Workspace would be the application's first user-written data. Its load path would be the first new failure surface since the shipped parameter files.
  - 5666–5676: gap 3, the values the display must show sit inside the container that is about to become live. Facts: `src/anesthesia_sim/app/simulation_view.py:296-309` puts the readout section inside `inert_splitter`. The readout section holds simulated time, the rate at which time advances, the six compartment concentrations and the delivered concentration. The control-input record's panel sits in the sidebar, inside the nested horizontal splitter. Only run state, halt reason and agent identity are outside the container. Once the handles work, a learner could close values that `docs/MODEL.md` says no Workspace may remove. The violation is already built in rather than absent. A session reading today's code would treat `.claude/rules/ui-areas.md` rule 5 as already satisfied, because nothing can be closed yet.
  - 5678–5683: the end state. Facts: the learner divides a window into non-overlapping Areas, each holding one View. They switch between named task Workspaces shown as tabs. They rearrange, duplicate and save their own Workspaces, and return to them at the next launch. Throughout, the values the display must show cannot be closed, cannot be collapsed to make room, and cannot leave the screen with the surface that drew them.

  - 6069 PL-08CR (S): owner decision on whether format_time_bookmark and format_mac_target survive. bookmark_panel now builds every drawn row from the _stated_ helpers.

The three agent files store De Wolf et al. 2012's 2.0% and 1.2% as the same program's set. The two tier-3 descriptions agree on every partition coefficient.

  - 6073 PL-19Y0 (M): a direct write to a checked field skips the constructor and setter checks. Classes: BreathingCircuit, VenousBloodCompartment, TissueGroup, AlveolarCompartment, AgentSimulationValidator. A mark added to a branch after it opened is never checked against its opening step. fork_at at a keyframe the trunk halted on opens on no crossing, while fork_here opens on the halt's.

At step time the same record shows as a SimulationNumericalError in L/s, while the readouts use L/min. Found reviewing PL-LBQY.

Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Deferred from this gate", rows 6299-6413
  - 6299: Heading "Deferred from this gate".
  - 6301-6315: Rule for open debt items captured after the cut.
    - The cut is `e6cdfd93`, 07:04 on 2026-09-21.
    - An item captured after the cut is deferred to the next gate.
    - Exceptions: the item is classed `safety` or `science`, or an entry on the list waits on it.
    - Reason: the item was not in the store that the snapshot was taken from, so § "The gate is a snapshot, not a moving target" applies the presence test, and the test returns no.
    - A capture that an entry waits on is not deferred for its date. Either it is let in beside the entry, or the entry is deferred with it. These are two of that section's three places for a prerequisite (`PL-D388`).
    - A deferred item records `deferred-from: v0.6.0 - <why>`, written by the triage pass that classes it.
    - `bin/docket check` fails an open debt item that this section neither places nor defers.
    - `bin/docket wave` lists deferrals with each one's state and the release that took it (`PL-WD5Z`).
    - A frozen entry that this gate defers carries the same field, and `wave` counts it on the gate's own line instead (`PL-8QMD`).

Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Required scope", rows 6415-6780
  - **6415 heading:** "Required scope".
  - **6417-6427, entry 1, paragraph 1 (the layout model):**
    - queue items `PL-1FT6`, `PL-HJPY`, `PL-G60V`, `PL-5HSG`;
    - pure Python, imports no Qt;
    - a tree of splits and Areas, with the operations `split`, `join`, `resize`, `swap` and `set_view`;
    - a `borders()` query returns the handles collinear with and adjacent to a given handle;
    - serializes to versioned JSON, and from version 1 the serialized root holds a set of windows;
    - `PL-HJPY` settles what owns the set, how an Area is addressed across windows, and where the border-chain query stops;
    - settled now because adding the window set later would be a schema migration, run against files in which learners have already saved their own Workspaces.
  - **6429-6434, entry 1, paragraph 2 (refuse a join the tree cannot express):**
    - such a join is refused, never approximated;
    - `PL-3J2P` chose the nested-splitter tree knowing that it is strictly less expressive than Blender's shared-vertex graph;
    - it accepted the limit because the limit "shows up as a join being unavailable rather than as a wrong value";
    - the model must be built to have this property; it does not come free.
  - **6436-6445, entry 1, paragraph 3 (offers depend on the visible rectangles, not on how the tree was built):**
    - `PL-G60V` and `PL-5HSG` are corrections that the model's close-out review found (`#1448`);
    - decided (project owner, 2026-10-12, ratified, over keeping the join and the proportional drag that `#1448` shipped);
    - first, where four Areas meet, whether a join is available no longer depends on which split came first;
    - second, a drag moves only the borders on its line, as in Blender;
    - `PL-5HSG` lands before the adapter (`PL-W9P6`) makes the handles live;
    - `PL-G60V` lands before the reader-facing join (`PL-2KXB`);
    - so a reader never meets either behaviour in its old form (`PL-F6H6`).
  - **6447-6451, entry 2, paragraph 1 (the adapter):**
    - queue item `PL-W9P6`;
    - one module, the only one that imports `QSplitter`;
    - builds and updates the widget tree from the model;
    - v0.4.26's inert handles go live here;
    - the Qt side holds no layout state: no view calls `saveState()`, reaches up to a parent splitter or stores its own geometry.
  - **6453-6463, entry 2, paragraph 2 (Qt's saved state, measured):**
    - `QSplitter.saveState()` returns 35 bytes for a three-pane splitter;
    - those bytes hold magic number `0xff`, format version 1, the sizes, `childrenCollapsible`, `handleWidth`, `opaqueResize` and orientation;
    - no widget identity, so the same three children in reverse order save byte-identical state;
    - `restoreState()` returns false only on a bad magic number or a newer format version;
    - a three-pane state restores into a two-pane splitter and returns `True`;
    - a two-pane state restores into a three-pane splitter, returns `True`, and leaves the third pane at zero;
    - re-measured on PySide6 6.11.2 / Qt 6.11.2 on 2026-09-16;
    - this is the evidence behind `PL-C842`, "stronger than the form that item recorded".
  - **6465-6469, entry 3 (the View registry and kind tag):**
    - queue item `PL-R1WQ`;
    - decides where the registry lives;
    - decides how a tag is allocated, so that renaming a class does not trigger the loud failure meant for a missing View;
    - decides how the pure-Python model validates a tag without importing a Qt view.
  - **6471-6479, entry 4 (loud failure per Area on an unknown View kind):**
    - queue items `PL-R1WQ`, `PL-SSQW`;
    - the flagship divergence from Blender;
    - a rule per pane, which the file-level version policy "in entry 10" cannot cover;
    - a saved Workspace that names a View this build lacks fails visibly in that Area, and substitutes or drops nothing;
    - Blender silently installs a 3D viewport;
    - `docs/interface-provenance.md` § "Diverged" refuses that behaviour, preferring an obvious failure to a plausible-looking wrong one;
    - entry 20 (`PL-DB5G`) decides how the failure looks.
  - **6481-6489, entry 5 (the View contract):**
    - queue item `PL-TH35`;
    - written with the area system and validated against two existing Views;
    - carries four clauses added by the 2026-09-16 audit:
      - what a View's saved state is and who writes it;
      - what a View owes when handed state it cannot read;
      - what a split, a swap and a stack round trip each preserve;
      - what the container does when an Area cannot honour a size;
    - small by construction;
    - re-checked against the Views that implement it, as the provenance record's § "Prune the contract" requires.
  - **6491-6497, entry 6 (the import boundary):**
    - queue item `PL-L8RN`;
    - `QSplitter` is confined to the adapter, and concrete View modules stay out of the layout tree;
    - this needs `tools/import_boundary_check.py` to decide a dotted import target rather than a root package, which it has done since `PL-YXFF` declared the first such boundary;
    - both entries land in the commit that creates the adapter, because a boundary over a tree that does not exist yet is an `empty` error.
  - **6499-6519, entry 7 (the unconditional region):**
    - queue items `PL-NWTM`, `PL-W54S`, `PL-NNML`;
    - a named region outside the layout container that holds every unconditional value;
    - the model has no address for it, so the adapter cannot place an Area there and no saved Workspace can name it;
    - `PL-NWTM` supplies a pure-Python declaration, with no UI code, of that set of values;
    - the declaration is addressable by tier: one tier is invariant, and the other is made once per modelled substance, which keeps a later intravenous run from being asked for a vaporizer dial;
    - `PL-W54S` is the per-window shape, settled 2026-09-16 and recorded in `docs/MODEL.md`;
    - every top-level window carries the invariant tier and the name of its run;
    - the per-substance tier lives once, in a main window that cannot be closed while any other window is open;
    - v0.6.0 opens one window;
    - with this shape, break-out ("the unscheduled second half of item 34") will not need to rebuild the region;
    - entry 20 (`PL-J8K2`) decides where the region sits and how it looks, within `PL-84QX`'s budget;
    - `PL-NNML` joined on 2026-10-20, when the check of the Definition of done found that the page still scrolled the region and the use disclaimer out of view;
    - the region now sits above the part of the page that scrolls, and the disclaimer sits below it.
  - **6521-6528, entry 8 (an Area that cannot honour a size says so):**
    - queue item `PL-K285`;
    - the second of three properties that `docs/interface-provenance.md` records Blender as not supplying;
    - Blender's behaviour is `RGN_FLAG_TOO_SMALL` and a collapse to zero extent;
    - the divergence: "a required value is never hidden to make room";
    - decides whether a declared minimum is a request or a constraint;
    - decides what a split or a border drag does when the minimum cannot be met;
    - entry 20 (`PL-DB5G`) decides how the refusal looks.
  - **6530-6538, entry 9, paragraph 1 (state that a layout could duplicate or move):**
    - queue items `PL-VN6M`, `PL-9LNF`, `PL-CNCF`, `PL-PGZF`;
    - four pieces of state:
      - the compartment selection, owned outside `TraceLegend`;
      - `RunView`'s nine `build_*` accessors, which hand out the instance's own widgets, so a second request silently reparents a widget;
      - the chart time base, owned by a dropdown in one plot's panel yet governing both plots;
      - the chart's column budget, read as a maximum over two named siblings that stop being siblings once each is a View in its own Area.
  - **6540-6552, entry 9, paragraph 2 (`PL-CNCF` and `PL-PGZF` are named here and not re-labelled):**
    - decided (project owner, 2026-09-21, ratified, over a `cleared-by:` item field whose design was falsified before it was built, and over overwriting their `feature: chart-readout`);
    - both items measure a per-frame cost against the column budget that this entry moves;
    - `simulation_view.py` computes `plot_width_px=max(self._concentration_chart.plot_width_px(), self._wash_in_chart.plot_width_px())`;
    - measuring first and moving the budget afterwards would measure the wrong number, so this milestone clears both items;
    - no new scope;
    - `PL-YVP7` records why the field route was refused, and `PL-C4W8`'s sweep reached the verdict.
  - **6554-6558, entry 10 (run identity):**
    - queue item `PL-7Z84`;
    - a run's identity is stable across serialization and independent of drawing order;
    - `run_label(run_index)` becomes a rendering of that identity;
    - `docs/MODEL.md`'s rule for when a view names its run is restated in terms of identity, not of how many runs are shown.
  - **6560-6567, entry 11, paragraph 1 (the Workspace object):**
    - queue item `PL-WV9K`;
    - decides what a Workspace carries beyond its layout: the pinned run (by run identity), each View's saved state, and what a Workspace switch does to a run in progress;
    - decides how the set of Workspaces behaves: order, rename, duplicate, delete, save-as-mine;
    - Blender refuses to delete the last Workspace; here the type enforces that, not a warning dialog;
    - shipped defaults are told apart from the learner's own;
    - "reset" is defined.
  - **6569-6575, entry 11, paragraph 2 (a View's state survives leaving its Area and coming back):**
    - the provenance record adopts Blender's per-Area stack of previously open Editors, so the outgoing View's state is kept;
    - this milestone ships `swap` and `set_view`, the operations that would destroy that state;
    - the minimum form is in scope: one kept entry per View kind, which is how Blender bounds the stack without a cap.
  - **6577-6594, entry 11, paragraph 3 (a Workspace holds no preferences; a setting's tier follows how many instances can exist, not Workspace membership):**
    - run state is the simulated values: one set that every View draws from, and that no reader setting changes;
    - per-View-instance state is the compartments shown, axis denomination and range, and time window;
    - a View saves that state into its Workspace through the delegation that `docs/interface-provenance.md` § "Persistence, and what happens when an editor is missing" adopts from Blender's `SpaceType`;
    - two instances of one kind in one Workspace hold that state independently;
    - the owner's case (2026-09-17): one graph shows the vessel-rich group against the MAC-awake band and the 1 MAC line, a smaller one shows every compartment, and a third, in a squarer Area, is zoomed to the first 15 minutes for the wash-in, all in one Workspace;
    - reader preferences are settings for which a second simultaneous value is incoherent; the agent price and its currency is the only one today.
  - **6596-6608, entry 11, paragraph 4 (the test):**
    - can a reader sensibly have two on screen at once? If yes, the setting goes to the View; if incoherent, to preferences;
    - a setting that changes the numbers stays in the versioned data files;
    - a Workspace holds no preferences because it contains View instances and a preference has one value, not because a unit or price "is not layout", the weaker argument that sends the axis range to the wrong tier;
    - this lets Blender's four Save & Load entries be added later without a migration;
    - Blender's preference reset reads `use_data = false, use_userdef = true` and resets the third tier alone;
    - "reset this Workspace" restores the second tier from "item 13's" shipped JSON.
  - **6610-6613, entry 11, paragraph 5 (these tiers hold only while a run is not saved):**
    - this is a condition, not a permanent property;
    - planned-milestone items 9, 10, 12, 26 and 30 add a saved scenario, which is a fourth tier;
    - where it sits is decided when the first of those items is scoped.
  - **6615-6626, entry 12 (persistence):**
    - queue item `PL-SSQW`;
    - decides the file location on each platform, and the override for a headless run or a test;
    - an atomic write is required, so that an interrupted save leaves no truncated JSON;
    - decides what a first run with no file does;
    - sets the schema-version policy for older, newer and invalid-at-the-supported-version files, each named: migrated, refused, or set aside and replaced, and what the learner is told;
    - `core/parameters.py`'s single-version rule is right for files shipped with the code and wrong for files the learner wrote, so the policy is decided here.
  - **6628-6639, entry 13 (the shipped default Workspaces):**
    - queue items `PL-KXTL`, `PL-L59K`;
    - three defaults: one with the graph in the centre and the other Views around it, an induction Workspace with the concentration graph zoomed in, and a big-picture maintenance Workspace;
    - shipped as validated, versioned JSON beside the other shipped parameter files;
    - they carry the provenance line that `docs/interface-provenance.md` asks of anything derived from Blender's named task Workspaces;
    - composed within entry 20's pixel budget (`PL-84QX`);
    - the induction zoom is the chart's own saved state, so `PL-L59K` adds it once the View contract (entry 5, `PL-TH35`) says what that state holds.
  - **6641-6650, entry 14 (what a reader can do):**
    - queue item `PL-2KXB`;
    - a border drag moves the collinear chain that `borders()` returns, and a corner drag splits and joins;
    - a reader can swap two Areas;
    - the Workspace tab strip carries entry 11's lifecycle;
    - the chain query recovers "the one behaviour" the tree gives up compared with Blender's graph, which `PL-3J2P` found differing exactly once across 32 shipped Blender Workspaces;
    - entry 20 decides how the strip, handle and corner affordance look (`PL-QW1Z`, `PL-RQT1`, `PL-30L5`), and `PL-MS34` decides how the Area header looks.

    - queue item `PL-M352`, through a menu and the keyboard;
