    - the rule: this milestone adds no interaction that a keyboard-only reader cannot perform;
    - cheap now, a retrofit later.
  - **6659-6667, entry 16 (the View chooser, and keeping the accounting reachable):**
    - queue item `PL-50PZ`;
    - `docs/MODEL.md` puts the agent accounting in a tier that a Workspace may omit but that "must stay reachable in every layout, and must not be removable from the application";
    - the accounting is cumulative delivered, cumulative exhausted, total stored, and the mass-balance residual with its absolute error;
    - it is the one required class whose guarantee cannot rest on being built into the layout, so the guarantee becomes an invariant of the registry and the chooser;
    - entry 20 (`PL-RBXL`) decides how the chooser looks.
  - **6669-6677, entry 17 (the visibility predicate):**
    - queue item `PL-904Y`;
    - `SimulationView.interface_strings` is the project's single definition of what is on screen, and it tests whether a string is in a widget's subtree;
    - that equals "can the reader see it" only while the dashboard is one tree with nothing closeable;
    - this milestone breaks that once an Area can be closed or joined;
    - one predicate covers every Area and top-level window the application owns, and tells present-in-the-tree from visible;
    - entry 18 makes its assertions through it.
  - **6679-6685, entry 18 (tests over the real interface):**
    - queue item `PL-G5SX`;
    - headless;
    - they drive split, join, swap, resize, Workspace switch, save and reload;
    - after each step they assert that every unconditional value is on screen, that the accounting tier is reachable, and that no required value left with the surface that drew it;
    - they also make the assertion that `PL-9LNF` says cannot be written today: resize one Area while paused, and the redrawn series is sampled for its new width.
  - **6687-6697, entry 19 (documents this milestone makes untrue):**
    - queue items `PL-ZBBP`, `PL-JSY5`, `PL-RTG9`;
    - `.claude/rules/ui-areas.md` prohibits what this milestone builds, and its `paths:` misses a layout package outside `app/`, so the rule is retired or re-scoped;
    - `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` describes the layout that exists;
    - `docs/MODEL.md`'s readout-row rung and its always-on-screen disclaimer line assume one window width and one permanent surface;
    - `README.md` gains the Blender attribution line, which `docs/interface-provenance.md` § "Attribution" held back until the shipped interface is the area/workspace model.
  - **6699-6714, entry 20 opener (the look and edge states of every new surface):**
    - queue items `PL-QW1Z`, `PL-MS34`, `PL-RQT1`, `PL-30L5`, `PL-RBXL`, `PL-DB5G`, `PL-J8K2`, `PL-84QX`, plus `PL-F2YS`, `PL-JG82`, `PL-QLZ5`, which joined 2026-10-18; the entry was `PL-M3X6`;
    - looks are decided here, not inherited from the toolkit and not deferred to the visual pass;
    - entries 1-19 are mechanism: entry 14 says what a border drag does and entry 16 what the chooser guarantees, and none says how anything looks;
    - without this entry the surfaces would look like toolkit defaults, and planned-milestone item 33 would redo them one release later;
    - membership test: whether the surface exists in the application today, which separates new from inherited, not furniture from content, so the region and the two failure states qualify;
    - seven surfaces, each with a queue item.
  - **6716-6719, tab strip (`PL-QW1Z`):** where it sits; how an active tab differs from an inactive one; how rename, duplicate and delete appear; how a shipped default is told apart from a learner's Workspace.
  - **6721-6725, Area header (`PL-MS34`):**
    - whether there is a header;
    - if there is: its height, its contents, and whether the View name and chooser sit in it;
    - if not: where the chooser goes;
    - item 33's argument "for the move" names the header, but "no entry specifies it".
  - **6727-6729, splitter handle (`PL-RQT1`):** v0.4.26 built it inert and this milestone makes it live; decides its width, look at rest and on hover, cursor and drag feedback.
  - **6731-6736, corner split/join affordance (`PL-30L5`):**
    - a visible widget as in Blender, or a hot zone;
    - whether a drag previews the split before it commits;
    - of all this milestone's interactions, its look most decides whether learners find it;
    - if it is invisible, most learners never discover that the layout is theirs.
  - **6738-6739, View chooser (`PL-RBXL`):** as a learner sees it; entry 16 gives only its guarantees.
  - **6741-6746, two failure states (`PL-DB5G`):**
    - a matter of presentation safety, not taste;
    - an Area naming a missing View (entry 4), and an Area refused a size (entry 8);
    - both depart from Blender on purpose, and a learner sees both;
    - a failure state that looks plausible is the hazard that `CLAUDE.md`'s clinical-output standard names.
  - **6748-6751, where the region sits (`PL-J8K2`):** entry 7 guarantees it is outside the layout container; "nothing decides" where it goes or how it looks; it is on screen in every Workspace.
  - **6753-6780, pixel budget (`PL-84QX`):**
    - the one question that none of the seven surfaces covers alone;
    - the strip, headers and live handles take space from the chart, which is the teaching payload;
    - `PL-Z4K6` asked whether seven readout columns are wanted on a 1366 px laptop with none of this furniture yet, and that number is needed before adding three new consumers of space;
    - the budget (project owner, 2026-10-11, ratified, over lowering the chart's minimum and over keeping the in-page title row, `PL-84QX`) has three parts:
      - on 1366 × 768, about 700 px of client area, the shipped default Workspace with one run shows the concentration chart at no less than its current 360 px minimum, with the parameter View visible;
      - furniture above the first Area is capped at two text rows (tab strip and one Area header) plus 8 px per handle;
      - the region gives back the in-page title row (`PL-J8K2`);
    - compare mode falls below the minimum on that screen;
    - on 2026-10-18 `PL-F2YS` paired the runs' readouts and measured the chart at about 200 px, 159 px short;
    - compare mode accepts a shorter chart on this screen;
    - each run's transport row and heading are folded into one line (project owner, 2026-10-18, ratified, over scrolling the page and over refusing the chart's Area, `PL-JG82`);
    - `PL-QLZ5` did the folding on 2026-10-18 and measured it with the offscreen font: with two runs the region drops from 366 px to 303 px, leaving the chart about 264 px, 96 px short;
    - entry 13's Workspaces are composed within the budget;
    - all eight decided 2026-10-11 (project owner, ratified, over the alternatives each item names);
    - the items record the decisions, and the build items they name build the look.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Definition of done", rows 6782-6812
  - 6782: heading "Definition of done".
  - 6784-6786: layout operations. A learner can split, join, resize, swap and close Areas. They can switch Workspaces by tab, and rearrange, duplicate, rename and delete them. They can save their own Workspaces and find them on the next launch.
  - 6787-6788: keyboard. Every one of those operations works from the keyboard as well as by drag.
  - 6789-6791: layout tests. Tests run the layout model with no `QApplication`. They include a fixture file one schema version ahead of the build and a file truncated mid-write.
  - 6792-6794: import boundary. `tools/import_boundary_check.py` fails the build if any module other than the adapter imports `QSplitter`, or if the layout tree imports a concrete View module.
  - 6795-6797: load failures. A Workspace that names an unknown View kind fails visibly in that Area, and nothing is substituted. A Workspace file of an unsupported version is handled by "the written policy", not by the load path's default.
  - 6798-6801: unconditional values. Every unconditional value is outside the layout container. For each value, a test asserts that no ancestor of its widget is that container, and that the value is still on screen after a split, a join, a close, a Workspace switch and a reload.
  - 6802-6804: accounting tier. From every shipped Workspace, a learner can bring the accounting tier on screen through the interface alone. A Workspace that names none of those Views still leaves them reachable.
  - 6805-6806: View contract. Two existing Views implement the View contract. A test builds two surfaces for one run and asserts that both draw.
  - 6807-6810: no regression a learner can see, against v0.5.0. Compare mode ships before this milestone, and this milestone then rearranges it. Everything a learner could do with two runs on 2026-09-16 still works. This is checked against v0.5.0's own definition of done.
  - 6811-6812: closing gate. `make check` is green, and the documents listed in Required scope entry 19 describe the interface as built.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Explicitly out of scope for v0.6.0", rows 6814-6877
  - 6814: heading "Explicitly out of scope for v0.6.0". The section lists what release v0.6.0 does not build.
  - 6816-6831: break-out into a top-level window is out of scope. It is the second half of planned-milestone item 34, wanted eventually but not soon, so it is on no row of the timeline (project owner, 2026-09-26). From 2026-09-16 until that decision, break-out was the next release, "the second screen", with `PL-Y04W` as its build item, which was dropped the same day. Nothing in this release creates a second window. Splitting break-out off costs nothing structural, because break-out would otherwise force a rewrite of two things that are both built here: the serialized root carries a window set from version 1 (Required scope entry 1), and the unconditional region is built in its per-window shape (entry 7). When built, break-out adds the window, the window's lifetime and the test that drives it. Both reservations stay although break-out is unscheduled, as the cost of keeping it possible. The reason: a root that gains a window set later needs a schema migration against every Workspace a learner has saved. Entry 7's tiering also keeps a later intravenous run from being asked for a vaporizer dial it does not have.
  - 6832-6838: the View catalogue (planned-milestone item 36) is out of scope. It stays after this milestone for the reason item 34 records. A View's contract is whatever the area system requires of its contents, so Views built first would be built against today's fixed layout and then rewritten. This milestone validates the contract against two Views that exist and builds no new one. Queue item `PL-L6QR` is the queue audit that re-runs once item 36 is scoped.
  - 6839-6845: floating Areas or panels are deferred, not refused. Taking a tiled Area out into a floating one is wanted later, and this milestone does not build it. Item 34 records that writing floating panels down as permanently refused was a session's overreach, not a decision anybody took, so the exclusion is stated with its condition. Floating arrives under `docs/MODEL.md`'s occlusion rule, which applies to it without being argued again.
  - 6846-6848: the visual pass (planned-milestone item 33) is out of scope. It covers palette, type scale, spacing rhythm, density and the visual composition of each surface. Arrangement belongs to this milestone, and appearance to item 33.
  - 6849-6854: Blender's snap-merge operator and extend-drag are out of scope, because the splitter-tree measurement found that the splitter tree cannot express them. The five-area pinwheel arrangement is out of scope for the same reason. `docs/interface-provenance.md` § "Refused" records the count, the construction, and the condition under which the choice of representation reverses.
  - 6855-6858: replacing an Area by dragging it into the middle of another: the operation is in scope as `set_view` through the Area's own chooser (Required scope entries 1 and 16). Only the corner-drag affordance is deferred, to whichever release takes up the rest of Blender's docking set.
  - 6859-6861: schema migration code is out of scope. The policy for an older file is in Required scope entry 12. No second schema version exists to migrate from, and writing the migrator before its target schema exists would mean writing it twice.
  - 6862-6864: remembering which screen a window was on is out of scope. Restoring a position against a changed set of monitors puts a window where the reader cannot see it, which is the occlusion failure arriving through persistence.
  - 6865-6870: nothing under `core/` is in scope. This milestone introduces filesystem state and display dimensions, two things that § "Development rules for scientific milestones" requires simulation code to stay independent of, so the boundary matters more than usual. `tools/import_boundary_check.py` enforces it, and Required scope entry 6 only adds to it.
  - 6871-6877: re-reading the Blender design rationale against the primary pages (queue item `PL-PV5Q`) is separate from this milestone. The document rests on search summaries because the session egress proxy refused `blender.org` when the document was written, on 2026-09-15. Every `blender.org` host responded to a probe on 2026-09-26 (`PL-M701`), so the pages can now be read. They were re-read on 2026-10-25, and the re-reading corrected three claims (`PL-PV5Q`).
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "v0.7.0 - the schematic", rows 6879-6920
  - 6879: heading "v0.7.0 - the schematic".
  - 6881–6894: what the milestone holds and how its choices were made.
    - The milestone is planned-milestone item 27 (the compartment schematic), with item 28 (agent cost) beside it.
    - `PL-LF0P` scoped it on 2026-10-25. Two choices went to the project owner.
    - Decision 1: the schematic's containers fill to tension, as in Gas Man. They are labelled with their amounts, and a bar shows each compartment's share of the agent (project owner, 2026-10-25, ratified, over containers drawn to scale and over Gas Man's Picture as it is).
    - Decision 2: item 28 is built in this milestone rather than left unscheduled (project owner, 2026-10-25, ratified, over leaving it out and deferring its four entries to Gate 4).
    - The schematic and the agent cost readout are each a View of their own, which any Area can hold. The project owner confirmed this in the same round.
    - The remaining choices were made in the scoping round and not put to the owner, because each had one clearly better option or concerned look, wording or placement.
    - Look, wording and placement are placeholders while structure and function are built.
  - 6896–6907: Gas Man's Picture shows tension, not where the agent is.
    - The Gas Man Workbook's Figure 2-1 (printed p. 11) draws seven compartments as containers of one width: circuit, alveoli, arterial blood, vessel-rich group, muscle, fat and venous blood.
    - Each container fills "to a height representing partial pressure or tension in that compartment" (printed p. 11).
    - The only amounts shown are two totals in litres, Uptake and Delivered. A setting turns these into money.
    - Appendix E (printed p. 188) gives the Picture's purpose as "viewing and manipulating the state of an experiment at a given point in its evolution". It also says "the compartment schematic helps you visualize the relationships between compartment tensions".
    - Conclusion: the Picture shows where the tension is, not where the agent is. Showing where the agent is is the lesson planned-milestone item 27 was written for, and item 27 now says so.
  - 6909–6920: containers drawn to scale do not work in this model.
    - A container whose width is its capacity holds an amount equal to its filled area.
    - Mapleson's electric analogue rests on the same relation, q = C·v: a condenser's charge equals its capacitance times its voltage (Mapleson WW, *J Appl Physiol* 1963;18(1):197-204, https://doi.org/10.1152/jappl.1963.18.1.197, read from the private reference corpus, equations 5 and 6).
    - This model's capacities span almost three orders of magnitude. For sevoflurane, fat holds 493 L of gas equivalent and venous blood 0.79 L, a ratio of about 620.
    - In a View 600 px wide drawn to scale, fat takes about 500 px. The circuit, alveoli, venous blood and vessel-rich group take 1 to 7 px each.
    - Isoflurane and desflurane give the same picture.
    - The figures come from `AgentUptakeSystem.for_agent` run on the shipped parameter files on 2026-10-25.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Goal", rows 6922-6969
  - 6922: heading "Goal".
  - 6924-6928: The interface shows tension and almost nothing else. The concentration chart and the readouts give each compartment's partial pressure, in % atm and as a MAC multiple. As a result, the slow compartments look empty while they hold most of the agent. The table's values come from a run of this model (the text says "measured", though they are simulated): sevoflurane held at a 1 MAC dial, the reference adult, the shipped defaults. No date is given.
  - 6930-6931: table header. Columns: At; Muscle: tension, share of stored agent; Fat: tension, share of stored agent.
  - 6932: at 60 min, muscle 0.50% atm and 49%; fat 0.03% atm and 20%.
  - 6933: at 180 min, muscle 1.16% atm and 54%; fat 0.11% atm and 30%.
  - 6935-6938: Fat's trace sits within a few pixels of zero on the chart for that whole time. `PL-H5DV` found the same pattern for all three agents, plus the hours fat takes to overtake muscle (no figure given; the text says "measured", for model results). As of 2026-10-25 there are four gaps.
  - 6940-6944: Gap 1: no surface says where the agent is. The only amounts on screen are the agent accounting View's delivered, exhausted and stored totals, in litres of vapour. That View is a check that the model conserves mass. The owner called it "not info the user really needs or cares to see" (2026-09-16, recorded in `PL-B396`).
  - 6945-6950: Gap 2: the amounts exist but are not passed on. Each compartment in `core/` stores `agent_amount_l`. `SimulationSnapshot` in `src/anesthesia_sim/app/controller.py` carries only the total, `stored_agent_l`. This was deliberate: § "Designed for forking" declined per-compartment amounts until a consumer needed them, and this milestone is that consumer.
  - 6951-6954: Gap 3: no View shows how the compartments connect. The chart puts six traces on one axis. Nothing shows the agent passing from the circuit to the alveoli, the blood and each tissue, driven by differences in tension. Showing that is the purpose of Gas Man's Picture.
  - 6955-6958: Gap 4: nothing says what the agent cost. The delivered total appears only in litres of vapour, inside the mass-conservation check (the same View as gap 1). Clinicians buy and fill agent as liquid. The low-flow lesson of planned-milestone item 28 is about money.
  - 6960-6969: End state. A learner places the compartment schematic in any Area. For each displayed run it shows the circuit, the alveoli, venous blood and the three tissue groups, in the order the agent reaches them. Each compartment fills to its tension on one shared scale, as in Gas Man, and is labelled with its amount of agent vapour. A bar shows each compartment's share of all the agent the run holds, so fat's share grows while its tension stays near zero. A second View, the agent cost View, shows the delivered agent in millilitres of liquid equivalent, and in money at a price "the reader" sets. Every number on it matches the rest of the interface for the same run at the same instant, and the View says that its values are modelled.

Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Deferred from this gate", rows 7173-7196
  - 7173: Heading "Deferred from this gate".
  - 7175-7182: The rule for open debt items captured after the cut, and how deferrals are recorded and checked.
    - Such an item is deferred to the next gate unless it is classed `safety` or `science`, or an entry on this list depends on it.
    - A capture that an entry depends on is either let onto the list beside that entry, or the entry is deferred with it (`PL-D388`).
    - Each deferred item records `deferred-from: v0.7.0 - <why>`, which this gives as v0.7.0. The triage pass that classes the item writes that field.
    - `bin/docket check` fails on any open debt item that this section neither places nor defers.
    - `bin/docket wave` lists the deferrals with the state of each (`PL-WD5Z`).
  - 7184-7189: `PL-QX2G` and `PL-YT3T` came onto the list after the cut. The head they came from and its other members are deferred.
    - Both are classed `safety` and dated 2026-10-25.
    - Both were split from the generator head `PL-58D7` when its design round chose to give each core input a declared numerical envelope.
    - `PL-QX2G` bounds the volumes and partition coefficients. `PL-YT3T` bounds a compartment's own step.
    - `PL-58D7` and its other two members, `PL-06LS` and `PL-XST1`, are not `safety` and are deferred.
  - 7191-7196: `PL-J5GC` came onto the list after the cut.
    - It is classed `safety` and dated 2026-10-26.
    - It is the generator head that `PL-R4Y2`'s defect review recorded on 2026-10-25, over `PL-6RJ0`, `PL-3B34` and `PL-HF4B`.
    - Its guard requires values shown together on the run screen to come from one state.
    - Each of its members is a `safety` entry already on this list.
    - It was first given `deferred-from:`, which a `safety` item cannot take.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Required scope", rows 7198-7319
  - Row 7198: heading "Required scope".
  - Rows 7200-7224, item 1, "The specification, before anything is drawn" (`PL-2881`). Point: before anything is drawn, `docs/MODEL.md` gains a subsection under § "Minimum displayed outputs" saying what the schematic asserts and what it does not. Facts:
    - a fill height is the compartment's modelled tension, on the same scale for every container of one run;
    - an amount is the compartment's modelled agent, in the unit § "Agent amount" defines, named as vapour so nobody reads it as liquid;
    - the subsection sets, once for the whole interface, how vapour and liquid-equivalent amounts name their units;
    - the schematic labels amounts in millilitres of vapour, and the agent cost View gives millilitres of liquid equivalent;
    - about 183 mL of sevoflurane vapour come from 1 mL of liquid (§ "Agent amount"), so neither may show a bare "mL";
    - the share bar shows each compartment's amount as a share of all the agent the run holds, circuit included;
    - the subsection derives the displayed precision of a per-compartment amount in § "Displayed precision", not from the run totals' entry in `AGENT_AMOUNT_DISPLAY_PRECISION`, which was derived for the three totals;
    - at that entry's 0.1 L resolution, venous blood and the alveoli read 0.0 L for a whole hour at 1 MAC;
    - it adds two rows to the hazard table in § "Reasonably foreseeable misuse, and the hazards the presentation carries": a height read as an amount, and a vapour amount read as liquid;
    - it records why no container is drawn for arterial blood: the model gives arterial blood no amount of its own, because F_a ≡ F_A (§ "Blood compartments"), so such a container would show agent the model does not hold.
  - Rows 7226-7238, item 2 (`PL-KFTM`). Point: `SimulationSnapshot` carries the six compartments' amounts beside their tensions. Facts:
    - each amount is read from that compartment's `agent_amount_l` in `core/`;
    - the amounts travel as one record per compartment, not six more flat floats, so v0.8.0 can key the same record by substance instead of adding fields;
    - `stored_agent_l` stays `core/`'s one sum of the amounts, and a test keeps the record's amounts equal to it to the last bit;
    - summing again in `app/` would be a second sum of the same fact, so the build kept the one sum (`PL-KFTM`, 2026-11-01);
    - no equation changes, because `core/` already computes every amount and the item only passes them on;
    - a test compares each amount at stated instants with an oracle independent of `core/`, as `.claude/rules/test-oracles.md` requires.
  - Rows 7240-7253, item 3 (`PL-RNYV`). Point: the schematic View draws the compartments as containers. Facts:
    - it is one registered View kind, built against the View contract (`src/anesthesia_sim/app/view_contract.py`) like every View;
    - any Area can hold it, and compare mode draws one schematic per displayed run;
    - the containers are the circuit, the alveoli, venous blood, the vessel-rich group, muscle and fat, in the order agent reaches them, joined as the circulation joins them;
    - each container fills to its tension and is labelled with its name, tension and amount;
    - it shows the run's current instant, as the readouts do, and redraws on every frame the readouts redraw, so it never shows a state the run has left;
    - it declares its smallest readable size;
    - its colours are the compartment trace colours in `app/theme.py`, so each compartment means the same on the chart and the schematic.
  - Rows 7255-7263, item 4 (`PL-S79P`). Point: one share bar carries the amount, because containers drawn to scale cannot in this model. Facts:
    - one bar, divided by compartment, with each segment's length equal to that compartment's share of all the agent the run holds;
    - each segment is named in text;
    - containers filled to tension show fat nearly empty, although at one hour fat holds a fifth of the stored agent;
    - position on a common scale is read more accurately than area (Cleveland WS, McGill R, *J Am Stat Assoc* 1984;79(387):531-554, https://doi.org/10.1080/01621459.1984.10478080).
  - Rows 7265-7270, item 5 (`PL-PR1W`). Point: one shipped Workspace places the schematic beside the concentration chart. Facts:
    - the schematic is already reachable from every Workspace through the View chooser;
    - a learner meets tension over time and where the agent is now side by side, without building the layout;
    - which Workspace places it is a placeholder decision.
  - Rows 7272-7277, item 6 (`PL-KZ99`). Point: the agent's parameter file stores the constants a liquid figure comes from. Facts:
    - each agent's molar mass;
    - each agent's liquid density, with the temperature at which it was measured and its source;
    - the vapour-to-liquid factor is derived from these values in one place;
    - the factor is checked against Biro's and Gas Man's published constants.
  - Rows 7279-7282, item 7 (`PL-0S0V`). Point: `docs/MODEL.md` § "Displayed precision" derives a precision for each quantity and each unit. Fact: because the reader chooses the unit, precision depends on both quantity and unit.
  - Rows 7284-7290, item 8 (`PL-B396`). Point: the agent cost View is a View of its own that any Area can hold. Facts:
    - by default it shows the delivered agent in millilitres of liquid equivalent, or litres of vapour if the reader chooses;
    - either way, the unit is named on the value;
    - the basis is the delivered agent, not the exhausted agent, as item 28 records (`PL-H4N8`);
    - the agent accounting View keeps litres of vapour, because it is a check that the model conserves mass.
  - Rows 7292-7297, item 9 (`PL-VJZK`). Point: `docs/MODEL.md` § "A cost at the reader's price" states what a stored price carries and what a cost readout shows. Facts:
    - a stored price carries, among other things, its currency and the date on which it applied;
    - beside the cost, a readout shows the modelled liquid volume the cost multiplies, the price used, and words saying the price is the reader's rather than an authoritative figure.
  - Rows 7299-7302, item 10 (`PL-93B3`). Point: the price and the cost View's unit are kept in a preferences file. Facts:
    - both belong to the reader, not to a layout;
    - they are kept in a versioned preferences file separate from the Workspaces, as `PL-MQHN` decided;
    - `app/preferences.py` is that store (`PL-93B3`, 2026-11-01).
  - Rows 7304-7308, item 11 (`PL-1P15`). Point: the agent cost View shows the delivered agent's cost at a price per agent that the reader sets. Facts:
    - the project ships no price, because a shipped price goes stale and differs by institution;
    - with no price set, the View says so instead of showing a cost.
  - Rows 7310-7319, item 12 (`PL-HGZS`, `PL-HRPK`, `PL-PS5H`, `PL-XBJ3`, `PL-XT53`, `PL-YVL0`). Point: the copy editor is fixed, then five `docs/MODEL.md` rewrites are made. Facts:
    - the project owner added this to v0.7.0 (project owner, 2026-11-04 20:12Z: "At the copy editor stuff to .7");
    - the copy editor is the review that every rewrite of `docs/MODEL.md` passes through;
    - it keeps text its reader does not need and lengthens some of it, so `PL-HGZS` records it as a live generator;
    - it is fixed and stress-tested first;
    - then the five rewrites it blocks are made, so that the sections a learner reads beside the schematic, the agent cost View and the other displays are written for a clinician.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Definition of done", rows 7321-7362
  - 7321: heading "Definition of done".
  - 7323-7324: where the schematic goes. A learner can put the compartment schematic in any Area from its View chooser, in every Workspace. One shipped Workspace places it.
  - 7325-7327: what the schematic shows. For each displayed run, it shows the circuit, the alveoli, venous blood and the three tissue groups, in the order agent reaches them. It has no container for arterial blood.
  - 7328-7332: fill, labels and the share bar. Each container fills to that compartment's modelled tension, on one scale for every container of the run. Each is labelled with its tension and its amount, and the amount is named as vapour. A bar shows each compartment's share of the agent the run holds. Each segment is named in text, and the shares add up to the whole.
  - 7333-7336: the numbers agree with the rest of the interface. Every number equals what the rest of the interface gives for the same run at the same instant. A test asserts that each tension equals the readout's, and that the amounts add up to the agent accounting View's stored total.
  - 7337-7339: the schematic's end-to-end test. It runs patient inputs through the model to the drawn labels. It compares each amount at stated instants with an oracle independent of `core/`.
  - 7340-7342: no out-of-date state. The schematic never shows a state the run has left. A test drives a reset, an agent change, a branch, a return to a mark, and compare mode on and off. After each one, it checks that every label matches the snapshot.
  - 7343-7345: modelled notice and precision. The schematic says on its face that its values are modelled. Its amounts are given at the precision that `docs/MODEL.md` § "Displayed precision" derives.
  - 7346-7347: colour. `tools/contrast_check.py` covers every colour the schematic draws. Each container is named in text, so no compartment is told apart by colour alone.
  - 7348-7349: nothing is lost from v0.6.0. Nothing a learner could do on v0.6.0 is lost, checked against v0.6.0's own definition of done.
  - 7350-7355: the agent cost View. A learner can place it in any Area. They can read the delivered agent in millilitres of liquid equivalent or in litres of vapour, with the unit named on the value. Once they set a price, the View shows the cost with the price's currency and date. An end-to-end test checks the liquid figure against an independently computed conversion, and the cost against that figure times the price.
  - 7356-7357: settings kept across a restart. The price and the unit survive a restart, in a preferences file separate from every Workspace.
  - 7358-7360: the copy editor. It cuts text its reader does not need and keeps good text as it is, as `PL-HGZS`'s stress test judges. The five `docs/MODEL.md` rewrites that waited on it have merged.
  - 7361-7362: release-wide checks. `make check` is green. `docs/MODEL.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and `README.md` describe the interface that exists.

Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Development rules for scientific milestones", rows 7413-7453
  - 7413: heading, "Development rules for scientific milestones".
  - 7415–7416: rule for the scientific core. Define the equations, units, assumptions and reference cases before changing the scientific core.
  - 7417–7418: rule. Simulation code stays independent of the UI toolkit, wall-clock time, filesystem state and display dimensions.
  - 7419–7420: rule. Model parameters are stored as validated data, with a schema version and provenance. Data files hold no executable equations.
  - 7421–7422: rule. Results are deterministic for an identical initial state, identical events and identical time steps.
  - 7423–7424: rule. Mass accounting and independent reference cases are release gates, not optional diagnostics.
  - 7425–7427: rule for scoping. Keep each milestone narrow. A new idea stays in "the sections below" until it is promoted into a scoped release. An idea that is a task rather than a release goes in `docs/items/`.
  - 7428–7439: rule. A milestone's Goal is frozen and dated at scoping.
    - It records the problem the release was taken on to solve, in the tense and state of the day it was scoped.
    - Closing items do not amend it.
    - What the milestone achieved is recorded in its Required scope and Definition of done, which is where a reader looking for the current state is sent.
    - Reason: rewriting a Goal into the past tense clause by clause leaves the section describing neither the problem nor the product. It also puts an edit to `ROADMAP.md` on the critical path of every closure.
    - History: `PL-DXQC` is why the rule is written down. Two of v0.4.0's three Goal bullets were fixed while the section still asserted them in the present tense. One had been stale for two releases. No convention existed to breach.
  - 7440–7453: rule. An exclusion goes under `Explicitly out of scope`, never inside a `Required scope` bullet.
    - Mechanism: these two subsections are the only structures `bin/docket next` reads to decide membership.
    - It reads `Required scope` in full, because a milestone names what it covers in any grammar, so "(queue item PL-DHV7)" in the middle of a bullet counts.
    - Cost: an id named inside a scope bullet in order to exclude it is treated as scope.
    - History: `PL-NBCS` is why the rule is written down. A stage-3 exclusion in v0.4.0 put `PL-B9PY` into v0.4.0's `scope_ids`. While v0.4.0 was the anchor, `docket next` told every session, as a fact and naming the milestone, that Gate-1 work was what v0.4.0 was waiting on.
    - Enforcement: `tools/doc_check.py` fails when an id is named under both headings of one milestone. It gives an advisory where a scope bullet contains exclusion language.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Keeping the toolchain current", rows 7455-7513
  - 7455: heading "Keeping the toolchain current".
  - 7457-7458: lead. When a dependency or the interpreter changes, a scheduled run notices and the owner decides. Nothing upgrades on a schedule, and nothing gates on it.
  - 7460-7467: why the run is needed. The project is multi-year, so every version constraint in `pyproject.toml` will eventually be wrong. `requires-python` admits two CPython minor versions, 3.14 and 3.15. CPython ships a new minor version each year. Flet is pre-1.0, and an upper bound excludes Flet 1.0. The dev tools have only lower bounds, so their behaviour changes underneath "the gate". The failure guarded against is not a stale pin. It is coming back after a gap to a tree that no longer builds, when the fixing upgrade is several versions wide and costs more than a hobby project will pay.
  - 7469-7473: a real case, recorded in `pyproject.toml`. pydantic 2.12.4 began passing `prefer_fwd_module=` to `typing._eval_type`. CPython added that keyword between 3.14.0rc2 and 3.14.0 final. Parameter loading in the scientific core stopped working. It took one dependency, one patch version and one interpreter build.
  - 7475-7477: "What notices". `.github/workflows/drift.yml` runs monthly and on demand. It asks two questions that `quality.yml` is "right not to ask", plus a third that runs weekly.
  - 7479-7482: `dependencies` check. It checks the newest release that each bound in `pyproject.toml` allows, resolved with `uv sync --upgrade`. A failure means a dependency broke the project inside a range the project already accepts.
  - 7483-7486: `interpreter` check. It checks the newest stable CPython, relaxing the upper bound on `requires-python` in the runner only. A failure means the next interpreter is not ready for the tree, or the tree is not ready for it.
  - 7487-7490: `order` check. It runs the whole suite in shuffled order every Monday, using pytest-randomly with the date as the seed. A failure means a test passes only when another runs before it. The output names the seed that reproduces the failure (`PL-75D9`).
  - 7492-7495: the workflow also prints what exists beyond the declared bounds, without failing. The reason it does not fail: crossing a major bound is a deliberate migration that CI should not attempt. This report is the only way anyone learns that a Flet 1.0 or a pydantic 3 is available.
  - 7497-7503: "What does not happen". There are no automated dependency pull requests and no version-bump campaign. The current pins are correct "today" (no date given). The point is to know when a pin stops being correct, not to raise numbers on a schedule. Automated bumps were considered and rejected, on evidence the project cites about narrow versus broad automation. Broad automation builds up noise until people route around it, the failure that `PL-ZBJ0` records.
  - 7505-7508: "Why it reports rather than gates". Nobody here controls the versions the workflow resolves. Gating would let an unrelated upstream release block the project's own work. A red run is news to act on when convenient, not a signal to stop.
  - 7510-7513: "retired if it stops being news". `CLAUDE.md`'s retirement test applies: a check that fires every run without changing a decision is a defect in the check. If the workflow's failures become routine background that nobody acts on, delete the workflow rather than work around it.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "The debt gate", rows 7515-7910
  - 7515: heading "The debt gate".
  - 7517-7520: the rule.
    - Recorded technical debt is cleared before a new milestone begins.
    - Phase 0 applied the rule once, to get out of a queue that had stopped shrinking.
    - This section makes the rule standing, so it runs before every milestone.
  - 7522-7526: why.
    - Debt is cheapest to clear while someone still remembers the code.
    - A defect carried across a milestone boundary has to be diagnosed again, against code that has moved.
    - A decision left unanswered stops being one that anyone can make.
    - Deferred debt is either paid twice or silently dropped.
  - 7528: heading "What counts".
  - 7530-7533: what debt is.
    - Debt is what the queue records, minus new work.
    - An open item is debt when it is classed `defect`, `safety`, `science`, `refactor` or `perf`.
    - It is also debt when its status is `needs-decision`, whatever it is about.
  - 7535-7537: `feature` and `planning` items are not debt.
    - They are the work the gate protects.
    - Counting them would mean "do everything before doing anything", and no gate could open.
  - 7539-7548: process work is debt once the mechanism is live.
    - Building a new tooling capability is new work and does not keep a gate shut.
    - A mechanism that `main` depends on and that is unreliable is debt, classed `defect`. The class carries the rule, so nothing extra is tracked.
    - What decides is the mechanism's state, not its layer. An unbuilt idea costs nothing, while a half-working mechanism costs something every session.
    - Half-finished process machinery must not be in production use: it is either made reliable or abandoned and removed.
  - 7550-7555: the same test decides a hazard, the one carve-out from the `safety` and `science` exception.
    - An `anticipated` finding describes a hazard a later milestone will create, so it is not debt until that milestone builds it.
    - Points to § "The gate is a snapshot, not a moving target" for the rule, what it was chosen over, and its cost.
  - 7557-7562: caution from the first pass.
    - Classing tooling breakage `infra` rather than `defect` makes it look like a capability nobody has built.
    - `docket release` leaving `uv.lock` stale and breaking `make check` at every release is a defect. Adding release tagging is not.
    - If the class does not separate them, the gate cannot.
  - 7564-7566: clearing an item.
    - Clearing means `done` or `dropped`, with the reason recorded.
    - Deciding that something is not worth doing is legitimate, and often right.
    - Leaving an item open and starting anyway is not legitimate.
  - 7568: heading "The gate is a snapshot, not a moving target".
  - 7570-7575: a freeze closes new scope, not the completeness of a fix (project owner, 2026-08-30).
    - A frozen list exists to fix a specific set of problems, and an entry may need a further finding before it is fixed.
    - Refusing that finding defeats the list's purpose.
    - The rule is deliberately asymmetric: it keeps new work out and lets in what an entry needs.
  - 7577-7582: an admitted finding is worked with the entry it completes (project owner, 2026-08-30).
    - Working it later lets the first entry ship half-fixed.
    - Both go in one branch with one review and close together, before anything else starts.
  - 7584-7595: three homes for what a frozen entry waits on (`PL-D388`, project owner, 2026-10-03, ratified, over admitting every open prerequisite of an entry automatically).
    - First: work named in the milestone's `Required scope` makes the entry the milestone's, per § "Debt inside the milestone's own scope".
    - Second: work that no release schedules before the milestone begins defers the entry on the terms of § "The cadence" step 3, recorded as `deferred-from:`.
    - Third: anything else is let in beside the entry, dated, and worked with it.
    - `bin/docket check` rejects an open frozen entry waiting on work that none of the three covers, when the release train does not schedule that work first (`PL-8QMD`).
  - 7597-7605: the list freezes when the gate freezes.
    - The moment is the later of the milestone's scoping and the ship of the milestone before it (§ "The cadence").
    - A finding re-enters this gate when its problem was present at that moment, whatever its id and however late someone noticed it.
    - A finding defers to the next gate only when its problem is new: introduced while clearing this gate, or while implementing the milestone it protects.
  - 7607-7617: why the test is presence, not discovery.
    - Work generates findings: clearing 13 items in one session generated 14 new ones, which is normal and is the capture rule doing its job.
    - If a gate reopened for everything noticed after the freeze, its queue would never empty and its milestone would never start.
    - The rule would then be abandoned, which is worse than not having it.
    - The test still closes one gap: a problem found again under a new id, after a stranded branch or a slow session, re-enters (`PL-64LS`).
  - 7619-7626: friction that compounds is the clearest case of a problem present at the freeze (project owner, 2026-08-30).
    - Such a finding costs something again on every remaining entry. Examples: a tool that answers the wrong question, a check that cries wolf, a command that ends red when it succeeds.
    - It was almost always present at the freeze, and only became visible once the gate's work started paying its cost.
    - Deferring it means paying that cost once per remaining entry, so it re-enters and is worked early.
  - 7628-7636: the test is arithmetic.
    - Multiply what each remaining entry pays by the number of entries left.
    - `PL-0RS6` qualified at 18 entries, each paying a twelve-command detour. That is a large fraction of the saving the release exists to deliver.
    - Work that is merely valuable, cleaner or more interesting fails the test and waits for the roadmap, for example rewriting a working tool in another language or adopting a nicer abstraction.
    - If nobody can name the saving per entry, the finding does not qualify.
  - 7638-7649: presence is a presumption, and sessions should favour it.
    - A finding that continues or completes a frozen item belongs to this gate and is recorded with that item. `PL-SWFM`, which continues `PL-Z4GF`, motivated this.
    - A session may still defer a finding that qualifies when it has no real link to anything frozen, or when pulling it in would recreate the refilling-queue problem that the debt gate replaced Phase 0 to solve.
    - A session may also decline a finding that only superficially resembles frozen scope.
    - Either way it must say so and why. Silently changing which gate a finding belongs to is the renegotiation the freeze prevents.
  - 7651-7654: two exceptions re-enter regardless of presence.
    - They are anything at `P0`, and anything classed `safety` or `science`.
    - The project's standard does not allow those to be deferred.
  - 7656-7672: an `anticipated` `safety` or `science` finding is not debt until its hazard exists.
    - Decision tag: project owner, 2026-09-16, ratified, chosen over placing each finding on the next milestone's frozen list behind a marker nothing enforces, and over leaving the rule as it was and putting the 2026-09-16 area-model findings on v0.5.0's list.
    - The paragraph above is about timing; this one is about whether a finding counts at all.
    - Example: a splitter handle can only resize an Area (`AreaLayout` in `src/anesthesia_sim/app/area_layout.py`, live since `PL-W9P6` on 2026-10-18). So no reader can close, replace or cover a required value until the docking operations of planned-milestone item 34 are built.
    - A gate cannot clear a hazard that its own milestone introduces.
    - This is the live-mechanism test, quoted from § "What counts" with "is about" dropped.
  - 7674-7682: why this option.
    - It is the only one of the three that ends in a state a session can reach.
    - The other two leave the corrected safety-class advisory in `tools/doc_check.py` lit against items nobody can close. That is the defect `CLAUDE.md` names: a check that "fires every run without changing a decision".
    - Such a finding is placed in the milestone that creates the hazard, never deferred. These two classes are still not offered a deferral.
    - v0.6.0's `Required scope` lists the ten findings this rule was written for.
  - 7684-7696: the carve-out expires with the wait it is granted for.
    - Decision tag: project owner, 2026-09-16, ratified, chosen over leaving it on the class alone, which never stopped.
    - `anticipated` claims the hazard is not live yet. An item stops saying `status: blocked` once the hazard is live, and `blocked-by` names what must happen first.
    - Only an item that has written down what it waits for keeps the exemption, which lapses when the wait ends.
    - The class alone kept the exemption running past the milestone that created the hazard, the one event the rule exists to notice (`PL-ZF2G`).
    - `subprojects/docket/src/docket/checks.py` already requires the same pair of fields for the safety-band exemption. This rule reuses that pair, so `anticipated` means one thing in both tools.
  - 7698-7704: the exemption lapses when the item is promoted, not when its blocker closes.
    - Nothing changes `status` by itself.
    - Since `PL-2349` (2026-10-12), `bin/docket check` rejects a `blocked` item whose item blockers have all closed. For an item blocker, the promotion therefore lands in the pull request that closes the blocker, with no gap.
    - A milestone blocker leaves one grooming pass, through the "is scoped and every other blocker has closed" advisory. That replaces an open-ended gap.
  - 7706-7708: `check_gate_reentries` in `tools/doc_check.py` reads both fields, so the rule runs on every `make check`.
  - 7710-7718: what it costs.
    - `anticipated` now carries weight, so a wrongly classed `safety` item becomes invisible to the gate, the same failure as `PL-MVC2`.
    - Two things narrow the risk without removing it. The class is claimed, not inferred. And `PL-MVC2` made a class outside the declared list a `bin/docket check` error, so a misspelling fails closed and the item stays named.
    - A correctly spelled but wrongly applied class is caught only by a reviewer's judgment.
  - 7720: heading "The cadence".
  - 7722-7724: the gate is a recurring step on the plan. Every milestone goes through four steps, which § "The plan" shows on one timeline.
  - 7726-7728: step 1, scope.
    - Give the milestone's goal, required scope, definition of done and explicit out-of-scope list.
    - Add the heading for its frozen list, empty until the freeze.
  - 7729-7731: step 2, freeze and record.
    - Record the debt list in the milestone's own section, as item ids, on the day it froze.
    - The freeze day is the later of the day the milestone is scoped and the day the milestone before it ships.
  - 7732-7755: step 3, clear.
    - Clear the list before implementation begins. Each item ends `done`, `dropped` with its reason, or deferred to a later gate.
    - Sweep the list for staleness first. Decision tag: project owner, 2026-09-17, ratified, chosen over a second `verify:`-style field recording a fault test beside each fix test, and over leaving the sweep to whoever next noticed.
    - A `verify:` command fails before the work and passes after it, so it tests that the fix is there, never that the fault is. An entry whose defect no longer reproduces therefore fails forever and looks outstanding.
    - One pass (no date given) found 12 of 134 open items dead and 31 more overtaken, 32% in all. Nothing noticed this, and nothing can.
    - An advisory based on how often items changed was built, tested against that pass's verdicts, and rejected, because `partly` changes less than `yes` (`PL-LKGL`).
    - Staleness is a judgment, so it is a step, not a check. Read each entry against the tree, drop what no longer reproduces with its reason, and correct briefs that overstate what is left, all before spending a session on any of them.
    - File the pass as its own item under `feature: gate-staleness-sweep`, and do not name a standing one here.
    - An item named here can close: `PL-6ZQY` closed on 2026-09-19, two days before Gate 2 froze on a list it never saw (`PL-D8KW`). An instruction to file an item cannot go stale that way.
  - 7757-7761: the third outcome.
    - Decision tag: project owner, 2026-09-21, ratified, over leaving each milestone's own bullet to be read at its own cut.
    - An entry may be deferred instead of cleared, and the release ships without it, but only when all three conditions below hold.
  - 7763-7769: condition 1.
    - The snapshot section permits the deferral, and the deferral says so and why.
    - The item carries `deferred-from: vX.Y.Z - <why>`, naming this gate. That field is the one record every reader of the gate reads (`PL-8QMD`, project owner, 2026-10-11, ratified, over the deferral group headings this section used to carry).
    - The gate section may set out the decision at any length.
  - 7770-7776: condition 2.
    - The deferral lands in the next gate. That gate's freeze carries every open entry this list deferred, debt or not, from what `bin/docket gate` prints.
    - An entry deferred to nowhere holds a release open (`PL-S5Q9`).
    - The field names the gate the entry leaves, not the one it reaches (`PL-WD5Z`). That is enough, because the next freeze always takes the entry.
  - 7777-7778: condition 3: the entry stays on the frozen list, because removing it is the renegotiation the freeze prevents.
  - 7780-7783: a `safety` or `science` entry may be deferred only when the deferral records that its hazard is not live. That must be checked against the tree, and is recorded in the field's reason or in the gate section.
  - 7785-7798: why the terms are stated here and not in each definition of done.
    - The case recurs: Gate 1's last entry, `PL-WZVZ`, was deferred to Gate 2 on these terms.
    - v0.5.0's bullet allows only `done` or `dropped`. A session at that cut would have had to drop a `safety` entry whose hazard is real but not yet live, or hold the release.
    - `PL-F0L8` records `dropped` being chosen once, because the bullet was treated as exhaustive.
    - Gate 2 "freezes" the day v0.5.0 ships and inherits the entry, so a deferral is the normal case.
    - Completed milestones' bullets are left as written, because they record conditions those releases met (`PL-Y949`).
  - 7799-7802: step 4, implement.
    - A finding made while clearing or implementing goes to the next gate.
    - It re-enters this gate instead when its problem predates the freeze (§ "The gate is a snapshot") or when it is `P0`, `safety` or `science`.
  - 7804-7806: a scoped milestone carries its gate heading from the day it is scoped, empty until the freeze. A milestone without one is not scoped.
  - 7808-7823: why the later of the two days.
    - Decision tag: project owner, 2026-10-03, ratified, over keeping scoping as the trigger with exceptions recorded against it, `PL-KKRP`.
    - A gate holds the previous milestone's findings, so a list frozen before that milestone ships misses them. A list frozen before its own milestone is scoped has no section to be recorded in.
    - The timeline fixes the freeze date.
    - Gate 1 froze on 2026-09-06, the day v0.5.0 was scoped; v0.4.0 had shipped the day before.
    - Gate 2 froze on 2026-09-21, the day v0.5.0 shipped. v0.6.0 had been scoped early, on 2026-09-16, and that freeze was then recorded as an exception (project owner, 2026-09-16, ratified while scoping v0.6.0).
    - The timeline rows for Gates 3 and 4 were already written on the ship day.
    - v0.4.26 was counted as a second exception in error: it was a `v0.4.x` patch, and a patch freezes no gate.
  - 7825-7830: which step is due is computed, not remembered.
    - `bin/docket wave` reads the version, the timeline, the milestone sections and their frozen lists, and reports the current step and the step that follows it.
    - It does not report whether a gate should open early, or whether what is written beside a step is still true.
  - 7832-7839: a gate gets no version of its own.
    - Its work ships as it clears, in patch releases on the previous milestone's track: `v0.4.x` while Gate 1 drained, `v0.5.x` while Gate 2 does.
    - A session cuts a patch when it judges the finished work worth releasing.
    - The milestone's own number is reserved for its capability boundary (§ "Versioning decision"), and its release notes carry its own scope.
  - 7841-7852: this records the practice, not a change to it (project owner, 2026-09-27, `PL-LPH9`).
    - The sentence this replaced forbade interim releases while clearing, but every gate was worked the other way.
    - Gate 0 closed across the v0.2.6 to v0.3.0 patch series.
    - At v0.4.32, on 2026-09-20, `bin/docket wave` treated 172 of Gate 1's 175 entries as clear.
    - v0.5.1 to v0.5.14 shipped 67 of Gate 2's 190.
    - Since 2026-09-27, a release is cut when a feature finishes: a session cuts it and the project owner pushes the tag. This is dated to keep the current instance apart from the rule.
  - 7854-7858: Gate 0 is the single exception.
    - It was released as the minor v0.3.0 (§ "Versioning decision"), because it held the backlog from before this mechanism existed.
    - Later gates hold one milestone's findings. They are ordinary maintenance, ship in patches and get no number.
  - 7860: heading "Debt inside the milestone's own scope".
  - 7862-7866: debt that the milestone exists to clear is cleared by it.
    - v0.4.0 was scoped partly because six `safety`, `science`, `defect` and `perf` items were symptoms of one thing.
    - Requiring them to be cleared first would leave no order that works.
  - 7868-7872: the test is whether the item is in the milestone's "Required scope".
    - If it is, it is milestone work, listed under a heading that says so, and the gate opens when everything outside the scope is clear.
    - If it is not, it is cleared first.
  - 7874-7877: this does not weaken the `safety` and `science` rule. Such an item inside the scope still cannot be deferred, and cannot be finished before the work it belongs to. The milestone's definition of done enforces this.
  - 7879: heading "Recording it".
  - 7881-7883: record the frozen list in the milestone's own section, as item ids. An unwritten gate gets renegotiated.
  - 7885-7905: record a deferral on the item.
    - A debt item that the current gate neither places nor takes carries `deferred-from: vX.Y.Z - <why>`.
    - The version is the milestone whose list excuses the item, never the gate it goes to, because the next freeze takes every open debt item. The reason states the grounds.
    - `bin/docket set <id> --deferred-from "..."` writes the field. `bin/docket check` fails an open debt item that the gate neither places nor defers, and prints that command (`PL-WD5Z`).
    - A frozen entry deferred on the terms of step 3 carries the same field (`PL-8QMD`) and stays on its list. The next freeze takes it whatever its classes, because `bin/docket gate` prints every open entry of the last frozen list beside the store's open debt.
    - `bin/docket check` fails an open frozen entry waiting on work outside the plan, unless the field defers it. Work is outside the plan when neither the list nor `Required scope` includes it and the release train does not schedule it first.
    - A section may state its deferrals' shared grounds, as v0.6.0's does, but no tool reads that text.
    - Until `PL-WD5Z`, deferrals were ids in `###` subsections headed `Declined`, `Deferred` or `Sequenced`, and each disagreement between those and the items became an item of its own. Completed sections keep those subsections as the record of that period.
  - 7907-7910: do not write a deferred item's state or release beside it. `bin/docket wave` prints them from the store (`PL-B60Q`), so a hand-written mark would only copy a field.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Development pathway", rows 7912-8076
  - 7912: heading "Development pathway".
  - 7914-7925: what the section gives and how it relates to the timeline.
    - The numbered list below is the catalogue.
    - The section gives the intended order, with reasons, for catalogue work that § "The timeline" has not placed.
    - A release that the timeline names takes the timeline's order. Where the two disagree, the timeline's order governs (§ "The planning model: a rolling wave").
    - Each phase keeps its name and intent, because other documents cite phases by name.
    - A phase's order covers only unplaced work.
    - Where the timeline has taken a phase's first half, the phase says so without naming the release. How that half was taken is dated as history, not restated (`PL-5XG1`).
    - Phases are groupings of intent, not scoped milestones.
    - Each item is still specified individually before implementation, as the development rules above require.
  - 7927-7932: the organizing goal and the phases' original order.
    - The goal is a mature inhalational simulator before any IV work.
    - The phases were written in this order: the science, then an interface that can drive it, then reproducibility, then IV.
    - Since 2026-08-25, the timeline has taken interface and comparison work ahead of the science.
    - For unplaced work, that order still applies.
  - 7934: Phase 0 lead-in: "foundation"; superseded by the standing debt gate, 2026-08-25.
  - 7936-7938: Phase 0's status.
    - It is not closed, and on its own terms it never can be.
    - Its closing conditions are reproduced unchanged below, with their state on 2026-08-25.
  - 7940-7948: Phase 0's original rule and motive.
    - The rule: "no new feature work until the existing queue is closed out".
    - The motive was not that features are unwelcome: the last stretch of work felt like whack-a-mole.
    - The queue holds almost no defects; it holds unmade decisions.
    - When Phase 0 was written, 9 of 24 open items were at `needs-decision`.
    - Over a third of the queue could not be picked up, so the queue never visibly shrank.
    - "All bugs squashed" is not a closing condition, because the absence of defects cannot be demonstrated.
  - 7950-7951: table header: Phase 0 closing condition | State on 2026-08-25.
  - 7952: no open item classed `safety`, `science` or `defect`. Not met: 6 safety/science and 8 defect-classed items were open.
  - 7953: no open item classed only as process work (`session-cost`, `docs`, `infra`). Not met: 11 such items were open.
  - 7954: no item at `needs-decision`. Met, and deliberately unmet again the same day.
    - PL-Y2GG (the license choice) was moved to `needs-decision`, because only the project owner can make that decision.
    - The row judges that recording this is more honest than a `ready` that nobody can act on.
  - 7955: every outstanding branch merged and a release cut. Met for merges (`claude/pl-64ls-promote` is gone from the remote), and v0.2.4 is cut.
  - 7957-7963: why Phase 0 was superseded rather than pursued.
    - The first two conditions require zero items in categories that every session refills.
    - The queue held 24 open items when Phase 0 was written, and holds 48 "now" (no date given). It doubled while Phase 0 was meant to drain it.
    - This was not a failure of effort; it was the capture rule working as designed.
    - A gate that requires a refilling queue to empty never opens. It can only be quietly abandoned or block all work forever.
  - 7965-7971: the debt gate as Phase 0's replacement.
    - § "The debt gate" (above) replaced Phase 0, and is strictly better for the same purpose.
    - It freezes a list at a moment, so the list is finite and the gate can always open.
    - It recurs before every milestone, not once.
    - Phase 0 aimed at two things: debt cleared while the code is fresh, and decisions answered rather than accumulated. The gate gets both on a cadence, not in one push.
  - 7973-7976: what retirement means.
    - Phase 0 is retired as a phase.
    - Its unmet conditions are not carried forward as a backlog; their items are in the current frozen gate.
    - Phase 0 no longer blocks implementing a planned milestone; only that milestone's own gate does.
  - 7978-7986: Phase 1 ("scientific maturity") and its order.
    - First, generalize the patient's state from one agent to N substances present at the same time.
    - Then items 6 and 7 (nitrous oxide; concentration and second-gas effects), then item 1 (machine abstraction with interlocks), then item 2 (agent switching with residual washout).
    - § "The timeline" has placed the generalization with items 6 and 7, so this section sets the order of items 1 and 2 only.
    - Item 40, the machine-profile framework, is not in this phase. It was split out of item 1 on 2026-09-20 (project owner), because nothing in a config file needs the substance generalization.
  - 7988-7993: why the generalization comes first.
    - Three items need more than one substance at once, with kinetics for each.
    - Agent switching needs the old agent's residual while the new one washes in.
    - Nitrous oxide needs a second gas throughout.
    - The second-gas effect needs the coupling between the gases.
    - Doing the generalization once makes one design decision instead of three special cases.
  - 7995-8001: the generalization also prepares for IV.
    - It settles the IV question without a speculative abstraction.
    - "A substance with compartmental kinetics and an effect site" is the shape an IV agent needs, so IV becomes an extension, not a rewrite.
    - This works only if the design is built on substances, not on "volatile agent plus nitrous oxide as a special case". Designing it on substances is a requirement of this phase.
  - 8003-8010: Phase 1 as first written (history).
    - Nitrous oxide sat before any interface work, for two reasons.
    - Reason 1: the second-gas effect is the phenomenon this class of simulator is most used to teach; without it there is no credible inhalational simulator to build an interface on.
    - Reason 2: nitrous oxide changes the core equations, so an interface built on a single-gas core would be reworked.
    - The reordering below set this placement aside. The known risk at the end of the section is the general form of reason 2.
  - 8012-8030: the reordering (project owner, 2026-08-25): "the teachable case" is taken ahead of Phase 1.
    - "As written below", the generalization and nitrous oxide come before all interface work, on the reasoning that an interface built on a single-gas core would be reworked.
    - That order was reconsidered once the interface was judged against what a learner can do. The run advances at 1x real time and the chart spans 5 minutes (present tense, no date). So neither the muscle curve nor the fat curve can be observed, and no lesson in uptake and distribution gets through.
    - Building coupled-gas equations first would give a more capable engine that teaches nobody, and without feedback from real teaching use to shape the multi-gas display.
    - The rework accepted is bounded and known. The view consumes an immutable `SimulationSnapshot`, so the time base, playback, event marks and axis handling do not depend on the substance. The MAC readout changes, becoming a MAC sum when a second substance lands.
    - v0.4.0 is that work, and Phase 1 follows it unchanged.
    - That was the order on 2026-08-25 and is no longer: since then the timeline has placed further releases ahead of the generalization, each moved by a decision dated on its own row.
  - 8032-8035: Phase 2 ("an interface that can drive the machine"), the work that remains.
    - First item 24 (the preferences panel).
    - Then items 5, 3 and 4 (end-tidal control, override mode, direct injection).
    - Then item 8's scenario events and item 20 (accessibility).
  - 8037-8045: Phase 2's shipped history.
    - The rest of the phase has shipped.
    - The phase opened by consolidating the display constants that item 24 names as its prerequisite. That work came first because item 24 named it, and because every control added before it spreads the scattered defaults further. `PL-2CS8` landed it in v0.4.11.
    - Item 25 (playback speed) shipped in v0.4.0, and item 26 (run bookmarks) in v0.5.0.
    - Item 8's control-input timeline shipped in halves: recording in v0.4.0, the replay driver in v0.5.0.
    - Phase 3 cannot start without the control-input timeline, because save/load, replay and forking each restore from it.
  - 8047-8051: Phase 3 ("reproducibility and comparison").
    - Items 9 then 10 (save/load, then deterministic replay), built on item 8's control-input timeline.
    - Items 11 and 12 (side-by-side comparison, forking) shipped in v0.5.0, ahead of both.
    - That departed from the first sequence 9, 10, 11, 12; § "The timeline" records why.
  - 8053-8057: why Phase 3 precedes IV.
    - It is groundwork: save/load and forking force the whole simulation state to be explicit and copyable.
    - Working features enforce that, not good intentions.
    - This is a more reliable foundation for a drug subsystem than an abstraction designed before one exists.
  - 8059-8060: Phase 4 ("intravenous agents"): items 13, 14 and 15 (IV pharmacokinetics and effect site; hypnosis/eBIS; nociceptive response).
  - 8062-8066: work alongside the phases.
    - Items 21 and 22 (performance, documentation) are continuous, not phased.
    - Items 16 to 19 (renal/hepatic dysfunction, cardiopulmonary bypass, ECMO, species profiles) and item 23 (packaging and distribution) are deliberately later; each extends a mature model.
  - 8068-8076: the known risk.
    - As written with the phases, Phase 1 front-loads the hardest work in the plan.
    - Coupled-gas equations with reference cases are real scientific work, and the likeliest place for the project to stall. The risk is accepted deliberately.
    - Every milestone built before the generalization is code written for a single agent, and would have to be revisited.
    - Since 2026-08-25, the timeline builds milestones before the generalization.
    - Whether the argument still applies to those milestones is the project owner's open question; `PL-5XG1` records it "with its count".
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Planned milestones (run 1 of 3)", rows 8078-8672
  - 8078: heading "Planned milestones".
  - 8080-8087: what the section is. It is "the catalogue of intent". § "The timeline" orders the releases it names. § "Development pathway" (above) gives the order intended after them. Each item stays unspecified (no goal, required scope or definition of done) until promoted to a scoped milestone, as the development rules above require. Each item is kept to one improvement, so that scoping one does not drag in others. Version numbers after v0.2.3 are provisional, and each is assigned when its milestone is fully specified.
  - 8089-8100: item 1, a modular anesthesia-machine abstraction with normal single-halogenated-agent interlock behavior. It is the safety baseline that every later machine feature builds on. "Modular" means a real commercial machine is added as a data file, not as code, and three scoping items below serve that goal. The claim concerns fidelity as much as architecture: a learner attributes to a named machine the behavior of the one in their own operating room, so what the module may carry and what the interface may say are the same question. `PL-4DCG` surveyed the variation that affects a computed number. `PL-FG9D` designed the abstraction against it. `PL-WZVZ` keeps a machine's effect on a curve attributable to a named parameter.
  - 8102-8118: item 1 split (2026-09-20, project owner). The framework half is item 40, which is not gated behind Phase 1. Interlocks need the machine profile (which agents are mounted, what the machine forbids), and the profile needs nothing from the interlocks. The interlocks put item 1 in Phase 1, because a single-agent interlock is about holding more than one agent, which is the multi-substance generalization. The bundle carried the data half to the timeline's Beyond row, after multi-substance, although a config file had no need to wait. The cost was paid in the queue: items scoped against this line were written as though the deliverable were the survey's fleet of machines. Item 40 can be built now. Item 1 keeps the interlock baseline and the machine behaviors that need the solver work item 40 defers. The goal sentence is unchanged, and item 40 delivers it.
  - 8120-8130: the default fresh gas flow is no longer item 1's to place, and the earlier sentence was stale. The default was a literal in `core/circuit.py` with no provenance until `PL-4YY1` moved it into `src/anesthesia_sim/data/machines/reference_circle_system.json`, with a cited file and a stated `provenance_gap`. `PL-8DJ7` deferred the question of its home to this abstraction. `docs/machine-abstraction.md` places it among a run's opening conditions, not among machine specifications, because no surveyed machine publishes a startup flow, so a per-machine field would force every profile to invent one. Its value is open, under `PL-NM7X`.
  - 8132-8145: two queue items scoped item 1, one after the other, and both are done. `PL-4DCG` surveyed the machines in current clinical use for the variables that change a simulated result. `PL-FG9D` designed the base abstraction: what a module is, what the base type owns and what each machine supplies, and where the line between parameter and strategy falls. The design depended on the survey, because the extension points are the axes of real variation that affect a number, and designing them first would have been guessing. Three items wait on the two. `PL-8PS6` (fresh gas flow range is a machine property) and `PL-WZVZ` (make an inter-machine difference attributable) were carved out of Gate 1 for that reason. `PL-439V` (make the Tec 6 the concrete second device class) is answered by the design's dial-mapping slot. All three now wait on item 1 being implemented.
  - 8147-8159: the survey is done, in `docs/machine-survey.md`. It records which variables affect a number, which are per-machine data and which are different behavior, and what remains unknown. Finding 1: apparatus volume alone does not account for the measured differences between machines, so a profile made of a volume and a set of bounds is falsified. Finding 2: the fresh gas inlet's position changes the inspired/delivered ratio at a fixed volume, which a single well-mixed circuit compartment cannot represent. The milestone must therefore choose on the record among three options: represent internal topology; state that machines differing only in topology are indistinguishable here; or route the question to the per-compartment gas-phase model at item 39.
  - 8161-8182: the design is done, in `docs/machine-abstraction.md`. Four of its results bear on scoping.
    - The seam exists already. A machine's whole effect on the model is two rates and one volume in the breathing circuit's row of the system matrix in `core/governing_equations.py`, so adding machines cannot touch a patient row. The reference machine's strategies reproduce today's matrix entry for entry, so the abstraction changes no computed value.
    - Two solver properties close the set of machine behaviors: a strategy must be constant across a step and must keep the matrix Metzler. That is why item 5 is a milestone and not a slot.
    - The inlet question is answered: decline to represent internal topology, and say so where a machine is chosen. Folding topology into an effective volume is refused, and item 39 is the condition that reverses the decline.
    - Naming is decided (project owner, 2026-09-20, ratified, over naming the machine by its trade name in the running interface). An archetype label appears wherever a machine is running or chosen. The trade name is confined to the panel holding the machine's parameter set, its sources and its `not_modelled` list. This is a scope constraint on this milestone and on `PL-WZVZ`, not a preference. The trade name is admissible only where what the model does not claim about that machine is on screen beside it.
  - 8184-8188: scoping starts from a design. The scope statement's test is the design's cost target: a machine whose delivery and removal are already in the closed set is one data file under `src/anesthesia_sim/data/machines/`, with no change to `core/` and no new test of the physics.
  - 8189-8191: item 2, agent switching with residual washout accounting, after item 1. It needs the multi-substance patient state of "Development pathway", because washout means holding two agents at once.
  - 8192-8193: item 3, an optional experimental-override mode that can bypass standard interlocks, clearly labeled non-standard, after item 1.
  - 8194-8195: item 4, direct agent injection into the circuit, bypassing the vaporizer and its interlocks, after item 1.
  - 8196-8197: item 5, automated end-tidal control (closed-loop titration to a target end-tidal concentration), after item 1.
  - 8198-8202: item 6, nitrous oxide coadministration as a second inhaled gas, only after the coupled-gas equations and reference cases are defined. The patient state becomes a set of substances, not a volatile agent with nitrous oxide bolted on. Items 2 and 7 need the same generalization, and it decides whether item 13 is an extension or a rewrite.
  - 8203-8204: item 7, concentration and second-gas effects for coadministered gases, after item 6.
  - 8205-8220: item 8, scenario events (timed parameter or state changes during a run), built on a recorded control-input timeline.
    - Every change to fresh gas flow, the vaporizer dial, ventilation and cardiac output is stamped with the simulated time it took effect. The timeline is kept with the state, not in the view.
    - Today the controller records concentrations but not why they moved, so a run's inputs cannot be recovered.
    - The timeline is the prerequisite for items 9-12, and it is easy to mistake for solved. A snapshot lets a run be restored but not reach a point between snapshots, which needs the same inputs re-applied over the same interval. So "resimulate from the nearest prior snapshot", the fallback in every snapshot-interval design, cannot be built without the timeline.
    - The timeline makes a run reproducible and citable; a curve without its input history cannot be checked. Hence the 8-to-12 ordering in § "Development pathway" is not negotiable.
  - 8221: item 9, scenario save/load.
  - 8222: item 10, deterministic replay of a saved scenario, after item 9.
  - 8223-8226: item 11, side-by-side comparison of several runs on a shared time axis, each curve labelled with its run and settings. It is delivered with item 12.
  - 8227-8233: item 12, simulation forking (branching a running simulation into an independent copy), the educational payload of "the group". Example: coast on low flow or hold 0.5 MAC, then compare the time to a wake-up threshold. Comparing two managements of the same case isolates the variable. Building the case twice gives runs that differ in everything not reproduced identically.
  - 8235-8238: item 12's scope (project owner, 2026-08-25): flat, not a tree, with one trunk run and N branches. Sub-forks are out of scope, because they multiply without bound and buy little over re-branching from the trunk.
  - 8240-8262: item 12's branch points (project owner, 2026-08-25, confirmed against the Gas Man Owner's Manual, "Replaying Simulations" and "Edit menu — Rewind").
    - The decision supersedes the original framing, in which bookmarks were the branch mechanism and an arbitrary point was a rare fallback reached by resimulating.
    - Any recorded event on the timeline (a fresh gas flow, vaporizer, ventilation or cardiac output change) is a valid branch point, with or without a bookmark.
    - This matches Gas Man: a substantive change during replay truncates the run, and continuing extends it "with new, alternate results, just as it would have done had the original simulation included the revising adjustment." Gas Man keeps one active timeline, overwritten past the change.
    - This project keeps the branch from before the change, so both branches are available to item 11.
    - Because item 8 stamps every change, resimulating is the ordinary case, cheap per the timing below, and needs only item 8's data.
    - Bookmarks (item 26) remain the named, threshold-triggered subset that a learner fast-forwards to and forks from repeatably. They are not the only subset that qualifies.
  - 8264-8281: item 12's required property. A branch taken at t reproduces its parent's state exactly at every recorded sample up to t, asserted element by element, not within a tolerance.
    - A resimulated branch and a parent simulated straight through can diverge before the branch point. The divergence is subtle, a nominal test misses it, and it defeats forking because the learner cannot see it.
    - Three causes were named, and two were closed as of `PL-VM40` (v0.4.0's Required scope): the accumulation order of `elapsed_s` (simulated time is now the step count times the run's step), and a different number of steps per frame (the run loop now takes a fixed number of steps per tick and never catches up).
