    - Left: a different step size, which `docs/MODEL.md` § "The reproducibility guarantee" does not cover, and whatever a branch restores from a stored point.
    - If exactness is unreachable, the divergence must be bounded, documented in `docs/MODEL.md`, and shown to the user.
  - 8283-8290: item 12's costs, measured 2026-08-25. The full dynamic state is six concentrations, the elapsed time and the control settings, so a snapshot is about one history sample in size and dense snapshots are nearly free. `SimulationState.advance` takes 8.9 us per 0.1 s step, so rebuilding a 3-hour run from t=0 takes about 1 s, and 24 hours about 8 s. The per-step concentration history is not cheap.
  - 8291-8292: item 13, IV pharmacokinetic and effect-site models, after item 12.
  - 8293-8294: item 14, a modular hypnosis/eBIS effect model with explicit model version and provenance.
  - 8295-8296: item 15, a modular nociceptive-response effect model with explicit model version and provenance.
  - 8297-8298: item 16, validated renal/hepatic dysfunction modifiers, where the selected model supports them.
  - 8299-8300: item 17, validated cardiopulmonary bypass modeling, where the selected model supports it.
  - 8301: item 18, validated ECMO modeling, where the selected model supports it.
  - 8302-8303: item 19, species-specific patient/model profiles, without treating non-human patients as scaled humans.
  - 8304-8305: item 20, accessibility: keyboard navigation, contrast, screen-reader support, color-vision-safe encodings.
  - 8306-8310: item 21, performance, working from the open entries in `docs/items/`. The render payload and the coupling of simulation cadence to render cadence are resolved. What remains is the controller's unbounded concentration history and further headroom in building chart points.
  - 8311: item 22, continued documentation work.
  - 8312: item 23, packaging, signing and distribution for shipping the app.
  - 8313-8314: item 24, a user-facing preferences/settings panel (theme, chart window, slider ranges and similar display settings).
  - 8316-8327: item 24's prerequisite has landed, and the work that replaces it has a different shape (`PL-YD6X`). The prerequisite was to fold the UI and display constants into one module instead of adding a fourth location. The constants were spread across `app/theme.py`, module-level constants in `app/simulation_view.py`, and defaults duplicated between `core/*.py` dataclasses and `app/controller.py`. `PL-2CS8` consolidated them in `v0.4.11`, and `app/theme.py` is now the one location. `app/controller.py` declares no module-level constant and no `core/` default. `app/simulation_view.py` still declares Qt layout policy (stretch factors, object names), nothing a reader would set.
  - 8329-8333: what remains for item 24. `app/theme.py` holds constants bound at import time. A panel that writes settings needs a store that can be written and persisted, and none exists yet. That store is the prerequisite to scope, and it has to be built.
  - 8335-8345: what the panel must never make editable (`PL-QBX0`, 2026-09-19). Until then the exclusion named only `data/**/*.json`, which is a location, not a criterion, and the agent-identification colours and the contrast-checked palette fall outside it. The criterion is the guarantee a value carries, never its file. A constant whose value carries a safety, standards-conformance or accessibility guarantee enforced from outside the panel is out of the panel's reach, wherever it is declared. A list of cases cannot carry this rule, and the old one was wrong once. The table lists the cases known on that date, and the criterion governs later additions.
  - 8347-8348: table header: What / Where it is declared / What holds its value.
  - 8349: scientific parameters (partition coefficients, tissue volumes, MAC), declared in `data/**/*.json`; held by validation, versioning and citation, and `tools/doc_check.py` walks the provenance both ways.
  - 8350: the three ISO 5360:2016 agent-identification colours, declared in `app/theme.py`; `tests/unit/test_theme.py` pins each fill to Table 2, and `tools/agent_identity_check.py` keeps the measured pair equal to the rendered pair.
  - 8351: every colour `tools/contrast_check.py` measures: the six compartment traces at 3:1 against the panel under normal vision and three simulated dichromacies, and every text pair at WCAG 2.2 AA. Declared in `app/theme.py` and in any `app/` module that declares its own; `tools/contrast_check.py` reads every `app/` module with `ast`.
  - 8352: the dash patterns of the six compartment traces, declared in `app/chart_frame.py`; nothing mechanical holds them, only a recorded judgment, which is why they are listed.
  - 8354-8358: each listed value can change only through the review that set it, never through a settings screen. For the parameters, that is scientific review under `CLAUDE.md`'s safety-critical standard. For the other rows, it is the recorded reasoning in `app/theme.py` and `docs/MODEL.md`.
  - 8360-8370: why the colours are in scope. ISO 5360 Table 2 footnote b makes displaying an agent colour an obligation to display the right one. Recolouring would show one agent in another's identification colour, which is `CLAUDE.md`'s "the correct number with the wrong units, label". "The last row is the one to read twice": colour cannot separate six traces, because the 3:1 minimum caps every trace's luminance, so six traces cannot be more than 1.48 apart (no unit given), and `app/theme.py` records the search. The dash patterns are the channel that separates the traces, and they are the one entry no check would defend.
  - 8372-8379: item 25, a playback speed multiplier that does not change the simulation's time step. It is separate from replay (item 10): replay reproduces a recorded run, while the multiplier changes the rate at which any run is displayed. Shipped in v0.4.0 as `PL-SN2C` (§ "Completed: v0.4.0 - the teachable case"). Five rates (1, 5, 20, 60, 300x), each a number of steps per tick, so the step never changes size.
  - 8380-8390: item 26, run bookmarks that halt a run at a target, after item 25. There is no way to "run fast until something happens, then stop", so a learner watches the clock and pauses by hand, which is neither repeatable nor possible at speed. Bookmarks make fast-forward usable, and they are the branch points a learner returns to by name. Item 12 treats every timeline event as a branch point, but the snapshot policy should still be keyed on bookmarks, which learners revisit repeatedly.
  - 8392-8397: item 26's minimum scope (project owner, 2026-08-25, confirmed against the Gas Man Owner's Manual, "Using Bookmarks," and current-application screenshots the project owner supplied). "Scope floor" means minimum scope, and Gas Man's bookmark set is that minimum: two distinct mechanisms under one dialog ("Place or Remove a Bookmark"), not one kind of bookmark with two flavors.
  - 8399-8407: the two mechanisms. A time bookmark is an absolute simulated time (hours/min/sec); time bookmarks are the older kind and all that the manual's "Using Bookmarks" describes. A MAC target is a percent of MAC on a chosen graphed compartment (circuit, alveolar, vessel-rich, muscle, fat or mixed-venous). MAC targets were added after the manual text and are confirmed only from the current UI.
  - 8409-8414: keep the two as separate first-class collections, not one type with a kind field. That matches Gas Man's dialog and lets the UI list them separately. A MAC target needs MAC as a unit, so `PL-DHV7` (MAC as a displayed unit) lands first.
  - 8416-8424: required property: crossings are tested on every simulation step, not once per rendered frame. A per-frame test overshoots by a frame's worth of simulated time, and more at faster playback. The same bookmark would then halt at different concentrations, and the displayed halt value would not be the one asked for. That is a presentation-correctness failure `CLAUDE.md` treats as safety-critical, and it breaks the reproducibility of branches taken from the bookmark.
  - 8426-8434: crossing direction is withdrawn from the minimum scope (project owner, 2026-09-20). A run that passes a height on the way up and again on the way down has reached it both times. A target is a compartment and a height only, stored as a multiple of 1 MAC rather than the reference's percent. `MacTarget` in `app/bookmarks.py` records the reasoning, because the reference has a direction field and a later reader would put it back.
  - 8436-8442: a target halts on every crossing (project owner, 2026-09-20), against the session's recommendation to re-arm. A re-arm flag is a hidden mode: two targets that look identical would behave differently because one has fired. Resuming a paused run is one control the learner already watches.
  - 8444-8450: a threshold above a compartment's asymptote is never reached, so a bookmark needs a distinct "not reached, run-time cap hit" outcome, shown differently from "reached", instead of stopping silently. `PL-CTD7` shipped four outcome values; the fourth covers a time bookmark before a branch's fork instant, which no amount of running reaches. Bookmarks are part of the saved scenario, not local to one session, so item 12 can branch from them.
  - 8452-8463: item 27, a schematic compartment view beside the graph, after Gas Man's interactive "Picture". Gas Man draws the circuit, alveoli and blood and tissue groups as containers of one width, each filled "to a height representing partial pressure or tension in that compartment" (Gas Man Workbook, Figure 2-1, printed p. 11), so the Picture shows where the tension is. Until 2026-10-25 the entry said the containers were drawn to scale and filled with agent, which the Workbook does not say. This view adds each container's amount and a bar of each compartment's share of the agent. Scheduled in v0.7.0 (project owner, 2026-10-25, ratified, over containers drawn to scale and over Gas Man's Picture as it is). § "v0.7.0 - the schematic" has the scope.
  - 8465-8477: why item 27's view is needed. It teaches what the graph structurally cannot: the graph plots partial pressure, so fat shows near zero for hours while it holds a large and growing share of agent. In this model at a 1 MAC dial, fat holds a fifth of the stored agent at one hour and a third at three hours, and overtakes muscle only after about five to seven and a half hours (`PL-H5DV`). So the compartment chart keeps one linear axis and leaves this lesson to the view (project owner, 2026-09-23, ratified, over a reader-selected logarithmic scale on the chart, `PL-QYBW`). Where the drug is (in mL) and where the tension is are different questions, and confusing them is a standard novice error. The view needs per-compartment agent amounts on the snapshot. The core computes them, and since `PL-KFTM` (2026-11-01) the snapshot carries one record per compartment.
  - 8478-8486: item 28, agent cost, from the delivered-agent amount the model tracks. The economics of low fresh gas flow is a standard teaching point, and it is the one lesson in this class of simulator that the app has the numbers for and does not draw. It depends on nothing, and it was kept out of v0.4.0 as an addition, not a prerequisite. Scheduled in v0.7.0 (project owner, 2026-10-25, ratified, over leaving it unscheduled and deferring its four entries to Gate 4). § "v0.7.0 - the schematic" has the scope.
  - 8488-8499: until 2026-09-19 the item said "the exhausted-agent amount", the wrong quantity (`PL-H4N8`). `core/agent_simulation_validation.py` enforces `initial + delivered = exhausted + currently stored`. With `initial` at zero, delivered is the total, and exhausted and stored say only where the agent is now. Cost is what left the bottle, which is what the vaporizer added: `delivered_agent_l`. `exhausted_agent_l` is what left the circuit, and it understates delivered by `currently_stored_agent_l`. The two converge only at full washout, because the model has no metabolism or chemical degradation (`docs/MODEL.md` § "Assumptions"), and that convergence makes the wrong basis look plausible.
  - 8501-8505: other sources use the delivered amount. Gas Man bills delivered agent (Workbook Appendix, printed p. 174: `Cost = DELIVERED Flow x Cost/mL vapor`). The "Known limitations" entry of `docs/MODEL.md` on the 20 °C reference calls the delivered total "the figure that means cost". The old sentence was the only document that said otherwise.
  - 8507-8510: what the wrong basis would have cost. The figures come from a run on 2026-09-19 with `AgentUptakeSystem.default()`: a reference adult, sevoflurane at a fixed 1 MAC dial, a 0.1 s step. Each cell is the percentage by which exhausted falls below delivered.
  - 8512-8513: table header: fresh gas flow / 15 min / 30 min / 60 min.
  - 8514: 1.0 L/min: 63.7%, 53.8% and 45.7% low.
  - 8515: 4.0 L/min (the shipped default): 28.6%, 21.4% and 16.9% low.
  - 8516: 8.0 L/min: 16.2%, 11.8% and 9.1% low.
  - 8518-8527: the error is worst early, in the phase the low-flow lesson is about, and worst at low flow, so it distorts the comparison instead of shifting both arms together. At a fixed dial, delivered is proportional to flow, so 1 against 8 L/min is exactly 8:1 at all three times. The exhausted basis shows 18.4:1, 15.3:1 and 13.4:1, overstating the saving by as much as 2.3x. A display that flatters its own lesson is worse than one that understates it. `PL-H4N8` carries the method and the caveat that a fixed dial is not the equal-alveolar-concentration comparison of a real low-flow protocol.
  - 8529-8539: item 28's prerequisite, narrower than it looks. `delivered_agent_l` is in litres of vapour, and a bottle holds mL of liquid, so money or bottles need a vapour-to-liquid ratio. The constant is sourced and its reference condition decided. `docs/MODEL.md` § "Agent amount" carries Biro's 184 mL of vapour per mL of liquid sevoflurane, reproduces it to 182.8 mL from Laster, Fang and Eger's 20 °C density and the molar mass, and records that the project owner ratified a 20 °C reference over 37 °C on 2026-09-17, naming this item as the consumer (`PL-S6WW`, shipped in v0.4.28). One litre of vapour is 5.47 mL of liquid sevoflurane.
  - 8541-8550: the numbers were prose only until 2026-10-26. `PL-KZ99` then stored each agent's molar mass and liquid density, with the density's measurement temperature, in `data/agents/sevoflurane.json`, `data/agents/isoflurane.json` and `data/agents/desflurane.json`. `core/liquid_agent.py` derives the ratio in one place, resting on Laster's primary measurement instead of Biro's composite constant. The ratio is exact for the delivered amount, the one a cost needs, and reads a compartment store 5.8 % high (`docs/MODEL.md` § "Known limitations"). `PL-B396` chooses what the figure looks like.
  - 8552-8555: the exhausted/stored split keeps its own labels. The split is the uptake curve in mass form and worth drawing, but it is not the economics, and neither line may be relabelled as waste. `PL-B396` chooses that display.
  - 8557-8566: item 29, make `core/` read like the domain in one deliberate pass over the whole package. The bar in `CLAUDE.md`: a clinician who knows uptake and distribution recognizes the physiology without translation. The bar was written after most of `core/`, so it governs new code and was never applied backwards. The purpose is the project owner's fluency in the code: reviewing the coupled-gas equations of items 6 and 7 is the hardest scientific work in the plan, and code that reads like the textbook makes that a different task.
  - 8568-8576: item 29's placement (project owner, 2026-08-26; revised 2026-09-02): after v0.4.0 and ahead of item 6. "Ahead of item 6" is the original, unchanged constraint. Item 6 restructures every compartment, and settling the vocabulary first makes that a transformation of well-named code, not renaming and restructuring at once. The accepted cost is that some of the pass is revisited when compartments become per-substance. The convention survives, and it is the part that is expensive to invent twice.
  - 8578-8585: the first half of the placement moved. Placed between v0.3.0 and v0.4.0, the unscoped pass gated the whole teachable-case milestone and bought nothing there. v0.4.0 changes no equation, parameter or numerical method, so the fluency is not used until items 6 and 7. The pass sits immediately after v0.4.0, as the `v0.4.x` row of "The timeline", which is as early as the remaining constraint allows.
  - 8587-8599: the paragraph read differently until 2026-09-06 (`PL-3P2P`). It said "Nothing between v0.4.0 and v0.7.0 touches `core/` either — v0.5.0 and v0.6.0 are interface releases on an unchanged model — so the step is free anywhere in that span". That was true on 2026-09-02 and false three days later: the 2026-09-05 design round placed the score architecture in v0.5.0, chained behind `PL-GS5X`, and `PL-T691` and `PL-P1Z3` declare `src/anesthesia_sim/core` in their `touches`. So the step was pinned immediately ahead of v0.5.0, at the head of the MVP's critical path, and the old paragraph understated its priority. `PL-GS5X` closed on 2026-09-06 in pull request 376, which made the chain startable.
  - 8601-8602: item 29 was scoped on 2026-09-03 (project owner), and its three open questions are answered. It was re-scoped the same day and is "no longer a patch".
  - 8604-8608: the bar (project owner, 2026-09-03): a reviewer who knows the textbook's standard variables and equations can follow `core/` and recognize them without `docs/MODEL.md` and without a lookup table. It is sharper than the one-line bar in `.claude/rules/core-domain.md`, and the answers below are judged against it.
  - 8610-8622: naming alone could not meet the bar (the re-scope's finding). The alveolar balance's two terms were computed in two different operator-split steps, with a third step between them. The pulmonary uptake term was never formed. Three of the five composed sub-steps belonged to the splitting scheme, not to the domain. The independent RK4 oracle held the equations in exactly the right form, but `core/` may not import it without making verification a tautology. So the split was replaced by the exact matrix exponential (`PL-GS5X`, landed 2026-09-06). Assembling the system matrix is now transcribing the governing equations, and the code that computes the result is the code a reviewer recognizes. `PL-SPMQ` carries the measurement and the options weighed.
  - 8624-8629: this supersedes `PL-6GS0`, which decided in v0.2.8 to keep the split. That decision weighed accuracy and step size and was right on both, but readability was not in its frame. `PL-X9KD` re-derives every published statement justified by the splitting error: § "Displayed precision", the supported step bound, and the pinned reference states.
  - 8631-8641: item 29's version: a patch in the `v0.4.x` track, with no exception recorded (project owner, 2026-09-03; the specific number released 2026-09-05). The class of version is unchanged, but the promise of `v0.4.1` is dropped, because the release path cannot keep it. `docket release` gives the next free number to whatever is finished, so a patch cut first takes it. A number the roadmap has given to a milestone section ahead of the current one is withheld by the reserved-version guard (project owner, 2026-09-16, ratified, on `PL-KQHN`). The step ships under whatever number the cut assigns.
  - 8643-8654: why a patch. Earlier on 2026-09-03 the step was briefly recorded as a minor version, reasoning from the size of the change. § "Versioning decision" chooses by the capability boundary crossed, and this step crosses none: the same system, parameters, four controls and three agents, and nothing new the learner can do. Two things change, and neither is a capability. The displayed value moves in its last digit, which v0.2.11 shipped as a patch. The guarantee strengthens from "within a measured bound of the stated equations" to "the exact solution of them". The step bound may survive with a different justification, so the accepted inputs may be unchanged; `PL-X9KD` settles that.
  - 8656-8661: the temptation to number the step up so the change can be pointed at is the argument the v0.3.0 exception made, and that exception says it is no precedent. The release row and the baseline section are where a change is pointed at. v0.2.8's row records why the split was kept over an exact matrix exponential, and this release's row records why it no longer is.
  - 8663-8672: nine items carry item 29: `PL-GS5X` and `PL-X9KD` under `numerical-domain`, six under `core-domain-language`, and `PL-X2XX`. `PL-VZL0` requires `PL-X2XX`, which went unlisted until `PL-GGCN` taught the checker to read the second half of a compound prerequisite. `PL-P0BB` is no longer counted as a tenth: its state-vector decision shipped in v0.3.2, and counting it made the step look as though it still had an open design question. Corrected 2026-09-05 (`PL-YYL2`). The timeline row above carries the same list and the same correction.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Planned milestones (run 2 of 3)", rows 8674-9257
  - 8674-8684 (item 29, continued) A symbol maps to an expression, not to an identifier. Identifiers carry their unit or kind (`alveolar_ventilation_l_min`). The compartment object supplies the subscript, so F_A in code is `alveoli.<accessor>`. The mapping is the fourth column of `docs/MODEL.md` § "Symbols" (`PL-H46J`), which makes the pass finite and checkable. Beside it sits one name per modelled quantity. `core/` gives the partial-pressure-equivalent fraction eight names across four compartments, and `PL-9SH6` collapses them to one. Symbol-to-identifier was the shape guessed before the pass was measured.
  - 8686-8693 Equations are both in the code and cited from it. `docs/MODEL.md` writes the differential equations, and `core/` implements their analytic solutions. The spec carries a solved form in exactly one place. A docstring that restates the solved form supplies the missing half. Rule: always cite, restate only the solved form the spec lacks, never restate what the spec states (`PL-VZL0`).
  - 8695-8702 Three mechanical rules: every Code cell resolves to a real attribute; the retired accessor names never reappear; a partition-coefficient identifier names both phases in order (`PL-FZ6T`). A unit-suffix check was considered and rejected, because suffixes are already near-universal and the check would be a pure ratchet whose vocabulary needs maintaining. Judgment remains: would a reader who knows the domain guess this?
  - 8704-8717 The owner supplied two references during scoping, and they settled questions the code could not. Hendrickx and De Wolf: the gas-phase cascade is F_D → F_I → F_A, with no F_C. So the middle state is named after its container, and the denominator of the F_A/F_I curve cannot be found by name in the code (`PL-3TLK`). They also record that "fraction", "concentration" and "partial pressure" are interchangeable in the gas phase, which settles the accessor name against a convention that distinguishes them. Baker and Farmery name one partition coefficient three ways in one chapter, so the ratio-naming rule is enforced mechanically (`PL-212V`).
  - 8719-8726 The numerical method changes and the displayed value moves in its last digit, but no capability boundary is crossed. The model, parameters, controls and agents are unchanged, and the learner can do nothing new. So the step takes a patch version, not a minor, per "Versioning decision". An earlier paragraph said "no behavior, equation, parameter, or numerical method changes"; it was written when item 29 was a naming pass and was superseded by the 2026-09-03 re-scope.
  - 8728-8734 Item 30: add age, sex and weight as inputs so the learner sees how patient characteristics change the anesthetic. It is a core teaching component, not a later extension. `data/patients/reference_adult.json` is a placeholder. "How would this differ in this patient" is the question a compartment model exists to answer. Today the application states that there is no patient variability.
  - 8736-8740 Built-in patient profiles stay read-only. A "duplicate and customize" workflow carries lineage and schema metadata, so a modified profile traces to the cited profile it came from. The requirement predates the item and used to be an unattached sentence at the end of the section.
  - 8742-8749 The item removes a safeguard (`PL-DXJR`). The fixed reference adult is one of the two safeguards in `docs/MODEL.md` § "What keeps a real patient's data out of a run". With these inputs a run can be built from a real patient's data, so the item rewrites that section and its hazard row. The test `test_every_run_simulates_the_shipped_reference_adult` pins the one shipped patient file and the absence of any patient argument or control, so it fails when the inputs land.
  - 8751-8770 Body composition has a default and is editable for each case (project owner, 2026-09-02). A case opens with a body composition set, and the case settings let the learner edit it. This settles "the derivation question below". The default is derived from the case's other inputs, so an edit overrides an existing value. Two consequences are outside the current scope. (1) The derived value is shown and attributed wherever it acts, naming its rule and whether it is the default or overridden; a silent substitution that moves fat uptake is the plausible-but-unexplained value the safety standard refuses. (2) An edited composition is an unsourced covariate set, so the supported-range refusal applies to the edit control as it does to the input; accepting an out-of-domain composition is the same failure as extrapolating to it. No case-settings surface exists today: the only patient is the hard-coded `reference_adult.json`, so this is an intended surface.
  - 8772-8790 MAC basis (project owner, 2026-09-02). In the release that adds age, `mac_percent` moves to the Mapleson MAC40 basis. Today it uses flat Gas Man values: sevoflurane 2.0, isoflurane 1.2, desflurane 6.0. Nickalls and Mapleson (Br J Anaesth 2003;91(2):170-4) is already cited in all three `data/agents/*.json` files, with a note that it is unused because the model has no age parameter; this change makes the note live. Log10 MAC falls linearly, in parallel for every inhaled agent, by about 6% per decade for age >= 1 year, relative to MAC at 40 years. MAC40 is reported as 1.17% isoflurane, 1.80% sevoflurane and 6.60% desflurane. Confirm these against the primary paper when scoping, which is the provenance standard for every parameter in `data/`. The files carry other values, so displayed MAC changes at every age, including 40. It is a parameter-set change needing a provenance row and a release note, not a new input field. It lands after `PL-DHV7` (MAC multiples as a display unit), because without a MAC unit the learner sees no change.
  - 8792-8798 Placement (project owner, 2026-09-02): Phase 1, ahead of the substance generalization. That generalization is the plan's stated known risk and the most likely place to stall, and a core teaching component does not belong behind it. Once compartments hold N substances, changing how volumes and flows are derived touches N times the surface. The same argument places item 29 ahead of that work.
  - 8800-8805 Scaling is not through body composition (project owner, 2026-09-02). Weight scales the compartments directly. The declined route had weight, height and sex produce a body-composition estimate that scales the compartments. Sex is a covariate in its own right, not acting through fat mass.
  - 8807-8821 The trade. Fat is pivotal for volatile agents: its tissue:gas coefficients are 34 for sevoflurane, 70 for isoflurane and 13 for desflurane. Between two patients of the same mass and different composition, fat separates them, and a rule keyed on total mass cannot express that. The direct route buys one fewer layer of unsourced derivation, because a composition estimate needs its own formula and provenance. An unsourced scalar ahead of the dominant compartment is the "plausible but incorrect clinical value" `CLAUDE.md` forbids, as surely as no scalar. Whichever rule is chosen is sourced before it ships. Weber et al. 2021, cited in `reference_adult.json`, explores the obese case with the same reference simulator the parameters come from; scoping starts there.
  - 8823-8838 Cardiac output scales, as part of a coupled package (`PL-YKSM`, 2026-09-08). This decides which quantities weight scaling includes. A time constant is V_i λ_{i:b}/Q_i. Scaling the flows while volumes stay at the stored fixed litres gives τ ∝ M^{-3/4}: a heavier patient equilibrates faster and a lighter one more slowly. At 20 kg with the shipped sevoflurane coefficients, the child has 2.64 times the adult's time constants, the opposite of every textbook. Scaling volumes with the flows gives 0.76 times; scaling nothing gives 1.00. The half-measure is worse than the status quo. Compartment volumes, alveolar gas volume, alveolar ventilation and the perfusion fractions move with cardiac output and the tissue flows, or none of them moves. `PL-YKSM` carries the arithmetic and the refused alternative.
  - 8840-8848 The point is also in `docs/MODEL.md` "Known limitations" and in the Cattermole note in `reference_adult.json`, where a reader of the stored value meets it. It is recorded here because the scoping session reads this item. The risk is that scoping makes "cardiac output becomes a function of weight", the change `PL-YKSM` refused, and ships a paediatric patient whose kinetics are wrong in the most-taught direction. One paragraph now saves a rebuild later.
  - 8850-8862 Suggestion, not a decision: for the default (cardiac output), prefer measured weight-banded normal ranges to an allometric formula, because the learner sees the default, not the law behind it. Cattermole et al. 2017, cited in `reference_adult.json`: 2218 healthy subjects aged 0.5 to 89, reported by weight band, a measurement rather than a scaling exponent. Its limits: subjects awake, supine and at rest; transcutaneous Doppler, not thermodilution; the authors state normal ranges are method-specific. So the file cites the paper and does not adopt it. Adopting it would be a recorded decision under § "Source hierarchy", on the same terms as every value in `data/`.
  - 8864-8871 Out-of-range covariates: agreed in principle, cutoffs unresearched (project owner, 2026-09-02). Accepted: bound each covariate, and report an out-of-bound value by blocking, logging or both. Not settled: where any cutoff sits. No number enters `core/` until researched and sourced like `data/`. An invented bound would refuse the paediatric or obese case a learner most wants, on an uncitable number, which is worse than no bound.
  - 8873-8883 The bounds are a research question, part of scoping this milestone, not a queue item to pick up ahead of it. `core/supported_ranges.py` is the nearest pattern, and its argument applies: a silently extrapolated patient would simulate, display and chart an unsupported value. It differs: that module's four quantities are operating settings inside a run, where refusing costs nothing, whereas a covariate defines the case. Refusing a 3 kg or 200 kg patient refuses the learner's question. That asymmetry is why the mechanism is open.
  - 8885-8890 The teaching payoff needs item 11. A single trace cannot show an effect, so the full payoff needs side-by-side comparison (item 11) and the two-run rendering `PL-B9PY` covers. This is a reason to scope item 11 with this use in mind, not to hold item 30 behind it.
  - 8892-8904 Item 31: ship a primary-literature partition-coefficient set beside the Gas Man set, selectable, so one case runs under both on the same axes. Building the primary set is the prerequisite and the larger half. `vessel_rich` is lumped while Yasuda 1989 reports per organ, so an organ-weighting scheme must be built and justified before any tissue value is called primary. Shipping both is the point: no commercial simulator lets a resident ask "how much does the parameter set matter?", and the comparison teaches provenance better than a data-file note. `PL-D6LX` holds the analysis and verified primary values. The owner chose this route on 2026-09-03.
  - 8906-8912 Item 1 (isoflurane and desflurane) was promoted, delivered as v0.2.0 and removed; see § "Completed: v0.2.0". Further volatile agents (halothane, enflurane, ether, xenon) remain an unscoped idea. Nitrous oxide is excluded because items 6-7 cover it. The idea returns as its own item when someone is ready to scope it.
  - 8914-8919 Item 32: make the repository presentable to a first-time visitor in one pass. On 2026-09-05 the owner deferred the README rewrite to this pass rather than running it once its scope was settled. The owner expects similar human-facing pieces to collect, and pieces written for one reader over one week cohere better than pieces written one at a time over months.
  - 8921-8931 The repository went public before the pass (project owner, 2026-09-05: "public today. Will do human facing pass in the nearish future."). The pass is no longer a gate and blocks nothing. `PL-XYRN` was closed against that decision. `PL-N092` (README rewrite) ran alone on 2026-09-06. The reason to defer was coherence; the reason not to wait was that a stranger could arrive at a repository with no `README.md`. The root `README.md` exists again, for the two audiences `PL-RM83` settled. The check enforcing its absence retired in the same commit.
  - 8933-8937 What is left is the item's original scope minus the README. Nothing raises it, nothing is blocked on it, and no check reports it. It waits for a session offering to scope it, or for the owner to ask.
  - 8939-8942 Item 33: one deliberate visual design pass over the whole interface (palette, type scale, spacing rhythm, density, visual composition of each surface), in place of per-defect corrections made one at a time.
  - 8944-8946 The owner requested it on 2026-09-08, as "a decent size overhaul (theme, style, overall polish)", not urgent, wanted after the simulator works.
  - 8948-8955 Arrangement is not in this pass; "layout" used to be on the list (corrected 2026-09-16, `PL-BNYF`). Item 34 decides which areas exist, where they sit and what the reader may do to them; this item decides how their contents look. The two were separable on 2026-09-08 against one fixed screen, but not now: under item 34 the reader owns the arrangement, so a design pass cannot decide it once.
  - 8957-8967 This resumes a shelved thread. `docs/WORKING_NOTES.md` § "Shelved, then resumed: UI structure/form mockups" records a mockup round shelved on the owner's call, ending "Do not resume this without the project owner asking again"; that condition is met. The note's reasoning (no mature UI on the functionality then available) was written against v0.1.0. Its principle, that UI/UX ambition follows the maturity of the scientific core, places this item after MVP. The three artefacts from that round are not a starting point.
  - 8969-8972 Placement (project owner, 2026-09-08; moved 2026-09-16; renumbered 2026-09-26): after v0.6.0, as the `v0.6.x — the interface pass` row of "The timeline", a patch track rather than a numbered milestone, for the reason that row gives.
  - 8974-8983 It runs after item 34 (project owner, 2026-09-16, ratified on `PL-PHKP` - chosen over leaving it between MVP and Gate 2, where 2026-09-08 had placed it), because there is more to style after item 34 and the pass would otherwise be partly redone. Item 34 and break-out add an area header, a workspace tab strip, a live splitter handle and a drag affordance, none of which exists today. The header belongs to the View, not the container (`docs/interface-provenance.md` § "What an editor must implement", from the source). A pass run first would style one set of surfaces and then meet four more.
  - 8985-8995 Renumbered `v0.6.x` on 2026-09-26 (`PL-V1Y7`), when break-out came off the timeline (project owner, 2026-09-26). The row now follows v0.6.0 directly, as `v0.7.x` had followed break-out's release. The reason survives because all four surfaces arrive in item 34's tiled half: the live splitter handle, drag affordance and Workspace tab strip are v0.6.0 Required scope entry 14; the area header comes with the View contract and each Area's chooser, entries 5 and 16. Break-out, whenever built, adds windows with the same chrome, and styling anything else it adds is that build's work. The cost is now one release rather than two.
  - 8997-9005 The option not taken: running the pass first would have given a styled interface two releases sooner. `PL-BNYF` had separated arrangement (item 34) from appearance (this row), so the visual composition of existing surfaces would mostly have survived. Cost accepted: the current visual language stays until after item 34. Nothing in either release was blocked on the answer, so the row stayed in place while the question was open.
  - 9007-9025 Absorbed into the Qt port on 2026-09-10, returned on 2026-09-16 (project owner, 2026-09-16, ratified, on `PL-L9RD`). The port took the item on the argument that it would redecide palette, type scale, spacing and layout. Measured after landing, it changed no palette entry, type size, padding or radius. The port reserves the layout containers with inert splitter handles (§ "Completed: v0.4.26 - the interface moves to Qt" → "Required scope" item 2), and item 34 turns them live; corrected 2026-09-16 (`PL-BNYF`) from "this item". This item inherits the other half: every dashboard surface is an independent widget with its own spacing and density, which makes one pass possible. It inherits none of the splitting, joining or workspace behaviour. The placement is unchanged. A port is not a design round: re-expressing widgets in a new toolkit decides nothing about their look.
  - 9027-9040 The structural half is not part of this item and has landed. Most of an overhaul seen from outside is not polish, and all three pieces are closed. `PL-2CS8` consolidated the display constants in v0.4.11 (item 24 named it as its prerequisite; "Development pathway" Phase 2 orders it first). `PL-B9PY` decomposed the panels in v0.4.25, making six repeated panel recipes one. `PL-NGF7` settled the theme-object question in v0.4.26: the Qt port did not dissolve it as the deferral predicted (tested and found false), so the item scoped what the stylesheet owns and added a check that fails the build when a module under `app/` writes a colour outside that scope. No gate still has to clear any of the three. What is left is taste.
  - 9042-9051 Two hard prerequisites. Nothing in the repository draws the interface. `PL-7J96`: two defects shipped while every tree-level test passed (a required label wrapped mid-phrase; four of seven readouts dropped to a second baseline at some widths), both found only by screenshot and undetectable from the control tree. `PL-2QMK`: no web-container session can render Flet, because the egress proxy refuses the CanvasKit fetch. The test suite cannot see visual changes, so both prerequisites land first.
  - 9053-9066 Three constraints on any palette. (1) `tools/contrast_check.py` parses every `app/` module with `ast` (`PL-BXB2`), requires every declared pair to meet WCAG 2.2 AA under four vision models, and fails the build on a renamed or undeclared colour constant; a restyle is also a `REQUIREMENTS` rewrite in the same change (`.claude/rules/ui-color.md` judgment 1). (2) The three ISO 5360:2016 Table 2 agent colours are fixed (judgment 4), and a palette keeps them legible on its surfaces. (3) Traces are separated by dash pattern, not colour: `PL-GVXP` measured the pairwise ceiling at about 1.48 when each of six traces also clears 3:1 against the panel, so re-picking the chart palette cannot buy separation.
  - 9068-9074 Out of scope: dark mode is a separate decision, because it doubles the declared contrast matrix and the ISO colours were chosen for a light context. It belongs to scoping this milestone. So does the publication-quality figure export (`docs/WORKING_NOTES.md` § "Long-term vision"), which implies its own rendering path, separate from the Flet live chart, and is a capability, not a restyle.
  - 9076-9079 Item 34: take Blender's window management as the model. The window is divided into resizable, modular areas, each holding one view. Task-oriented layout presets can be switched, customized and saved by the learner.
  - 9081-9084 Requested by the owner on 2026-09-12, naming four properties: resizable windows, modular views, task presets, and presets the user can customize and save rather than a fixed shipped set.
  - 9086-9097 Why Blender: its window system is tiling, not floating. Resizable areas do not overlap and each hosts one view; the user splits and joins them from corners and borders. Areas are grouped into Workspaces, shown as tabs, each geared to a task, which the user can reorder, duplicate and delete. A layout persists when saved into the startup file. Tiling earns its place: no panel overlaps or hides another, and a covered value is a misread value, which `CLAUDE.md`'s standard treats as a safety failure. Source: Blender Manual, "Interface → Window System → Areas" and "→ Workspaces".
  - 9099-9105 The vocabulary is Blender's concept (project owner, 2026-09-16: "the workspace/area concept ... is what I want to implement"). The terms were read at the source; the manual's "Areas" and "Workspaces" pages were supplied directly because the session egress proxy refused `docs.blender.org` when this was written, though the host answered a probe on 2026-09-26 (`PL-M701`). Three terms, two adopted from Blender.
  - 9107-9108 An Area is a rectangle reserving screen space. It holds one thing, and areas never overlap.
  - 9109-9110 A View occupies an area and has the functionality. Item 36 is the catalogue of views.
  - 9111-9114 A Workspace is a set of areas holding views, geared to a task and switched as tabs. Blender puts the tabs in the Topbar, saves workspaces in the file, and lets a custom set become the defaults.
  - 9116-9122 Docking is the manual's word for corner-drag operations. A corner drag can join, split or both; dragged into the middle of a second area, it replaces that area. Adjacent areas swap through the border's Area Options, and any two through Ctrl-LMB from a corner. Resizing is a border drag; modifier keys snap the border or move aligned borders together.
  - 9124-9131 Two precedents from the source. Blender refuses to delete the last workspace, structurally rather than by warning, the same shape `PL-WLWY` settled for the minimum display. A workspace carries settings: Pin Scene switches back to the remembered scene. The equivalent here is a workspace that pins which run it shows, which a broken-out window needs in order to name its run.
  - 9133-9150 Tiled first, with break-out into a separate window (project owner, 2026-09-15); scoped across two releases on 2026-09-16. § "v0.6.0 - the layout is the reader's" (ratified 2026-09-16) builds the tiled no-overlap invariant, the View contract, the registry, workspaces, persistence and the unconditional region. Break-out was the next release ("the second screen") until it came off the timeline on 2026-09-26. The split costs nothing structural: v0.6.0 builds the two things break-out would otherwise force rewriting, namely a serialized layout root that carries a set of windows from version 1, and the unconditional region in the per-window shape "the next paragraph settles". Together the halves build the invariant plus break-out. Break-out takes an area into its own top-level window with its own areas (Blender Manual "Areas": View > Duplicate Area into New Window, or Shift-LMB on the splitter widget), a fully functional window of the same running instance, useful across monitors. Every window is tiled, so nothing inside a window covers anything else in it.
  - 9152-9159 What is refused is silent occlusion, not floating (project owner, 2026-09-15, correcting the paragraph's first form). Floating is not ruled out: taking a tiled area out into a floating one is wanted later, and this milestone does not build it. The first version called floating panels "not admitted", a permanent refusal that was the writing session's overreach. Tiled first is only the order of work.
  - 9161-9198 The occlusion rule: nothing may cover a value the display is required to show while the application believes it is showing it. A floating panel and a broken-out window dragged over the main window are two ways to break it. The rule belongs to the display, not to a window type, and is stated once. Settled (`PL-WLWY`, project owner, 2026-09-15) in `docs/MODEL.md` § "Minimum displayed outputs" → "What this list requires once the layout is the reader's". The list has three parts. Unconditional: no workspace may remove it and nothing may cover it; it is stated as a test, with an invariant tier and a per-substance tier, so the inhaled agent's concentrations and MAC are today's instance, not permanent (project owner, 2026-09-16). Accounting: reachable, not simultaneously visible. Conditional: applies only when its surface is shown, so removing a chart removes no required value. The unconditional set sits outside the area system, which is the guarantee v0.6.0 builds. A second window (project owner, 2026-09-16, ratified, answering `PL-W54S`, chosen over that item's own two live readings): every top-level window carries the invariant tier and the name of its run; the per-substance tier appears once, in a main window that cannot close while another is open. The tiers are unconditional for different reasons, which carry over differently. `docs/MODEL.md` carries the argument, the two refused readings and the limit on observable occlusion. This was recorded before break-out because it decides the region's shape in v0.6.0. Later floating must comply with the rule. The division is a stepping stone, rigid while the layout apparatus is built and expected to be revisited; the occlusion rule must survive.
  - 9200-9217 Licence. `docs/interface-provenance.md` is the record (study protocol, what was read, what is adopted, what is done differently), and a building session starts there. Blender's source files are GPL-2.0-or-later (the whole work GPL-3.0-or-later), and this project is Apache-2.0. Reading the source is unrestricted (GPLv2 s.0). Copying, translating or porting it is restricted and would make the stated licence wrong. The Blender Manual and Developer Documentation, including the HIG, are CC-BY-SA 4.0, so pasting their prose is copying: paraphrase and cite, never paste (`PL-P5QX`). The design rationale is in the Developer Docs and HIG, not the Manual. Qt supplies the primitives: nested `QSplitter` for tiling, and a project file for a named preset.
  - 9219-9232 The layout is a nested-splitter tree (`PL-3J2P`, decided 2026-09-16 on research the owner asked for). The options were Blender's shared-vertex graph (each area names four corner points shared with neighbours) and nested splitters, decided by measurement against a threshold written first. Across all 32 workspaces Blender ships (11 defaults, 21 in five application templates), none needs an arrangement a tree cannot express. One has a four-way junction, where the tree splits one crossing border into two handles. The two behaviours a tree cannot express are opt-in: extend-drag (Shift on a border drag) and snap-merge (a separate operator on the border's right-click menu).
  - 9234-9245 One supporting argument failed. A tree is strictly less expressive: five full-span splits and one ordinary join reach a five-area pinwheel that no sequence of full-span cuts can produce. The tree is chosen knowing this, on four grounds: Blender's designers never used the extra expressiveness in 32 layouts; this audience picks a workspace rather than authoring one; the limit shows as an unavailable join, not a wrong value; a planar subdivision is owned by one person forever. `docs/interface-provenance.md` carries the count, method, construction and reversal condition.
  - 9247-9257 (continues into the next chapter) The container sits behind the project's own layout model (`PL-C842`, recommended 2026-09-16 with the Blender review behind it; the owner delegated the architecture call). The negative half was settled by measurement and not overturned. `QSplitter.saveState()` returns 35 opaque bytes holding sizes and a child count, and cannot record which view occupies which pane, which is the whole of a named workspace. Restoring a three-child state into a two-child splitter returns `True` with sizes `[318, 318]` instead of failing, which disqualifies it under the file's standard of preferring an obvious failure to a plausible wrong result.
Chapter: ROADMAP.md § "Planned milestones (run 3 of 3)", rows 9259-9674
  - **9259-9273 (item 34, continued):** the Blender review found three properties Blender lacks, which this project must build itself, and the paragraph sets out the layout model's design.
    - Loud failure when a saved layout names a view this build lacks; Blender silently substitutes a 3D viewport.
    - A required value is never hidden to make room; Blender collapses a region that will not fit.
    - "Isolation with teeth"; Blender's views can access the screen, and ten of twenty-one do.
    - The pure-Python `LayoutModel` is the source of truth: a tree of splits and panes with `split`, `join`, `resize`, `swap` and `set_view`, serialized to versioned JSON.
    - One adapter module is the only place that imports `QSplitter`.
    - A view is registered by kind, never sees a splitter, never calls `saveState()` and never stores its own geometry.
    - `PL-LH18`'s path-scoped rule already requires views to be interchangeable rather than merely movable; this decision extends that reasoning to the container.
  - **9275-9294:** decision of 2026-09-16 that item 34 goes after v0.5.0 and splits.
    - Tag: project owner, ratified on `PL-NMTF`. The alternatives were inserting item 34 ahead of v0.5.0, which renumbers a scoped milestone, and shipping it undivided with break-out inside.
    - Ground 1: a workspace that pins a run has no content until more than one run exists. v0.5.0 introduces the second run. Today `run_label` names a run `None` when it is shown alone.
    - Ground 2: inserting earlier renumbers a milestone already scoped with a frozen gate. Placing it after renumbers only rows that § "Planned milestones" calls provisional.
    - Ground 3: no known failure is live. `PL-NWTM` and `PL-9LNF` are `safety`-classed and pre-loaded. Their failures need a working handle, and the port's handles are inert.
    - Item 34 splits across two releases under the "keep each milestone narrow" rule. Undivided, it was roughly twice the largest milestone run so far. The cut is at break-out, the one place where splitting costs nothing structural.
    - Consequence: schematic (item 27) to v0.8.0, multi-substance (items 6 and 7) to v0.9.0. Rows 9300-9302 supersede this.
  - **9296-9319:** break-out unscheduled on 2026-09-26, and what keeps it possible.
    - Owner's words: "This is something I don't want to rule out doing eventually. Not something that needs done soon."
    - Break-out is still item 34's second half and still wanted, but has no row on § "The timeline". Its row, "the second screen", came off the timeline.
    - The schematic (item 27) moved back to v0.7.0, and multi-substance (items 6 and 7) moved to v0.8.0 (`PL-V1Y7`).
    - Break-out is placed when the owner asks for it, as any catalogue entry is. What keeps it possible stays in v0.6.0, unchanged.
    - First, the saved layout root carries a set of windows from version 1 (Required scope entry 1, `PL-HJPY`). Adding them later would need a schema migration against every Workspace a learner has saved.
    - Second, the unconditional region is built in the per-window shape (entry 7, `PL-W54S`).
    - The build item `PL-Y04W` was dropped the same day, because an eventual feature is recorded here as intent, not as a queue item.
    - The item brief keeps a measurement taken on PySide6 6.11.2 / Qt 6.11.2 on 2026-09-16. A window built as `QWidget(main, Qt.Window)` has `main` as its transient parent. Qt counts only primary windows when it decides the last window has closed. So closing the main window quits the application while the broken-out window is still visible.
    - The build must satisfy `docs/MODEL.md` § "Minimum displayed outputs" -> "What this list requires once the layout is the reader's".
  - **9321-9348:** the 2026-09-17 reopening; the order is kept.
    - Tag: project owner, ratified, over moving item 34 ahead of v0.5.0.
    - The argument, which the `PL-NMTF` round had not weighed: build order decides whether a new display is a widget registered against a contract or an edit to the current layout. So interface questions settled before the area model are settled against a layout that is going away. The argument is sound.
    - Instance: `PL-J4NW` found `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` routing every new display panel to `run_view.py` or `simulation_view.py` "by asking whose it is", which is the fixed two-level layout this item refuses. `.claude/rules/ui-areas.md` stands in for the area system until it exists.

    - The order is kept. The rule below, once only implied by the row order of § "The timeline", is now written out, because a session does not treat row order as a rule.
  - **9350-9368:** rule: no new display surface is built before item 34.
    - Tag: project owner, 2026-09-17, ratified, over leaving the order implied by row position.
    - A surface built earlier is built against the current layout and no view contract, so it is built twice: into a fixed parent, then as a View.
    - Every remaining surface is already behind this item: the schematic (item 27), multi-substance and its readouts (items 6 and 7), and the interface pass (item 33). So the rule costs nothing today. It exists so that the next row move cannot reopen the question by accident.
    - It binds a new surface, not a new value. It never defers a reading that `docs/MODEL.md` -> "Minimum displayed outputs" requires, or a correction to a reading on screen. The safety-critical standard is a minimum that no ordering rule lowers, and a misleading displayed value is never held back for a layout.
    - The rule ends when the View contract (`PL-TH35`) and the view registry (`PL-R1WQ`) ship. After that, a new surface is one registry entry.
    - A surface wanted before then is filed against item 36's catalogue, not built.
  - **9370-9385:** the Qt port reserves the layout for item 34 (project owner, 2026-09-12, `PL-8VL1`).
    - The Qt port rewrites every dashboard layout onto Qt.
    - That release argued for absorbing item 33, because applying a decision to Flet and then to Qt is the same work twice. The argument applies to layout architecture at least as strongly as to palette and spacing.
    - The absorption was reversed for palette and spacing on 2026-09-16, because the port did not in fact decide them again.
    - The reservation is unaffected. The port really does build a layout container once, so reserving it costs nothing and rebuilding it would cost the work twice; this is the test the palette half failed.
    - The port builds none of the layout. Each dashboard surface is an independent widget inside nested `QSplitter`s with the handles inert, and item 34 makes the handles live.
    - § "Completed: v0.4.26 - the interface moves to Qt" → "Required scope" item 2 carries this.
  - **9387-9400:** views are separate work, and the layout comes first (project owner, 2026-09-15).
    - Owner's words: "the layout part with the adding, resizing, removing windows etc. and then telling the program what should be in that window ... is the functionality I want to end up with. Then separately want to work on widgets."
    - Item 36 is that catalogue.
    - The order is also sound: a view's contract is whatever the area system requires of its contents, so views built first would be rewritten.
    - A container abstraction that only one view uses has not really been tested, so item 34 validates against two existing views, not a new one.
    - § "Completed: v0.4.26" supplies those two views, because it makes each dashboard surface an independent widget.
  - **9402-9407:** example workspaces (project owner, 2026-09-15).
    - An induction workspace with the concentration graph zoomed in.
    - A big-picture workspace to switch to during maintenance.
    - The learner can create workspaces.

  - **9409-9413 (item 35):** an optional model of anaesthesia's own effect on cardiac output and regional perfusion.
    - The model keeps both fixed today.
    - It would be an option the user turns on, labelled as an illustrative overlay, not as the model's own prediction.
    - A default run would not change.
  - **9415-9419:** framing (project owner, 2026-09-13).
    - A possible future feature, not a settled exclusion.
    - `PL-8GV5` proposed closing the question outright.
    - The owner agreed for now that the model keeps perfusion fixed, and directed that the feature stay on the list as an option.
  - **9421-9438:** the volunteer studies support no single relation.
    - The literature was checked while working `PL-8GV5`.
    - Desflurane alone did not change cardiac index at 0.83-1.66 MAC (Weiskopf et al., Anesth Analg 1991;73:143-56, PMID 1854029).
    - The same volunteers given 60% nitrous oxide showed a dose-dependent fall (Cahalan et al., Anesth Analg 1991;73:157-64, PMID 1854030).
    - With sevoflurane, cardiac index fell at 1.0 and 1.5 MAC and returned to baseline at 2.0 MAC as systemic vascular resistance fell. The depression diminished over hours and under spontaneous ventilation (Malan et al., Anesthesiology 1995;83:918-28, PMID 7486177).
    - So the sign depends on the carrier gas, the dose-response is not monotonic, and the effect changes with time at a fixed dose. No monotonic, time-invariant relation can be supported.
    - The hard part of scoping is what the option asserts and how its uncertainty is shown.
    - `docs/MODEL.md` § "Known limitations" records the measurements, the citations and the consequence for the tissue time constants.
  - **9440-9459:** resting fat flow is uncertain too.
    - The regional half needs the blood flow to each tissue under anaesthesia. Most measurements the project can reach are from animals.
    - Two human comparisons for the stored resting fat flow point different ways. `docs/MODEL.md` § "Known limitations" weighs them (`PL-HBH2`).
    - Heinonen et al. measured resting subcutaneous adipose perfusion by PET, in one depot of six healthy young women (J Appl Physiol 2012;112:1059-63, PMID 22223450). Their value is close to half of what the stored fraction implies.
    - Both Yasuda 1991 washout studies (Anesth Analg 1991;72:316-24, PMID 1994760; Anesthesiology 1991;74:489-98, PMID 2001028) fit a slowest compartment, taken to be fat. Its flow per 100 mL of tissue is at or a little above the stored figure.
    - Neither source settles the question. One compares a single depot at rest with a whole-body lumped compartment. In the other, the fat compartment is a fitted assignment, not an anatomy.
    - So the resting flow is uncertain by up to about a factor of two, and the direction of its error is unsettled. A varying fraction would sit on top of it.
  - **9462-9482 (item 36):** the catalogue of views an area can hold.
    - Separate from the layout mechanism, and after it. Item 34 decides what an area is, how it is docked, split, joined, swapped and broken out, and how a workspace is saved. Item 36 decides what a learner can put in an area.
    - The occupant of an area is a View (project owner, 2026-09-17, ratified - chosen over `Editor`, and over `Component` and `Widget`). This reverses the 2026-09-16 decision that adopted Blender's "Editor" and retired views and widgets.
    - Blender's editors mostly edit, but these mostly display modelled values. `QWidget` is the base class of every one.
    - § "v0.6.0 - the layout is the reader's" has the full reasoning, and `PL-GPYV` has the comparison.
    - Candidates: the compartment schematic (item 27), the control-input timeline, the numeric readouts, item 37's interaction display, and the concentration chart and F_A/F_I plot as views a workspace places.
    - The owner requested this on 2026-09-15 as its own track (`PL-4D1M`).
    - Each view owes what any displayed clinical value owes under `CLAUDE.md`'s clinical-output standard, settled once for each view.
  - **9484-9499 (item 37):** an opioid/hypnotic interaction display, after items 13 and 15.
    - Iso-effect contours over a pair of effect-site concentrations, for a named endpoint such as tolerance of laryngoscopy.
    - A response-surface interaction model is a model class of its own and measures the synergy itself, so it cannot be derived from two single-drug models.
    - It needs its own versioned parameter file, provenance record and source-hierarchy decision, as each agent file has.
    - `CLAUDE.md`'s safety-critical standard names interaction surfaces among its examples.
    - The display names the endpoint on screen, because laryngoscopy, intubation and skin incision are different surfaces. It marks the plotted point as a predicted pair, not a measurement, and shows the contours' population uncertainty.
    - The owner requested this on 2026-09-15 (`PL-JFXG`).

    - The owner raised this on 2026-09-16 as something to think about down the road.
    - It is intent, and scoping it is a later decision (`PL-KFJQ`).

    - Item 33 (the interface pass) sets the palette and type, which is adjacent to an identity but is not one.

    - `open-anesthesia-sim` is the repository name. Whether it is also the product name is open.
    - The icon, wordmark, window title and bundle identifier derive from it.

  - **9547-9557:** two limitations in `docs/MODEL.md` end at this change.
    - The note "One reference condition, where the model physically has two": the alveolar, venous and tissue stores hold 5.5 % fewer moles than their 20 °C label implies.
    - The note "The modeled alveoli are dry, and real ones are saturated at 47 mmHg": the water half is 47/760.

    - Neither had anywhere to point, so readers got no answer and sessions worked out the same conclusion again.
  - **9559-9566:** it is one change.
    - The gas is carried at one declared condition where it physically has several.
    - The fix is a condition for each compartment, covering temperature, pressure and water content together.
    - Correcting the water half alone moves all four validated wash-in ratios outside their published spread: applying 713/760 to the inspired term while the partition coefficients keep an unrecorded gas-phase basis.
  - **9568-9574:** nothing displayed depends on it.
    - Displayed concentrations are dimensionless fractions, and partition coefficients are ratios.
    - So the trajectories, the mass-balance identity and the MAC multiples do not change.
    - The condition matters only where an amount leaves the unit: item 28's planned liquid-equivalent consumption figure.
  - **9576-9583 (item 40):** an anesthesia machine as a config file.
    - The framework half of item 1, split out on 2026-09-20 (project owner).
    - The deliverable is the extension point. A second profile in `src/anesthesia_sim/data/machines/` can be loaded and selected in code, and is refused where it is not admissible.
    - Which machines exist, and how faithfully each is modelled, belongs to item 1, which has no timeframe.
  - **9585-9595:** the data half has shipped; the selection half does not exist.
    - `PL-4YY1` moved the circuit volume and the fresh gas flow from field defaults in `core/circuit.py` into a cited, strictly validated `reference_circle_system.json`.
    - `PL-8PS6` added the first per-machine capability field.
    - `load_reference_circle_system_parameters()` names one filename, and nothing looks a profile up by id. Nothing in `src/` reads `id` or `display_name`.
    - A second profile is ignored at run time and passes the unit suite, then fails `make check` on its provenance rows.
  - **9597-9604:** separable from item 1 while it changes no rate.
    - Per `docs/machine-abstraction.md`, a machine affects the model only through two rates and one volume. All three are in the breathing circuit's row of the system matrix, and none touches a patient row.
    - The item declares where a rate would come from and leaves the rates where they are.
    - Computing a rate differently belongs to item 1.
  - **9606-9610:** one profile and no machine chooser.
    - A comparison surface belongs to `PL-WZVZ` and waits behind item 34's View contract.
    - A second profile plus a selection surface would make `PL-WZVZ`'s misattribution hazard live.
    - Nothing displayed changes.
  - **9612-9619:** deferred work.
    - Extracting the delivery and removal rates from `core/governing_equations.py` and `core/circuit.py` costs the same for one profile or ten, so it waits for a machine that needs it.
    - Also waiting: the second member of each strategy slot, automated end-tidal control (item 5), fresh gas decoupling and ventilator drive, and internal circuit topology.
    - Internal circuit topology was declined, and item 39 reverses that.
  - **9621-9626:** the cost of a machine.
    - The target "One data file and nothing else" cannot be reached. `tools/doc_check.py` requires a provenance row in `docs/MODEL.md` for every leaf number in every data file.
    - So a machine is one data file plus one provenance row per number. The target should say so.
  - **9628-9631:** six queue items carry the work, under the feature `machine-profile-framework`.
    - Two matter for the first real profile: what `circuit_volume_l` means, and a required startup-flow field that no manufacturer publishes.
  - **9633-9638 (item 41):** a chart View can opt in to keeping its time window in step with the other opted-in charts in its Workspace.
    - Blender's "Sync Visible Range" does this.
    - It comes after item 34's per-chart windows (`PL-L59K`).
    - Wanted "later" (project owner, 2026-10-19). `PL-1RFZ` has notes from the Blender source and a Done-when.
  - **9640-9645 (item 42):** a learner can rename a run, after item 12.
    - The name shows wherever `dashboard_frame.run_label` names the run. A Workspace's pin stays on the run's `RunId`.
    - Two open runs never show the same name, so that a legend entry still tells which run's settings drew a curve.
    - Wanted "eventually" (project owner, 2026-10-19). `PL-YBZJ` has the design note.
  - **9647-9669 (item 43):** one source of truth everywhere, enforced structurally.
    - A value, grammar, predicate, state or reading is read from the one module, data file or writer that owns it, in the simulator, its documents and the apparatus (project owner, 2026-10-19: "one source of truth everywhere", "a core design principle", "fix this structurally everywhere").
    - `CLAUDE.md` § "Architecture and development discipline" states the principle.
    - A second copy takes one of four shapes: a value copied (`PL-JS6P`), state with two writers (`PL-WM5P`), a rule made in several places (`PL-VWXL`), and configuration restated (`PL-SH0W`).
    - `PL-K88Z`'s inventory sizes the work, `PL-2LB5` has the design, and the items carry the feature `one-source-of-truth`.
    - Inventory of 2026-10-19: 93 second copies in the literal and fact baselines; run state outside the run view in 2 surfaces with two writers and 5 surfaces outside the guard; 14 duplicate blocks found by pylint; 34 high-confidence hand re-checks in 18 mechanisms; 30 configuration facts restated, 3 of which disagree.
    - Result: 13 items filed, plus the baselines of four check items.
    - Numbers restated in `docs/MODEL.md` and `README.md` were not counted; `PL-JS6P` sizes them.
  - **9671-9674:** for the whole list.
    - No item 1-43 mixes scientific-core and UI/tooling concerns in one milestone.
    - Dependencies are noted inline, not bundled (e.g. 2-5 on 1, 7 on 6, 10 on 9, 13 on 12).
